<p> Remove blocking artifacts from input video. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filter</code></p>
<dd> Set filter type, can be <i>weak</i> or <i>strong</i>. Default is <i>strong</i>. This controls what kind of deblocking is applied.</p>
<dt><code>block</code></p>
<dd> Set size of block, allowed range is from 4 to 512. Default is <i>8</i>.</p>
<dt><code>alpha</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>beta</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gamma</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>delta</code></p>
<dd> Set blocking detection thresholds. Allowed range is 0 to 1. Defaults are: <i>0.098</i> for <i>alpha</i> and <i>0.05</i> for the rest. Using higher threshold gives more deblocking strength. Setting <i>alpha</i> controls threshold detection at exact edge of block. Remaining options controls threshold detection near the edge. Each one for below/above or left/right. Setting any of those to <i>0</i> disables deblocking.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set planes to filter. Default is to filter all available planes.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Deblock using weak filter and block size of 4 pixels. </p>
<pre>
        deblock=filter=weak:block=4
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Deblock using strong filter, block size of 4 pixels and custom thresholds for deblocking more edges. </p>
<pre>
        deblock=filter=strong:block=4:alpha=0.12:beta=0.07:gamma=0.06:delta=0.05
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Similar as above, but filter only first plane. </p>
<pre>
        deblock=filter=strong:block=4:alpha=0.12:beta=0.07:gamma=0.06:delta=0.05:planes=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Similar as above, but filter only second and third plane. </p>
<pre>
        deblock=filter=strong:block=4:alpha=0.12:beta=0.07:gamma=0.06:delta=0.05:planes=6
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>decimate</h3>
<p> Drop duplicated frames at regular intervals. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>cycle</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of frames from which one will be dropped. Setting this to <i>N</i> means one frame in every batch of <i>N</i> frames will be dropped. Default is <tt>5</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>dupthresh</code></p>
<dd> Set the threshold for duplicate detection. If the difference metric for a frame is less than or equal to this value, then it is declared as duplicate. Default is <tt>1.1</tt></p>
<dt><code>scthresh</code></p>
<dd> Set scene change threshold. Default is <tt>15</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>blockx</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>blocky</code></p>
<dd> Set the size of the x and y-axis blocks used during metric calculations. Larger blocks give better noise suppression, but also give worse detection of small movements. Must be a power of two. Default is <tt>32</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>ppsrc</code></p>
<dd> Mark main input as a pre-processed input and activate clean source input stream. This allows the input to be pre-processed with various filters to help the metrics calculation while keeping the frame selection lossless. When set to <tt>1</tt>, the first stream is for the pre-processed input, and the second stream is the clean source from where the kept frames are chosen. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>chroma</code></p>
<dd> Set whether or not chroma is considered in the metric calculations. Default is <tt>1</tt>.</dl>
<h3>deconvolve</h3>
<p> Apply 2D deconvolution of video stream in frequency domain using second stream as impulse. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to process.</p>
<dt><code>impulse</code></p>
<dd> Set which impulse video frames will be processed, can be <i>first</i> or <i>all</i>. Default is <i>all</i>.</p>
<dt><code>noise</code></p>
<dd> Set noise when doing divisions. Default is <i>0.0000001</i>. Useful when width and height are not same and not power of 2 or if stream prior to convolving had noise.</dl>
<p> The <tt>&quot;deconvolve&quot;</tt> filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options.</p>
<h3>dedot</h3>
<p> Reduce cross-luminance (dot-crawl) and cross-color (rainbows) from video. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>m</code></p>
<dd> Set mode of operation. Can be combination of <i>dotcrawl</i> for cross-luminance reduction and/or <i>rainbows</i> for cross-color reduction.</p>
<dt><code>lt</code></p>
<dd> Set spatial luma threshold. Lower values increases reduction of cross-luminance.</p>
<dt><code>tl</code></p>
<dd> Set tolerance for temporal luma. Higher values increases reduction of cross-luminance.</p>
<dt><code>tc</code></p>
<dd> Set tolerance for chroma temporal variation. Higher values increases reduction of cross-color.</p>
<dt><code>ct</code></p>
<dd> Set temporal chroma threshold. Lower values increases reduction of cross-color.</dl>
<h3>deflate</h3>
<p> Apply deflate effect to the video. </p>
<p> This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3&#215;3) average by taking into account only values lower than the pixel. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold3</code></p>
<dd> Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535. If 0, plane will remain unchanged.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>deflicker</h3>
<p> Remove temporal frame luminance variations. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Set moving-average filter size in frames. Default is 5. Allowed range is 2 &#8211; 129.</p>
<dt><code>mode, m</code></p>
<dd> Set averaging mode to smooth temporal luminance variations. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>am</code></p>
<dd> Arithmetic mean</p>
<dt><code>gm</code></p>
<dd> Geometric mean</p>
<dt><code>hm</code></p>
<dd> Harmonic mean</p>
<dt><code>qm</code></p>
<dd> Quadratic mean</p>
<dt><code>cm</code></p>
<dd> Cubic mean</p>
<dt><code>pm</code></p>
<dd> Power mean</p>
<dt><code>median</code></p>
<dd> Median</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>bypass</code></p>
<dd> Do not actually modify frame. Useful when one only wants metadata.</dl>
<h3>dejudder</h3>
<p> Remove judder produced by partially interlaced telecined content. </p>
<p> Judder can be introduced, for instance, by <code>pullup</code> filter. If the original source was partially telecined content then the output of <tt>&quot;pullup,dejudder&quot;</tt> will have a variable frame rate. May change the recorded frame rate of the container. Aside from that change, this filter will not affect constant frame rate video. </p>
<p> The option available in this filter is:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>cycle</code></p>
<dd> Specify the length of the window over which the judder repeats. </p>
<p> Accepts any integer greater than 1. Useful values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>4</code></p>
<dd> If the original was telecined from 24 to 30 fps (Film to <font SIZE="-1">NTSC</font>).</p>
<dt><code>5</code></p>
<dd> If the original was telecined from 25 to 30 fps (<font SIZE="-1">PAL</font> to <font SIZE="-1">NTSC</font>).</p>
<dt><code>20</code></p>
<dd> If a mixture of the two.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default is <code>4</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<h3>delogo</h3>
<p> Suppress a <font SIZE="-1">TV</font> station logo by a simple interpolation of the surrounding pixels. Just set a rectangle covering the logo and watch it disappear (and sometimes something even uglier appear &#8211; your mileage may vary). </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Specify the top left corner coordinates of the logo. They must be specified.</p>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> Specify the width and height of the logo to clear. They must be specified.</p>
<dt><code>show</code></p>
<dd> When set to 1, a green rectangle is drawn on the screen to simplify finding the right <i>x</i>, <i>y</i>, <i>w</i>, and <i>h</i> parameters. The default value is 0. </p>
<p> The rectangle is drawn on the outermost pixels which will be (partly) replaced with interpolated values. The values of the next pixels immediately outside this rectangle in each direction will be used to compute the interpolated pixel values inside the rectangle.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set a rectangle covering the area with top left corner coordinates 0,0 and size 100&#215;77: </p>
<pre>
        delogo=x=0:y=0:w=100:h=77
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>derain</h3>
<p> Remove the rain in the input image/video by applying the derain methods based on convolutional neural networks. Supported models:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Recurrent Squeeze-and-Excitation Context Aggregation Net (<font SIZE="-1">RESCAN</font>). See &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://openaccess.thecvf.com/content_ECCV_2018/papers/Xia_Li_Recurrent_Squeeze-and-Excitation_Context_ECCV_2018_paper.pdf">http://openaccess.thecvf.com/content_ECCV_2018/papers/Xia_Li_Recurrent_Squeeze-and-Excitation_Context_ECCV_2018_paper.pdf</a></code>&gt;.</dl>
<p> Training as well as model generation scripts are provided in the repository at &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://github.com/XueweiMeng/derain_filter.git">https://github.com/XueweiMeng/derain_filter.git</a></code>&gt;. </p>
<p> Native model files (.model) can be generated from TensorFlow model files (.pb) by using tools/python/convert.py </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filter_type</code></p>
<dd> Specify which filter to use. This option accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>derain</code></p>
<dd> Derain filter. To conduct derain filter, you need to use a derain model.</p>
<dt><code>dehaze</code></p>
<dd> Dehaze filter. To conduct dehaze filter, you need to use a dehaze model.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>derain</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>dnn_backend</code></p>
<dd> Specify which <font SIZE="-1">DNN</font> backend to use for model loading and execution. This option accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>native</code></p>
<dd> Native implementation of <font SIZE="-1">DNN</font> loading and execution.</p>
<dt><code>tensorflow</code></p>
<dd> TensorFlow backend. To enable this backend you need to install the TensorFlow for C library (see &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://www.tensorflow.org/install/install_c">https://www.tensorflow.org/install/install_c</a></code>&gt;) and configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libtensorflow&quot;</tt></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>native</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>model</code></p>
<dd> Set path to model file specifying network architecture and its parameters. Note that different backends use different file formats. TensorFlow and native backend can load files for only its format.</dl>
<p> It can also be finished with <code>dnn_processing</code> filter.</p>
<h3>deshake</h3>
<p> Attempt to fix small changes in horizontal and/or vertical shift. This filter helps remove camera shake from hand-holding a camera, bumping a tripod, moving on a vehicle, etc. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> Specify a rectangular area where to limit the search for motion vectors. If desired the search for motion vectors can be limited to a rectangular area of the frame defined by its top left corner, width and height. These parameters have the same meaning as the drawbox filter which can be used to visualise the position of the bounding box. </p>
<p> This is useful when simultaneous movement of subjects within the frame might be confused for camera motion by the motion vector search. </p>
<p> If any or all of <i>x</i>, <i>y</i>, <i>w</i> and <i>h</i> are set to -1 then the full frame is used. This allows later options to be set without specifying the bounding box for the motion vector search. </p>
<p> Default &#8211; search the whole frame.</p>
<dt><code>rx</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>ry</code></p>
<dd> Specify the maximum extent of movement in x and y directions in the range 0-64 pixels. Default 16.</p>
<dt><code>edge</code></p>
<dd> Specify how to generate pixels to fill blanks at the edge of the frame. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>blank, 0</code></p>
<dd> Fill zeroes at blank locations</p>
<dt><code>original, 1</code></p>
<dd> Original image at blank locations</p>
<dt><code>clamp, 2</code></p>
<dd> Extruded edge value at blank locations</p>
<dt><code>mirror, 3</code></p>
<dd> Mirrored edge at blank locations</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>mirror</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>blocksize</code></p>
<dd> Specify the blocksize to use for motion search. Range 4-128 pixels, default 8.</p>
<dt><code>contrast</code></p>
<dd> Specify the contrast threshold for blocks. Only blocks with more than the specified contrast (difference between darkest and lightest pixels) will be considered. Range 1-255, default 125.</p>
<dt><code>search</code></p>
<dd> Specify the search strategy. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>exhaustive, 0</code></p>
<dd> Set exhaustive search</p>
<dt><code>less, 1</code></p>
<dd> Set less exhaustive search.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>exhaustive</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>filename</code></p>
<dd> If set then a detailed log of the motion search is written to the specified file.</dl>
<h3>despill</h3>
<p> Remove unwanted contamination of foreground colors, caused by reflected color of greenscreen or bluescreen. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>type</code></p>
<dd> Set what type of despill to use.</p>
<dt><code>mix</code></p>
<dd> Set how spillmap will be generated.</p>
<dt><code>expand</code></p>
<dd> Set how much to get rid of still remaining spill.</p>
<dt><code>red</code></p>
<dd> Controls amount of red in spill area.</p>
<dt><code>green</code></p>
<dd> Controls amount of green in spill area. Should be -1 for greenscreen.</p>
<dt><code>blue</code></p>
<dd> Controls amount of blue in spill area. Should be -1 for bluescreen.</p>
<dt><code>brightness</code></p>
<dd> Controls brightness of spill area, preserving colors.</p>
<dt><code>alpha</code></p>
<dd> Modify alpha from generated spillmap.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>detelecine</h3>
<p> Apply an exact inverse of the telecine operation. It requires a predefined pattern specified using the pattern option which must be the same as that passed to the telecine filter. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>first_field</code></p>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>top, t</code></p>
<dd> top field first</p>
<dt><code>bottom, b</code></p>
<dd> bottom field first The default value is <tt>&quot;top&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>pattern</code></p>
<dd> A string of numbers representing the pulldown pattern you wish to apply. The default value is <tt>23</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>start_frame</code></p>
<dd> A number representing position of the first frame with respect to the telecine pattern. This is to be used if the stream is cut. The default value is <tt>0</tt>.</dl>
<h3>dilation</h3>
<p> Apply dilation effect to the video. </p>
<p> This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3&#215;3) maximum. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold3</code></p>
<dd> Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535. If 0, plane will remain unchanged.</p>
<dt><code>coordinates</code></p>
<dd> Flag which specifies the pixel to refer to. Default is 255 i.e. all eight pixels are used. </p>
<p> Flags to local 3&#215;3 coordinates maps like this: </p>
<pre>
    1 2 3
    4   5
    6 7 8
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>displace</h3>
<p> Displace pixels as indicated by second and third input stream. </p>
<p> It takes three input streams and outputs one stream, the first input is the source, and second and third input are displacement maps. </p>
<p> The second input specifies how much to displace pixels along the x-axis, while the third input specifies how much to displace pixels along the y-axis. If one of displacement map streams terminates, last frame from that displacement map will be used. </p>
<p> Note that once generated, displacements maps can be reused over and over again. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted options follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>edge</code></p>
<dd> Set displace behavior for pixels that are out of range. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>blank</code></p>
<dd> Missing pixels are replaced by black pixels.</p>
<dt><code>smear</code></p>
<dd> Adjacent pixels will spread out to replace missing pixels.</p>
<dt><code>wrap</code></p>
<dd> Out of range pixels are wrapped so they point to pixels of other side.</p>
<dt><code>mirror</code></p>
<dd> Out of range pixels will be replaced with mirrored pixels.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <code>smear</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Add ripple effect to rgb input of video size hd720: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i INPUT &#8211; f lavfi &#8211; i nullsrc = s = hd720, lutrgb = 128 : 128 : 128 &#8211; f lavfi &#8211; i nullsrc = s = hd720, geq = &#8216;r=128+30*sin(2*PI*X/400+T):g=128+30*sin(2*PI*X/400+T):b=128+30*sin(2*PI*X/400+T)&#8217; &#8211; lavfi &#8216;[0][1][2]displace&#8217; OUTPUT</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Add wave effect to rgb input of video size hd720: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i INPUT &#8211; f lavfi &#8211; i nullsrc = hd720, geq = &#8216;r=128+80*(sin(sqrt((X-W/2)*(X-W/2)+(Y-H/2)*(Y-H/2))/220*2*PI+T)):g=128+80*(sin(sqrt((X-W/2)*(X-W/2)+(Y-H/2)*(Y-H/2))/220*2*PI+T)):b=128+80*(sin(sqrt((X-W/2)*(X-W/2)+(Y-H/2)*(Y-H/2))/220*2*PI+T))&#8217; &#8211; lavfi &#8216;[1]split[x][y],[0][x][y]displace&#8217; OUTPUT
</dl>
<h3>dnn_processing</h3>
<p> Do image processing with deep neural networks. It works together with another filter which converts the pixel format of the Frame to what the dnn network requires. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dnn_backend</code></p>
<dd> Specify which <font SIZE="-1">DNN</font> backend to use for model loading and execution. This option accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>native</code></p>
<dd> Native implementation of <font SIZE="-1">DNN</font> loading and execution.</p>
<dt><code>tensorflow</code></p>
<dd> TensorFlow backend. To enable this backend you need to install the TensorFlow for C library (see &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://www.tensorflow.org/install/install_c">https://www.tensorflow.org/install/install_c</a></code>&gt;) and configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libtensorflow&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>openvino</code></p>
<dd> OpenVINO backend. To enable this backend you need to build and install the OpenVINO for C library (see &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino/blob/master/build-instruction.md">https://github.com/openvinotoolkit/openvino/blob/master/build-instruction.md</a></code>&gt;) and configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libopenvino&quot;</tt> (&#8211;extra-cflags=-I&#8230; &#8211;extra-ldflags=-L&#8230; might be needed if the header files and libraries are not installed into system path)</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>native</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>model</code></p>
<dd> Set path to model file specifying network architecture and its parameters. Note that different backends use different file formats. TensorFlow, OpenVINO and native backend can load files for only its format. </p>
<p> Native model file (.model) can be generated from TensorFlow model file (.pb) by using tools/python/convert.py</p>
<dt><code>input</code></p>
<dd> Set the input name of the dnn network.</p>
<dt><code>output</code></p>
<dd> Set the output name of the dnn network.</p>
<dt><code>async</code></p>
<dd> use <font SIZE="-1">DNN</font> async execution if set (default: set), roll back to sync execution if the backend does not support async.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Remove rain in rgb24 frame with can.pb (see <code>derain</code> filter): </p>
<pre>
        ./ffmpeg -i rain.jpg -vf format=rgb24,dnn_processing=dnn_backend=tensorflow:model=can.pb:input=x:output=y derain.jpg
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Halve the pixel value of the frame with format gray32f: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.jpg -vf format=grayf32,dnn_processing=model=halve_gray_float.model:input=dnn_in:output=dnn_out:dnn_backend=native -y out.native.png
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Handle the Y channel with srcnn.pb (see <code>sr</code> filter) for frame with yuv420p (planar <font SIZE="-1">YUV</font> formats supported): </p>
<pre>
        ./ffmpeg -i 480p.jpg -vf format=yuv420p,scale=w=iw*2:h=ih*2,dnn_processing=dnn_backend=tensorflow:model=srcnn.pb:input=x:output=y -y srcnn.jpg
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Handle the Y channel with espcn.pb (see <code>sr</code> filter), which changes frame size, for format yuv420p (planar <font SIZE="-1">YUV</font> formats supported): </p>
<pre>
        ./ffmpeg -i 480p.jpg -vf format=yuv420p,dnn_processing=dnn_backend=tensorflow:model=espcn.pb:input=x:output=y -y tmp.espcn.jpg
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>drawbox</h3>
<p> Draw a colored box on the input image. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The expressions which specify the top left corner coordinates of the box. It defaults to 0.</p>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>height, h</code></p>
<dd> The expressions which specify the width and height of the box; if 0 they are interpreted as the input width and height. It defaults to 0.</p>
<dt><code>color, c</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the box to write. For the general syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. If the special value <tt>&quot;invert&quot;</tt> is used, the box edge color is the same as the video with inverted luma.</p>
<dt><code>thickness, t</code></p>
<dd> The expression which sets the thickness of the box edge. A value of <tt>&quot;fill&quot;</tt> will create a filled box. Default value is <tt>3</tt>. </p>
<p> See below for the list of accepted constants.</p>
<dt><code>replace</code></p>
<dd> Applicable if the input has alpha. With value <tt>1</tt>, the pixels of the painted box will overwrite the video&#8217;s color and alpha pixels. Default is <tt>0</tt>, which composites the box onto the input, leaving the video&#8217;s alpha intact.</dl>
<p> The parameters for <i>x</i>, <i>y</i>, <i>w</i> and <i>h</i> and <i>t</i> are expressions containing the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dar</code></p>
<dd> The input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (<i>w</i> / <i>h</i>) * <i>sar</i>.</p>
<dt><code>hsub</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>vsub</code></p>
<dd> horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><code>in_h, ih</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>in_w, iw</code></p>
<dd> The input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> The input sample aspect ratio.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The x and y offset coordinates where the box is drawn.</p>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> The width and height of the drawn box.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> The thickness of the drawn box. </p>
<p> These constants allow the <i>x</i>, <i>y</i>, <i>w</i>, <i>h</i> and <i>t</i> expressions to refer to each other, so you may for example specify <tt>&quot;y=x/dar&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;h=w/dar&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw a black box around the edge of the input image: </p>
<pre>
        drawbox
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw a box with color red and an opacity of 50%: </p>
<pre>
        drawbox=10:20:200:60:red@0.5
</pre>
<p> The previous example can be specified as: </p>
<pre>
        drawbox=x=10:y=20:w=200:h=60:color=red@0.5
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Fill the box with pink color: </p>
<pre>
        drawbox=x=10:y=10:w=100:h=100:color=pink@0.5:t=fill
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw a 2-pixel red 2.40:1 mask: </p>
<pre>
        drawbox=x=-t:y=0.5*(ih-iw/2.4)-t:w=iw+t*2:h=iw/2.4+t*2:t=2:c=red
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same commands as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</p>
<h3>drawgraph</h3>
<p> Draw a graph using input video metadata. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>m1</code></p>
<dd> Set 1st frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.</p>
<dt><code>fg1</code></p>
<dd> Set 1st foreground color expression.</p>
<dt><code>m2</code></p>
<dd> Set 2nd frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.</p>
<dt><code>fg2</code></p>
<dd> Set 2nd foreground color expression.</p>
<dt><code>m3</code></p>
<dd> Set 3rd frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.</p>
<dt><code>fg3</code></p>
<dd> Set 3rd foreground color expression.</p>
<dt><code>m4</code></p>
<dd> Set 4th frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.</p>
<dt><code>fg4</code></p>
<dd> Set 4th foreground color expression.</p>
<dt><code>min</code></p>
<dd> Set minimal value of metadata value.</p>
<dt><code>max</code></p>
<dd> Set maximal value of metadata value.</p>
<dt><code>bg</code></p>
<dd> Set graph background color. Default is white.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set graph mode. </p>
<p> Available values for mode is:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>bar</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>dot</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>line</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;line&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>slide</code></p>
<dd> Set slide mode. </p>
<p> Available values for slide is:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Draw new frame when right border is reached.</p>
<dt><code>replace</code></p>
<dd> Replace old columns with new ones.</p>
<dt><code>scroll</code></p>
<dd> Scroll from right to left.</p>
<dt><code>rscroll</code></p>
<dd> Scroll from left to right.</p>
<dt><code>picture</code></p>
<dd> Draw single picture.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;frame&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>size</code></p>
<dd> Set size of graph video. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. The default value is <tt>&quot;900x256&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the output frame rate. Default value is <tt>25</tt>. </p>
<p> The foreground color expressions can use the following variables:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>MIN</code></font></p>
<dd> Minimal value of metadata value.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>MAX</code></font></p>
<dd> Maximal value of metadata value.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VAL</code></font></p>
<dd> Current metadata key value.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The color is defined as 0xAABBGGRR.</dl>
</dl>
<p> Example using metadata from <code>signalstats</code> filter: </p>
<pre>
        signalstats,drawgraph=lavfi.signalstats.YAVG:min=0:max=255
</pre>
<p> Example using metadata from <code>ebur128</code> filter: </p>
<pre>
        ebur128=metadata=1,adrawgraph=lavfi.r128.M:min=-120:max=5
</pre>
<h3>drawgrid</h3>
<p> Draw a grid on the input image. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The expressions which specify the coordinates of some point of grid intersection (meant to configure offset). Both default to 0.</p>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>height, h</code></p>
<dd> The expressions which specify the width and height of the grid cell, if 0 they are interpreted as the input width and height, respectively, minus <tt>&quot;thickness&quot;</tt>, so image gets framed. Default to 0.</p>
<dt><code>color, c</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the grid. For the general syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. If the special value <tt>&quot;invert&quot;</tt> is used, the grid color is the same as the video with inverted luma.</p>
<dt><code>thickness, t</code></p>
<dd> The expression which sets the thickness of the grid line. Default value is <tt>1</tt>. </p>
<p> See below for the list of accepted constants.</p>
<dt><code>replace</code></p>
<dd> Applicable if the input has alpha. With <tt>1</tt> the pixels of the painted grid will overwrite the video&#8217;s color and alpha pixels. Default is <tt>0</tt>, which composites the grid onto the input, leaving the video&#8217;s alpha intact.</dl>
<p> The parameters for <i>x</i>, <i>y</i>, <i>w</i> and <i>h</i> and <i>t</i> are expressions containing the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dar</code></p>
<dd> The input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (<i>w</i> / <i>h</i>) * <i>sar</i>.</p>
<dt><code>hsub</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>vsub</code></p>
<dd> horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><code>in_h, ih</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>in_w, iw</code></p>
<dd> The input grid cell width and height.</p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> The input sample aspect ratio.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The x and y coordinates of some point of grid intersection (meant to configure offset).</p>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> The width and height of the drawn cell.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> The thickness of the drawn cell. </p>
<p> These constants allow the <i>x</i>, <i>y</i>, <i>w</i>, <i>h</i> and <i>t</i> expressions to refer to each other, so you may for example specify <tt>&quot;y=x/dar&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;h=w/dar&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw a grid with cell 100&#215;100 pixels, thickness 2 pixels, with color red and an opacity of 50%: </p>
<pre>
        drawgrid=width=100:height=100:thickness=2:color=red@0.5
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw a white 3&#215;3 grid with an opacity of 50%: </p>
<pre>
        drawgrid=w=iw/3:h=ih/3:t=2:c=white@0.5
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same commands as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</p>
<h3>drawtext</h3>
<p> Draw a text string or text from a specified file on top of a video, using the libfreetype library. </p>
<p> To enable compilation of this filter, you need to configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libfreetype&quot;</tt>. To enable default font fallback and the <i>font</i> option you need to configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libfontconfig&quot;</tt>. To enable the <i>text_shaping</i> option, you need to configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libfribidi&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> <i>Syntax</i> </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>box</code></p>
<dd> Used to draw a box around text using the background color. The value must be either 1 (enable) or 0 (disable). The default value of <i>box</i> is 0.</p>
<dt><code>boxborderw</code></p>
<dd> Set the width of the border to be drawn around the box using <i>boxcolor</i>. The default value of <i>boxborderw</i> is 0.</p>
<dt><code>boxcolor</code></p>
<dd> The color to be used for drawing box around text. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>boxcolor</i> is &#8220;white&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>line_spacing</code></p>
<dd> Set the line spacing in pixels of the border to be drawn around the box using <i>box</i>. The default value of <i>line_spacing</i> is 0.</p>
<dt><code>borderw</code></p>
<dd> Set the width of the border to be drawn around the text using <i>bordercolor</i>. The default value of <i>borderw</i> is 0.</p>
<dt><code>bordercolor</code></p>
<dd> Set the color to be used for drawing border around text. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>bordercolor</i> is &#8220;black&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>expansion</code></p>
<dd> Select how the <i>text</i> is expanded. Can be either <tt>&quot;none&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;strftime&quot;</tt> (deprecated) or <tt>&quot;normal&quot;</tt> (default). See the <code>drawtext_expansion, Text expansion</code> section below for details.</p>
<dt><code>basetime</code></p>
<dd> Set a start time for the count. Value is in microseconds. Only applied in the deprecated strftime expansion mode. To emulate in normal expansion mode use the <tt>&quot;pts&quot;</tt> function, supplying the start time (in seconds) as the second argument.</p>
<dt><code>fix_bounds</code></p>
<dd> If true, check and fix text coords to avoid clipping.</p>
<dt><code>fontcolor</code></p>
<dd> The color to be used for drawing fonts. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>fontcolor</i> is &#8220;black&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>fontcolor_expr</code></p>
<dd> String which is expanded the same way as <i>text</i> to obtain dynamic <i>fontcolor</i> value. By default this option has empty value and is not processed. When this option is set, it overrides <i>fontcolor</i> option.</p>
<dt><code>font</code></p>
<dd> The font family to be used for drawing text. By default Sans.</p>
<dt><code>fontfile</code></p>
<dd> The font file to be used for drawing text. The path must be included. This parameter is mandatory if the fontconfig support is disabled.</p>
<dt><code>alpha</code></p>
<dd> Draw the text applying alpha blending. The value can be a number between 0.0 and 1.0. The expression accepts the same variables <i>x, y</i> as well. The default value is 1. Please see <i>fontcolor_expr</i>.</p>
<dt><code>fontsize</code></p>
<dd> The font size to be used for drawing text. The default value of <i>fontsize</i> is 16.</p>
<dt><code>text_shaping</code></p>
<dd> If set to 1, attempt to shape the text (for example, reverse the order of right-to-left text and join Arabic characters) before drawing it. Otherwise, just draw the text exactly as given. By default 1 (if supported).</p>
<dt><code>ft_load_flags</code></p>
<dd> The flags to be used for loading the fonts. </p>
<p> The flags map the corresponding flags supported by libfreetype, and are a combination of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><i>default</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>no_scale</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>no_hinting</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>render</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>no_bitmap</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>vertical_layout</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>force_autohint</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>crop_bitmap</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>pedantic</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>ignore_global_advance_width</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>no_recurse</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>ignore_transform</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>monochrome</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>linear_design</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>no_autohint</i></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is &#8220;default&#8221;. </p>
<p> For more information consult the documentation for the FT_LOAD_* libfreetype flags.</dl>
<dt><code>shadowcolor</code></p>
<dd> The color to be used for drawing a shadow behind the drawn text. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>shadowcolor</i> is &#8220;black&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>shadowx</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>shadowy</code></p>
<dd> The x and y offsets for the text shadow position with respect to the position of the text. They can be either positive or negative values. The default value for both is &#8220;0&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>start_number</code></p>
<dd> The starting frame number for the n/frame_num variable. The default value is &#8220;0&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>tabsize</code></p>
<dd> The size in number of spaces to use for rendering the tab. Default value is 4.</p>
<dt><code>timecode</code></p>
<dd> Set the initial timecode representation in &#8220;hh:mm:ss[:;.]ff&#8221; format. It can be used with or without text parameter. <i>timecode_rate</i> option must be specified.</p>
<dt><code>timecode_rate, rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the timecode frame rate (timecode only). Value will be rounded to nearest integer. Minimum value is &#8220;1&#8221;. Drop-frame timecode is supported for frame rates 30 &amp; 60.</p>
<dt><code>tc24hmax</code></p>
<dd> If set to 1, the output of the timecode option will wrap around at 24 hours. Default is 0 (disabled).</p>
<dt><code>text</code></p>
<dd> The text string to be drawn. The text must be a sequence of <font SIZE="-1">UTF-8</font> encoded characters. This parameter is mandatory if no file is specified with the parameter <i>textfile</i>.</p>
<dt><code>textfile</code></p>
<dd> A text file containing text to be drawn. The text must be a sequence of <font SIZE="-1">UTF-8</font> encoded characters. </p>
<p> This parameter is mandatory if no text string is specified with the parameter <i>text</i>. </p>
<p> If both <i>text</i> and <i>textfile</i> are specified, an error is thrown.</p>
<dt><code>reload</code></p>
<dd> If set to 1, the <i>textfile</i> will be reloaded before each frame. Be sure to update it atomically, or it may be read partially, or even fail.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The expressions which specify the offsets where text will be drawn within the video frame. They are relative to the top/left border of the output image. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> is &#8220;0&#8221;. </p>
<p> See below for the list of accepted constants and functions.</dl>
<p> The parameters for <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> are expressions containing the following constants and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dar</code></p>
<dd> input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (<i>w</i> / <i>h</i>) * <i>sar</i></p>
<dt><code>hsub</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>vsub</code></p>
<dd> horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><code>line_h, lh</code></p>
<dd> the height of each text line</p>
<dt><code>main_h, h, H</code></p>
<dd> the input height</p>
<dt><code>main_w, w, W</code></p>
<dd> the input width</p>
<dt><code>max_glyph_a, ascent</code></p>
<dd> the maximum distance from the baseline to the highest/upper grid coordinate used to place a glyph outline point, for all the rendered glyphs. It is a positive value, due to the grid&#8217;s orientation with the Y axis upwards.</p>
<dt><code>max_glyph_d, descent</code></p>
<dd> the maximum distance from the baseline to the lowest grid coordinate used to place a glyph outline point, for all the rendered glyphs. This is a negative value, due to the grid&#8217;s orientation, with the Y axis upwards.</p>
<dt><code>max_glyph_h</code></p>
<dd> maximum glyph height, that is the maximum height for all the glyphs contained in the rendered text, it is equivalent to <i>ascent</i> &#8211; <i>descent</i>.</p>
<dt><code>max_glyph_w</code></p>
<dd> maximum glyph width, that is the maximum width for all the glyphs contained in the rendered text</p>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> the number of input frame, starting from 0</p>
<dt><code>rand(min, max)</code></p>
<dd> return a random number included between <i>min</i> and <i>max</i></p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> The input sample aspect ratio.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> timestamp expressed in seconds, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input timestamp is unknown</p>
<dt><code>text_h, th</code></p>
<dd> the height of the rendered text</p>
<dt><code>text_w, tw</code></p>
<dd> the width of the rendered text</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> the x and y offset coordinates where the text is drawn. </p>
<p> These parameters allow the <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> expressions to refer to each other, so you can for example specify <tt>&quot;y=x/dar&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>pict_type</code></p>
<dd> A one character description of the current frame&#8217;s picture type.</p>
<dt><code>pkt_pos</code></p>
<dd> The current packet&#8217;s position in the input file or stream (in bytes, from the start of the input). A value of -1 indicates this info is not available.</p>
<dt><code>pkt_duration</code></p>
<dd> The current packet&#8217;s duration, in seconds.</p>
<dt><code>pkt_size</code></p>
<dd> The current packet&#8217;s size (in bytes).</dl>
<p> <i>Text expansion</i> </p>
<p> If <code>expansion</code> is set to <tt>&quot;strftime&quot;</tt>, the filter recognizes <code>strftime()</code> sequences in the provided text and expands them accordingly. Check the documentation of <code>strftime()</code>. This feature is deprecated. </p>
<p> If <code>expansion</code> is set to <tt>&quot;none&quot;</tt>, the text is printed verbatim. </p>
<p> If <code>expansion</code> is set to <tt>&quot;normal&quot;</tt> (which is the default), the following expansion mechanism is used. </p>
<p> The backslash character <code>\</code>, followed by any character, always expands to the second character. </p>
<p> Sequences of the form <tt>&quot;%{...}&quot;</tt> are expanded. The text between the braces is a function name, possibly followed by arguments separated by &#8216;:&#8217;. If the arguments contain special characters or delimiters (&#8216;:&#8217; or &#8216;}&#8217;), they should be escaped. </p>
<p> Note that they probably must also be escaped as the value for the <code>text</code> option in the filter argument string and as the filter argument in the filtergraph description, and possibly also for the shell, that makes up to four levels of escaping; using a text file avoids these problems. </p>
<p> The following functions are available:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>expr, e</code></p>
<dd> The expression evaluation result. </p>
<p> It must take one argument specifying the expression to be evaluated, which accepts the same constants and functions as the <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> values. Note that not all constants should be used, for example the text size is not known when evaluating the expression, so the constants <i>text_w</i> and <i>text_h</i> will have an undefined value.</p>
<dt><code>expr_int_format, eif</code></p>
<dd> Evaluate the expression&#8217;s value and output as formatted integer. </p>
<p> The first argument is the expression to be evaluated, just as for the <i>expr</i> function. The second argument specifies the output format. Allowed values are <code>x</code>, <code>X</code>, <code>d</code> and <code>u</code>. They are treated exactly as in the <tt>&quot;printf&quot;</tt> function. The third parameter is optional and sets the number of positions taken by the output. It can be used to add padding with zeros from the left.</p>
<dt><code>gmtime</code></p>
<dd> The time at which the filter is running, expressed in <font SIZE="-1">UTC.</font> It can accept an argument: a <code>strftime()</code> format string.</p>
<dt><code>localtime</code></p>
<dd> The time at which the filter is running, expressed in the local time zone. It can accept an argument: a <code>strftime()</code> format string.</p>
<dt><code>metadata</code></p>
<dd> Frame metadata. Takes one or two arguments. </p>
<p> The first argument is mandatory and specifies the metadata key. </p>
<p> The second argument is optional and specifies a default value, used when the metadata key is not found or empty. </p>
<p> Available metadata can be identified by inspecting entries starting with <font SIZE="-1">TAG</font> included within each frame section printed by running <tt>&quot;ffprobe -show_frames&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> String metadata generated in filters leading to the drawtext filter are also available.</p>
<dt><code>n, frame_num</code></p>
<dd> The frame number, starting from 0.</p>
<dt><code>pict_type</code></p>
<dd> A one character description of the current picture type.</p>
<dt><code>pts</code></p>
<dd> The timestamp of the current frame. It can take up to three arguments. </p>
<p> The first argument is the format of the timestamp; it defaults to <tt>&quot;flt&quot;</tt> for seconds as a decimal number with microsecond accuracy; <tt>&quot;hms&quot;</tt> stands for a formatted <i>[-]HH:MM:SS.mmm</i> timestamp with millisecond accuracy. <tt>&quot;gmtime&quot;</tt> stands for the timestamp of the frame formatted as <font SIZE="-1">UTC</font> time; <tt>&quot;localtime&quot;</tt> stands for the timestamp of the frame formatted as local time zone time. </p>
<p> The second argument is an offset added to the timestamp. </p>
<p> If the format is set to <tt>&quot;hms&quot;</tt>, a third argument <tt>&quot;24HH&quot;</tt> may be supplied to present the hour part of the formatted timestamp in 24h format (00-23). </p>
<p> If the format is set to <tt>&quot;localtime&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;gmtime&quot;</tt>, a third argument may be supplied: a <code>strftime()</code> format string. By default, <i>YYYY-MM-DD </i><font SIZE="-1"><i>HH:MM:SS</i></font><i></i> format will be used.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports altering parameters via commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>reinit</code></p>
<dd> Alter existing filter parameters. </p>
<p> Syntax for the argument is the same as for filter invocation, e.g. </p>
<pre>
        fontsize=56:fontcolor=green:text='Hello World'
</pre>
<p> Full filter invocation with sendcmd would look like this: </p>
<pre>
        sendcmd=c='56.0 drawtext reinit fontsize=56\:fontcolor=green\:text=Hello\ World'
</pre>
</dl>
<p> If the entire argument can&#8217;t be parsed or applied as valid values then the filter will continue with its existing parameters. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw &#8220;Test Text&#8221; with font FreeSerif, using the default values for the optional parameters. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontfile=/usr/share/fonts/truetype/freefont/FreeSerif.ttf: text='Test Text'&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw &#8216;Test Text&#8217; with font FreeSerif of size 24 at position x=100 and y=50 (counting from the top-left corner of the screen), text is yellow with a red box around it. Both the text and the box have an opacity of 20%. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontfile=/usr/share/fonts/truetype/freefont/FreeSerif.ttf: text='Test Text':\
                  x=100: y=50: fontsize=24: fontcolor=yellow@0.2: box=1: boxcolor=red@0.2&quot;
</pre>
<p> Note that the double quotes are not necessary if spaces are not used within the parameter list.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Show the text at the center of the video frame: </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontsize=30:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text='hello world':x=(w-text_w)/2:y=(h-text_h)/2&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Show the text at a random position, switching to a new position every 30 seconds: </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontsize=30:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text='hello world':x=if(eq(mod(t\,30)\,0)\,rand(0\,(w-text_w))\,x):y=if(eq(mod(t\,30)\,0)\,rand(0\,(h-text_h))\,y)&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Show a text line sliding from right to left in the last row of the video frame. The file <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>LONG_LINE</i></font><i></i> is assumed to contain a single line with no newlines. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontsize=15:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=LONG_LINE:y=h-line_h:x=-50*t&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Show the content of file <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>CREDITS</i></font><i></i> off the bottom of the frame and scroll up. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontsize=20:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:textfile=CREDITS:y=h-20*t&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw a single green letter &#8220;g&#8221;, at the center of the input video. The glyph baseline is placed at half screen height. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontsize=60:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:fontcolor=green:text=g:x=(w-max_glyph_w)/2:y=h/2-ascent&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Show text for 1 second every 3 seconds: </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:fontcolor=white:x=100:y=x/dar:enable=lt(mod(t\,3)\,1):text='blink'&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Use fontconfig to set the font. Note that the colons need to be escaped. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext='fontfile=Linux Libertine O-40\:style=Semibold:text=FFmpeg'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw &#8220;Test Text&#8221; with font size dependent on height of the video. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;text='Test Text': fontsize=h/30: x=(w-text_w)/2: y=(h-text_h*2)&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Print the date of a real-time encoding (see <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/3-strftime/">strftime</a></code>(3)): </p>
<pre>
        drawtext='fontfile=FreeSans.ttf:text=%{localtime\:%a %b %d %Y}'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Show text fading in and out (appearing/disappearing): </p>
<pre>
        #!/bin/sh
        DS=1.0 # display start
        DE=10.0 # display end
        FID=1.5 # fade in duration
        FOD=5 # fade out duration
        ffplay -f lavfi &quot;color,drawtext=text=TEST:fontsize=50:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:fontcolor_expr=ff0000%{eif\\: clip(255*(1*between(t\, $DS + $FID\, $DE - $FOD) + ((t - $DS)/$FID)*between(t\, $DS\, $DS + $FID) + (-(t - $DE)/$FOD)*between(t\, $DE - $FOD\, $DE) )\, 0\, 255) \\: x\\: 2 }&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Horizontally align multiple separate texts. Note that <code>max_glyph_a</code> and the <code>fontsize</code> value are included in the <code>y</code> offset. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=fontfile=FreeSans.ttf:text=DOG:fontsize=24:x=10:y=20+24-max_glyph_a,
        drawtext=fontfile=FreeSans.ttf:text=cow:fontsize=24:x=80:y=20+24-max_glyph_a
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Plot special <i>lavf.image2dec.source_basename</i> metadata onto each frame if such metadata exists. Otherwise, plot the string &#8220;<font SIZE="-1">NA&#8221;.</font> Note that image2 demuxer must have option <code>-export_path_metadata 1</code> for the special metadata fields to be available for filters. </p>
<pre>
        drawtext=&quot;fontsize=20:fontcolor=white:fontfile=FreeSans.ttf:text='%{metadata\:lavf.image2dec.source_basename\:NA}':x=10:y=10&quot;
</pre>
</dl>
<p> For more information about libfreetype, check: &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://www.freetype.org/">http://www.freetype.org/</a></code>&gt;. </p>
<p> For more information about fontconfig, check: &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://freedesktop.org/software/fontconfig/fontconfig-user.html">http://freedesktop.org/software/fontconfig/fontconfig-user.html</a></code>&gt;. </p>
<p> For more information about libfribidi, check: &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://fribidi.org/">http://fribidi.org/</a></code>&gt;.</p>
<h3>edgedetect</h3>
<p> Detect and draw edges. The filter uses the Canny Edge Detection algorithm. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>low</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>high</code></p>
<dd> Set low and high threshold values used by the Canny thresholding algorithm. </p>
<p> The high threshold selects the &#8220;strong&#8221; edge pixels, which are then connected through 8-connectivity with the &#8220;weak&#8221; edge pixels selected by the low threshold. </p>
<p> <i>low</i> and <i>high</i> threshold values must be chosen in the range [0,1], and <i>low</i> should be lesser or equal to <i>high</i>. </p>
<p> Default value for <i>low</i> is <tt>&quot;20/255&quot;</tt>, and default value for <i>high</i> is <tt>&quot;50/255&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Define the drawing mode.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>wires</code></p>
<dd> Draw white/gray wires on black background.</p>
<dt><code>colormix</code></p>
<dd> Mix the colors to create a paint/cartoon effect.</p>
<dt><code>canny</code></p>
<dd> Apply Canny edge detector on all selected planes.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <i>wires</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Select planes for filtering. By default all available planes are filtered.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Standard edge detection with custom values for the hysteresis thresholding: </p>
<pre>
        edgedetect=low=0.1:high=0.4
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Painting effect without thresholding: </p>
<pre>
        edgedetect=mode=colormix:high=0
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>elbg</h3>
<p> Apply a posterize effect using the <font SIZE="-1">ELBG</font> (Enhanced <font SIZE="-1">LBG</font>) algorithm. </p>
<p> For each input image, the filter will compute the optimal mapping from the input to the output given the codebook length, that is the number of distinct output colors. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>codebook_length, l</code></p>
<dd> Set codebook length. The value must be a positive integer, and represents the number of distinct output colors. Default value is 256.</p>
<dt><code>nb_steps, n</code></p>
<dd> Set the maximum number of iterations to apply for computing the optimal mapping. The higher the value the better the result and the higher the computation time. Default value is 1.</p>
<dt><code>seed, s</code></p>
<dd> Set a random seed, must be an integer included between 0 and <font SIZE="-1">UINT32_MAX.</font> If not specified, or if explicitly set to -1, the filter will try to use a good random seed on a best effort basis.</p>
<dt><code>pal8</code></p>
<dd> Set pal8 output pixel format. This option does not work with codebook length greater than 256.</dl>
<h3>entropy</h3>
<p> Measure graylevel entropy in histogram of color channels of video frames. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Can be either <i>normal</i> or <i>diff</i>. Default is <i>normal</i>. </p>
<p> <i>diff</i> mode measures entropy of histogram delta values, absolute differences between neighbour histogram values.</dl>
<h3>epx</h3>
<p> Apply the <font SIZE="-1">EPX</font> magnification filter which is designed for pixel art. </p>
<p> It accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> Set the scaling dimension: <tt>2</tt> for <tt>&quot;2xEPX&quot;</tt>, <tt>3</tt> for <tt>&quot;3xEPX&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>3</tt>.</dl>
<h3>eq</h3>
<p> Set brightness, contrast, saturation and approximate gamma adjustment. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>contrast</code></p>
<dd> Set the contrast expression. The value must be a float value in range <tt>&quot;-1000.0&quot;</tt> to <tt>1000.0</tt>. The default value is &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>brightness</code></p>
<dd> Set the brightness expression. The value must be a float value in range <tt>&quot;-1.0&quot;</tt> to <tt>1.0</tt>. The default value is &#8220;0&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>saturation</code></p>
<dd> Set the saturation expression. The value must be a float in range <tt>0.0</tt> to <tt>3.0</tt>. The default value is &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>gamma</code></p>
<dd> Set the gamma expression. The value must be a float in range <tt>0.1</tt> to <tt>10.0</tt>. The default value is &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_r</code></p>
<dd> Set the gamma expression for red. The value must be a float in range <tt>0.1</tt> to <tt>10.0</tt>. The default value is &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_g</code></p>
<dd> Set the gamma expression for green. The value must be a float in range <tt>0.1</tt> to <tt>10.0</tt>. The default value is &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_b</code></p>
<dd> Set the gamma expression for blue. The value must be a float in range <tt>0.1</tt> to <tt>10.0</tt>. The default value is &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_weight</code></p>
<dd> Set the gamma weight expression. It can be used to reduce the effect of a high gamma value on bright image areas, e.g. keep them from getting overamplified and just plain white. The value must be a float in range <tt>0.0</tt> to <tt>1.0</tt>. A value of <tt>0.0</tt> turns the gamma correction all the way down while <tt>1.0</tt> leaves it at its full strength. Default is &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>eval</code></p>
<dd> Set when the expressions for brightness, contrast, saturation and gamma expressions are evaluated. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>init</code></p>
<dd> only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization or when a command is processed</p>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> evaluate expressions for each incoming frame</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>init</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> The expressions accept the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> frame count of the input frame starting from 0</p>
<dt><code>pos</code></p>
<dd> byte position of the corresponding packet in the input file, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if unspecified</p>
<dt><code>r</code></p>
<dd> frame rate of the input video, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input frame rate is unknown</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> timestamp expressed in seconds, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input timestamp is unknown</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> The filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>contrast</code></p>
<dd> Set the contrast expression.</p>
<dt><code>brightness</code></p>
<dd> Set the brightness expression.</p>
<dt><code>saturation</code></p>
<dd> Set the saturation expression.</p>
<dt><code>gamma</code></p>
<dd> Set the gamma expression.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_r</code></p>
<dd> Set the gamma_r expression.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_g</code></p>
<dd> Set gamma_g expression.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_b</code></p>
<dd> Set gamma_b expression.</p>
<dt><code>gamma_weight</code></p>
<dd> Set gamma_weight expression. </p>
<p> The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</dl>
<h3>erosion</h3>
<p> Apply erosion effect to the video. </p>
<p> This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3&#215;3) minimum. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold3</code></p>
<dd> Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535. If 0, plane will remain unchanged.</p>
<dt><code>coordinates</code></p>
<dd> Flag which specifies the pixel to refer to. Default is 255 i.e. all eight pixels are used. </p>
<p> Flags to local 3&#215;3 coordinates maps like this: </p>
<pre>
    1 2 3
    4   5
    6 7 8
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>estdif</h3>
<p> Deinterlace the input video (&#8220;estdif&#8221; stands for &#8220;Edge Slope Tracing Deinterlacing Filter&#8221;). </p>
<p> Spatial only filter that uses edge slope tracing algorithm to interpolate missing lines. It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> The interlacing mode to adopt. It accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Output one frame for each frame.</p>
<dt><code>field</code></p>
<dd> Output one frame for each field.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default value is <tt>&quot;field&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>parity</code></p>
<dd> The picture field parity assumed for the input interlaced video. It accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>tff</code></p>
<dd> Assume the top field is first.</p>
<dt><code>bff</code></p>
<dd> Assume the bottom field is first.</p>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Enable automatic detection of field parity.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default value is <tt>&quot;auto&quot;</tt>. If the interlacing is unknown or the decoder does not export this information, top field first will be assumed.</dl>
<dt><code>deint</code></p>
<dd> Specify which frames to deinterlace. Accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>all</code></p>
<dd> Deinterlace all frames.</p>
<dt><code>interlaced</code></p>
<dd> Only deinterlace frames marked as interlaced.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default value is <tt>&quot;all&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>rslope</code></p>
<dd> Specify the search radius for edge slope tracing. Default value is 1. Allowed range is from 1 to 15.</p>
<dt><code>redge</code></p>
<dd> Specify the search radius for best edge matching. Default value is 2. Allowed range is from 0 to 15.</p>
<dt><code>interp</code></p>
<dd> Specify the interpolation used. Default is 4-point interpolation. It accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>2p</code></p>
<dd> Two-point interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>4p</code></p>
<dd> Four-point interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>6p</code></p>
<dd> Six-point interpolation.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options.</p>
<h3>exposure</h3>
<p> Adjust exposure of the video stream. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>exposure</code></p>
<dd> Set the exposure correction in <font SIZE="-1">EV.</font> Allowed range is from -3.0 to 3.0 <font SIZE="-1">EV</font> Default value is 0 <font SIZE="-1">EV.</font></p>
<dt><code>black</code></p>
<dd> Set the black level correction. Allowed range is from -1.0 to 1.0. Default value is 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options.</p>
<h3>extractplanes</h3>
<p> Extract color channel components from input video stream into separate grayscale video streams. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set plane(s) to extract. </p>
<p> Available values for planes are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>u</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>r</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>g</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Choosing planes not available in the input will result in an error. That means you cannot select <tt>&quot;r&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;g&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;b&quot;</tt> planes with <tt>&quot;y&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;u&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;v&quot;</tt> planes at same time.</dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Extract luma, u and v color channel component from input video frame into 3 grayscale outputs: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i video.avi &#8211; filter_complex &#8216;extractplanes=y+u+v[y][u][v]&#8217; &#8211; map &#8216;[y]&#8217; y.avi &#8211; map &#8216;[u]&#8217; u.avi &#8211; map &#8216;[v]&#8217; v.avi
</dl>
<h3>fade</h3>
<p> Apply a fade-in/out effect to the input video. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>type, t</code></p>
<dd> The effect type can be either &#8220;in&#8221; for a fade-in, or &#8220;out&#8221; for a fade-out effect. Default is <tt>&quot;in&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>start_frame, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the number of the frame to start applying the fade effect at. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>nb_frames, n</code></p>
<dd> The number of frames that the fade effect lasts. At the end of the fade-in effect, the output video will have the same intensity as the input video. At the end of the fade-out transition, the output video will be filled with the selected <code>color</code>. Default is 25.</p>
<dt><code>alpha</code></p>
<dd> If set to 1, fade only alpha channel, if one exists on the input. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>start_time, st</code></p>
<dd> Specify the timestamp (in seconds) of the frame to start to apply the fade effect. If both start_frame and start_time are specified, the fade will start at whichever comes last. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>duration, d</code></p>
<dd> The number of seconds for which the fade effect has to last. At the end of the fade-in effect the output video will have the same intensity as the input video, at the end of the fade-out transition the output video will be filled with the selected <code>color</code>. If both duration and nb_frames are specified, duration is used. Default is 0 (nb_frames is used by default).</p>
<dt><code>color, c</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the fade. Default is &#8220;black&#8221;.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Fade in the first 30 frames of video: </p>
<pre>
        fade=in:0:30
</pre>
<p> The command above is equivalent to: </p>
<pre>
        fade=t=in:s=0:n=30
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Fade out the last 45 frames of a 200-frame video: </p>
<pre>
        fade=out:155:45
        fade=type=out:start_frame=155:nb_frames=45
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Fade in the first 25 frames and fade out the last 25 frames of a 1000-frame video: </p>
<pre>
        fade=in:0:25, fade=out:975:25
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Make the first 5 frames yellow, then fade in from frame 5-24: </p>
<pre>
        fade=in:5:20:color=yellow
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Fade in alpha over first 25 frames of video: </p>
<pre>
        fade=in:0:25:alpha=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Make the first 5.5 seconds black, then fade in for 0.5 seconds: </p>
<pre>
        fade=t=in:st=5.5:d=0.5
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>fftdnoiz</h3>
<p> Denoise frames using 3D <font SIZE="-1">FFT</font> (frequency domain filtering). </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>sigma</code></p>
<dd> Set the noise sigma constant. This sets denoising strength. Default value is 1. Allowed range is from 0 to 30. Using very high sigma with low overlap may give blocking artifacts.</p>
<dt><code>amount</code></p>
<dd> Set amount of denoising. By default all detected noise is reduced. Default value is 1. Allowed range is from 0 to 1.</p>
<dt><code>block</code></p>
<dd> Set size of block, Default is 4, can be 3, 4, 5 or 6. Actual size of block in pixels is 2 to power of <i>block</i>, so by default block size in pixels is 2^4 which is 16.</p>
<dt><code>overlap</code></p>
<dd> Set block overlap. Default is 0.5. Allowed range is from 0.2 to 0.8.</p>
<dt><code>prev</code></p>
<dd> Set number of previous frames to use for denoising. By default is set to 0.</p>
<dt><code>next</code></p>
<dd> Set number of next frames to to use for denoising. By default is set to 0.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set planes which will be filtered, by default are all available filtered except alpha.</dl>
<h3>fftfilt</h3>
<p> Apply arbitrary expressions to samples in frequency domain</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dc_Y</code></p>
<dd> Adjust the dc value (gain) of the luma plane of the image. The filter accepts an integer value in range <tt>0</tt> to <tt>1000</tt>. The default value is set to <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>dc_U</code></p>
<dd> Adjust the dc value (gain) of the 1st chroma plane of the image. The filter accepts an integer value in range <tt>0</tt> to <tt>1000</tt>. The default value is set to <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>dc_V</code></p>
<dd> Adjust the dc value (gain) of the 2nd chroma plane of the image. The filter accepts an integer value in range <tt>0</tt> to <tt>1000</tt>. The default value is set to <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>weight_Y</code></p>
<dd> Set the frequency domain weight expression for the luma plane.</p>
<dt><code>weight_U</code></p>
<dd> Set the frequency domain weight expression for the 1st chroma plane.</p>
<dt><code>weight_V</code></p>
<dd> Set the frequency domain weight expression for the 2nd chroma plane.</p>
<dt><code>eval</code></p>
<dd> Set when the expressions are evaluated. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>init</code></p>
<dd> Only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization.</p>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Evaluate expressions for each incoming frame.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>init</code>. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following variables:</dl>
<dt><code>X</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>Y</code></p>
<dd> The coordinates of the current sample.</p>
<dt><code>W</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>H</code></p>
<dd> The width and height of the image.</p>
<dt><code>N</code></p>
<dd> The number of input frame, starting from 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> High-pass: </p>
<pre>
        fftfilt=dc_Y=128:weight_Y='squish(1-(Y+X)/100)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Low-pass: </p>
<pre>
        fftfilt=dc_Y=0:weight_Y='squish((Y+X)/100-1)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Sharpen: </p>
<pre>
        fftfilt=dc_Y=0:weight_Y='1+squish(1-(Y+X)/100)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Blur: </p>
<pre>
        fftfilt=dc_Y=0:weight_Y='exp(-4 * ((Y+X)/(W+H)))'
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>field</h3>
<p> Extract a single field from an interlaced image using stride arithmetic to avoid wasting <font SIZE="-1">CPU</font> time. The output frames are marked as non-interlaced. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>type</code></p>
<dd> Specify whether to extract the top (if the value is <tt>0</tt> or <tt>&quot;top&quot;</tt>) or the bottom field (if the value is <tt>1</tt> or <tt>&quot;bottom&quot;</tt>).</dl>
<h3>fieldhint</h3>
<p> Create new frames by copying the top and bottom fields from surrounding frames supplied as numbers by the hint file.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>hint</code></p>
<dd> Set file containing hints: absolute/relative frame numbers. </p>
<p> There must be one line for each frame in a clip. Each line must contain two numbers separated by the comma, optionally followed by <tt>&quot;-&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;+&quot;</tt>. Numbers supplied on each line of file can not be out of [N-1,N+1] where N is current frame number for <tt>&quot;absolute&quot;</tt> mode or out of [-1, 1] range for <tt>&quot;relative&quot;</tt> mode. First number tells from which frame to pick up top field and second number tells from which frame to pick up bottom field. </p>
<p> If optionally followed by <tt>&quot;+&quot;</tt> output frame will be marked as interlaced, else if followed by <tt>&quot;-&quot;</tt> output frame will be marked as progressive, else it will be marked same as input frame. If optionally followed by <tt>&quot;t&quot;</tt> output frame will use only top field, or in case of <tt>&quot;b&quot;</tt> it will use only bottom field. If line starts with <tt>&quot;#&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;;&quot;</tt> that line is skipped.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Can be item <tt>&quot;absolute&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;relative&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;absolute&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> Example of first several lines of <tt>&quot;hint&quot;</tt> file for <tt>&quot;relative&quot;</tt> mode: </p>
<pre>
        0,0 - # first frame
        1,0 - # second frame, use third's frame top field and second's frame bottom field
        1,0 - # third frame, use fourth's frame top field and third's frame bottom field
        1,0 -
        0,0 -
        0,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        0,0 -
        0,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        0,0 -
</pre>
<h3>fieldmatch</h3>
<p> Field matching filter for inverse telecine. It is meant to reconstruct the progressive frames from a telecined stream. The filter does not drop duplicated frames, so to achieve a complete inverse telecine <tt>&quot;fieldmatch&quot;</tt> needs to be followed by a decimation filter such as <code>decimate</code> in the filtergraph. </p>
<p> The separation of the field matching and the decimation is notably motivated by the possibility of inserting a de-interlacing filter fallback between the two. If the source has mixed telecined and real interlaced content, <tt>&quot;fieldmatch&quot;</tt> will not be able to match fields for the interlaced parts. But these remaining combed frames will be marked as interlaced, and thus can be de-interlaced by a later filter such as <code>yadif</code> before decimation. </p>
<p> In addition to the various configuration options, <tt>&quot;fieldmatch&quot;</tt> can take an optional second stream, activated through the <code>ppsrc</code> option. If enabled, the frames reconstruction will be based on the fields and frames from this second stream. This allows the first input to be pre-processed in order to help the various algorithms of the filter, while keeping the output lossless (assuming the fields are matched properly). Typically, a field-aware denoiser, or brightness/contrast adjustments can help. </p>
<p> Note that this filter uses the same algorithms as <font SIZE="-1">TIVTC/TFM</font> (AviSynth project) and <font SIZE="-1">VIVTC/VFM</font> (VapourSynth project). The later is a light clone of <font SIZE="-1">TFM</font> from which <tt>&quot;fieldmatch&quot;</tt> is based on. While the semantic and usage are very close, some behaviour and options names can differ. </p>
<p> The <code>decimate</code> filter currently only works for constant frame rate input. If your input has mixed telecined (30fps) and progressive content with a lower framerate like 24fps use the following filterchain to produce the necessary cfr stream: <tt>&quot;dejudder,fps=30000/1001,fieldmatch,decimate&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>order</code></p>
<dd> Specify the assumed field order of the input stream. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Auto detect parity (use FFmpeg&#8217;s internal parity value).</p>
<dt><code>bff</code></p>
<dd> Assume bottom field first.</p>
<dt><code>tff</code></p>
<dd> Assume top field first.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Note that it is sometimes recommended not to trust the parity announced by the stream. </p>
<p> Default value is <i>auto</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set the matching mode or strategy to use. <code>pc</code> mode is the safest in the sense that it won&#8217;t risk creating jerkiness due to duplicate frames when possible, but if there are bad edits or blended fields it will end up outputting combed frames when a good match might actually exist. On the other hand, <code>pcn_ub</code> mode is the most risky in terms of creating jerkiness, but will almost always find a good frame if there is one. The other values are all somewhere in between <code>pc</code> and <code>pcn_ub</code> in terms of risking jerkiness and creating duplicate frames versus finding good matches in sections with bad edits, orphaned fields, blended fields, etc. </p>
<p> More details about p/c/n/u/b are available in <code>p/c/n/u/b meaning</code> section. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>pc</code></p>
<dd> 2-way matching (p/c)</p>
<dt><code>pc_n</code></p>
<dd> 2-way matching, and trying 3rd match if still combed (p/c + n)</p>
<dt><code>pc_u</code></p>
<dd> 2-way matching, and trying 3rd match (same order) if still combed (p/c + u)</p>
<dt><code>pc_n_ub</code></p>
<dd> 2-way matching, trying 3rd match if still combed, and trying 4th/5th matches if still combed (p/c + n + u/b)</p>
<dt><code>pcn</code></p>
<dd> 3-way matching (p/c/n)</p>
<dt><code>pcn_ub</code></p>
<dd> 3-way matching, and trying 4th/5th matches if all 3 of the original matches are detected as combed (p/c/n + u/b)</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The parenthesis at the end indicate the matches that would be used for that mode assuming <code>order</code>=<i>tff</i> (and <code>field</code> on <i>auto</i> or <i>top</i>). </p>
<p> In terms of speed <code>pc</code> mode is by far the fastest and <code>pcn_ub</code> is the slowest. </p>
<p> Default value is <i>pc_n</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>ppsrc</code></p>
<dd> Mark the main input stream as a pre-processed input, and enable the secondary input stream as the clean source to pick the fields from. See the filter introduction for more details. It is similar to the <code>clip2</code> feature from <font SIZE="-1">VFM/TFM.</font> </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>0</tt> (disabled).</p>
<dt><code>field</code></p>
<dd> Set the field to match from. It is recommended to set this to the same value as <code>order</code> unless you experience matching failures with that setting. In certain circumstances changing the field that is used to match from can have a large impact on matching performance. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Automatic (same value as <code>order</code>).</p>
<dt><code>bottom</code></p>
<dd> Match from the bottom field.</p>
<dt><code>top</code></p>
<dd> Match from the top field.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <i>auto</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>mchroma</code></p>
<dd> Set whether or not chroma is included during the match comparisons. In most cases it is recommended to leave this enabled. You should set this to <tt>0</tt> only if your clip has bad chroma problems such as heavy rainbowing or other artifacts. Setting this to <tt>0</tt> could also be used to speed things up at the cost of some accuracy. </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>y0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y1</code></p>
<dd> These define an exclusion band which excludes the lines between <code>y0</code> and <code>y1</code> from being included in the field matching decision. An exclusion band can be used to ignore subtitles, a logo, or other things that may interfere with the matching. <code>y0</code> sets the starting scan line and <code>y1</code> sets the ending line; all lines in between <code>y0</code> and <code>y1</code> (including <code>y0</code> and <code>y1</code>) will be ignored. Setting <code>y0</code> and <code>y1</code> to the same value will disable the feature. <code>y0</code> and <code>y1</code> defaults to <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>scthresh</code></p>
<dd> Set the scene change detection threshold as a percentage of maximum change on the luma plane. Good values are in the <tt>&quot;[8.0, 14.0]&quot;</tt> range. Scene change detection is only relevant in case <code>combmatch</code>=<i>sc</i>. The range for <code>scthresh</code> is <tt>&quot;[0.0, 100.0]&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>12.0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>combmatch</code></p>
<dd> When <code>combatch</code> is not <i>none</i>, <tt>&quot;fieldmatch&quot;</tt> will take into account the combed scores of matches when deciding what match to use as the final match. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> No final matching based on combed scores.</p>
<dt><code>sc</code></p>
<dd> Combed scores are only used when a scene change is detected.</p>
<dt><code>full</code></p>
<dd> Use combed scores all the time.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <i>sc</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>combdbg</code></p>
<dd> Force <tt>&quot;fieldmatch&quot;</tt> to calculate the combed metrics for certain matches and print them. This setting is known as <code>micout</code> in <font SIZE="-1">TFM/VFM</font> vocabulary. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> No forced calculation.</p>
<dt><code>pcn</code></p>
<dd> Force p/c/n calculations.</p>
<dt><code>pcnub</code></p>
<dd> Force p/c/n/u/b calculations.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <i>none</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>cthresh</code></p>
<dd> This is the area combing threshold used for combed frame detection. This essentially controls how &#8220;strong&#8221; or &#8220;visible&#8221; combing must be to be detected. Larger values mean combing must be more visible and smaller values mean combing can be less visible or strong and still be detected.
