Valid settings are from <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt> (every pixel will be detected as combed) to <tt>255</tt> (no pixel will be detected as combed). This is basically a pixel difference value. A good range is <tt>&quot;[8, 12]&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>9</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>chroma</code></p>
<dd> Sets whether or not chroma is considered in the combed frame decision. Only disable this if your source has chroma problems (rainbowing, etc.) that are causing problems for the combed frame detection with chroma enabled. Actually, using <code>chroma</code>=<i>0</i> is usually more reliable, except for the case where there is chroma only combing in the source. </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>blockx</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>blocky</code></p>
<dd> Respectively set the x-axis and y-axis size of the window used during combed frame detection. This has to do with the size of the area in which <code>combpel</code> pixels are required to be detected as combed for a frame to be declared combed. See the <code>combpel</code> parameter description for more info. Possible values are any number that is a power of 2 starting at 4 and going up to 512. </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>16</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>combpel</code></p>
<dd> The number of combed pixels inside any of the <code>blocky</code> by <code>blockx</code> size blocks on the frame for the frame to be detected as combed. While <code>cthresh</code> controls how &#8220;visible&#8221; the combing must be, this setting controls &#8220;how much&#8221; combing there must be in any localized area (a window defined by the <code>blockx</code> and <code>blocky</code> settings) on the frame. Minimum value is <tt>0</tt> and maximum is <tt>&quot;blocky x blockx&quot;</tt> (at which point no frames will ever be detected as combed). This setting is known as <font SIZE="-1"><code>MI</code></font> in <font SIZE="-1">TFM/VFM</font> vocabulary. </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>80</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>p/c/n/u/b meaning</i> </p>
<p> p/c/n </p>
<p> We assume the following telecined stream: </p>
<pre>
        Top fields:     1 2 2 3 4
        Bottom fields:  1 2 3 4 4
</pre>
<p> The numbers correspond to the progressive frame the fields relate to. Here, the first two frames are progressive, the 3rd and 4th are combed, and so on. </p>
<p> When <tt>&quot;fieldmatch&quot;</tt> is configured to run a matching from bottom (<code>field</code>=<i>bottom</i>) this is how this input stream get transformed: </p>
<pre>
        Input stream:
                        T     1 2 2 3 4
                        B     1 2 3 4 4   &lt;-- matching reference
        
        Matches:              c c n n c
        
        Output stream:
                        T     1 2 3 4 4
                        B     1 2 3 4 4
</pre>
<p> As a result of the field matching, we can see that some frames get duplicated. To perform a complete inverse telecine, you need to rely on a decimation filter after this operation. See for instance the <code>decimate</code> filter. </p>
<p> The same operation now matching from top fields (<code>field</code>=<i>top</i>) looks like this: </p>
<pre>
        Input stream:
                        T     1 2 2 3 4   &lt;-- matching reference
                        B     1 2 3 4 4
        
        Matches:              c c p p c
        
        Output stream:
                        T     1 2 2 3 4
                        B     1 2 2 3 4
</pre>
<p> In these examples, we can see what <i>p</i>, <i>c</i> and <i>n</i> mean; basically, they refer to the frame and field of the opposite parity:</p>
<dl>
<dt>*&lt;<i>p</i> matches the field of the opposite parity in the previous frame&gt;</p>
<dd>
<dt>*&lt;<i>c</i> matches the field of the opposite parity in the current frame&gt;</p>
<dd>
<dt>*&lt;<i>n</i> matches the field of the opposite parity in the next frame&gt;</p>
<dd></dl>
<p> u/b </p>
<p> The <i>u</i> and <i>b</i> matching are a bit special in the sense that they match from the opposite parity flag. In the following examples, we assume that we are currently matching the 2nd frame (Top:2, bottom:2). According to the match, a &#8216;x&#8217; is placed above and below each matched fields. </p>
<p> With bottom matching (<code>field</code>=<i>bottom</i>): </p>
<pre>
        Match:           c         p           n          b          u
        
                         x       x               x        x          x
          Top          1 2 2     1 2 2       1 2 2      1 2 2      1 2 2
          Bottom       1 2 3     1 2 3       1 2 3      1 2 3      1 2 3
                         x         x           x        x              x
        
        Output frames:
                         2          1          2          2          2
                         2          2          2          1          3
</pre>
<p> With top matching (<code>field</code>=<i>top</i>): </p>
<pre>
        Match:           c         p           n          b          u
        
                         x         x           x        x              x
          Top          1 2 2     1 2 2       1 2 2      1 2 2      1 2 2
          Bottom       1 2 3     1 2 3       1 2 3      1 2 3      1 2 3
                         x       x               x        x          x
        
        Output frames:
                         2          2          2          1          2
                         2          1          3          2          2
</pre>
<p> <i>Examples</i> </p>
<p> Simple <font SIZE="-1">IVTC</font> of a top field first telecined stream: </p>
<pre>
        fieldmatch=order=tff:combmatch=none, decimate
</pre>
<p> Advanced <font SIZE="-1">IVTC,</font> with fallback on <code>yadif</code> for still combed frames: </p>
<pre>
        fieldmatch=order=tff:combmatch=full, yadif=deint=interlaced, decimate
</pre>
<h3>fieldorder</h3>
<p> Transform the field order of the input video. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>order</code></p>
<dd> The output field order. Valid values are <i>tff</i> for top field first or <i>bff</i> for bottom field first.</dl>
<p> The default value is <code>tff</code>. </p>
<p> The transformation is done by shifting the picture content up or down by one line, and filling the remaining line with appropriate picture content. This method is consistent with most broadcast field order converters. </p>
<p> If the input video is not flagged as being interlaced, or it is already flagged as being of the required output field order, then this filter does not alter the incoming video. </p>
<p> It is very useful when converting to or from <font SIZE="-1">PAL DV</font> material, which is bottom field first. </p>
<p> For example: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i in.vob -vf &quot;fieldorder=bff&quot; out.dv
</pre>
<h3>fifo, afifo</h3>
<p> Buffer input images and send them when they are requested. </p>
<p> It is mainly useful when auto-inserted by the libavfilter framework. </p>
<p> It does not take parameters.</p>
<h3>fillborders</h3>
<p> Fill borders of the input video, without changing video stream dimensions. Sometimes video can have garbage at the four edges and you may not want to crop video input to keep size multiple of some number. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>left</code></p>
<dd> Number of pixels to fill from left border.</p>
<dt><code>right</code></p>
<dd> Number of pixels to fill from right border.</p>
<dt><code>top</code></p>
<dd> Number of pixels to fill from top border.</p>
<dt><code>bottom</code></p>
<dd> Number of pixels to fill from bottom border.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set fill mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>smear</code></p>
<dd> fill pixels using outermost pixels</p>
<dt><code>mirror</code></p>
<dd> fill pixels using mirroring (half sample symmetric)</p>
<dt><code>fixed</code></p>
<dd> fill pixels with constant value</p>
<dt><code>reflect</code></p>
<dd> fill pixels using reflecting (whole sample symmetric)</p>
<dt><code>wrap</code></p>
<dd> fill pixels using wrapping</p>
<dt><code>fade</code></p>
<dd> fade pixels to constant value</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <i>smear</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd> Set color for pixels in fixed or fade mode. Default is <i>black</i>.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</p>
<h3>find_rect</h3>
<p> Find a rectangular object </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>object</code></p>
<dd> Filepath of the object image, needs to be in gray8.</p>
<dt><code>threshold</code></p>
<dd> Detection threshold, default is 0.5.</p>
<dt><code>mipmaps</code></p>
<dd> Number of mipmaps, default is 3.</p>
<dt><code>xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax</code></p>
<dd> Specifies the rectangle in which to search.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Cover a rectangular object by the supplied image of a given video using <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i file.ts -vf find_rect=newref.pgm,cover_rect=cover.jpg:mode=cover new.mkv
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>floodfill</h3>
<p> Flood area with values of same pixel components with another values. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd> Set pixel x coordinate.</p>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Set pixel y coordinate.</p>
<dt><code>s0</code></p>
<dd> Set source #0 component value.</p>
<dt><code>s1</code></p>
<dd> Set source #1 component value.</p>
<dt><code>s2</code></p>
<dd> Set source #2 component value.</p>
<dt><code>s3</code></p>
<dd> Set source #3 component value.</p>
<dt><code>d0</code></p>
<dd> Set destination #0 component value.</p>
<dt><code>d1</code></p>
<dd> Set destination #1 component value.</p>
<dt><code>d2</code></p>
<dd> Set destination #2 component value.</p>
<dt><code>d3</code></p>
<dd> Set destination #3 component value.</dl>
<h3>format</h3>
<p> Convert the input video to one of the specified pixel formats. Libavfilter will try to pick one that is suitable as input to the next filter. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>pix_fmts</code></p>
<dd> A &#8216;|&#8217;-separated list of pixel format names, such as &#8220;pix_fmts=yuv420p|monow|rgb24&#8221;.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Convert the input video to the <i>yuv420p</i> format </p>
<pre>
        format=pix_fmts=yuv420p
</pre>
<p> Convert the input video to any of the formats in the list </p>
<pre>
        format=pix_fmts=yuv420p|yuv444p|yuv410p
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>fps</h3>
<p> Convert the video to specified constant frame rate by duplicating or dropping frames as necessary. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>fps</code></p>
<dd> The desired output frame rate. The default is <tt>25</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>start_time</code></p>
<dd> Assume the first <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> should be the given value, in seconds. This allows for padding/trimming at the start of stream. By default, no assumption is made about the first frame&#8217;s expected <font SIZE="-1">PTS,</font> so no padding or trimming is done. For example, this could be set to 0 to pad the beginning with duplicates of the first frame if a video stream starts after the audio stream or to trim any frames with a negative <font SIZE="-1">PTS.</font></p>
<dt><code>round</code></p>
<dd> Timestamp (<font SIZE="-1">PTS</font>) rounding method. </p>
<p> Possible values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>zero</code></p>
<dd> round towards 0</p>
<dt><code>inf</code></p>
<dd> round away from 0</p>
<dt><code>down</code></p>
<dd> round towards -infinity</p>
<dt><code>up</code></p>
<dd> round towards +infinity</p>
<dt><code>near</code></p>
<dd> round to nearest</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default is <tt>&quot;near&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>eof_action</code></p>
<dd> Action performed when reading the last frame. </p>
<p> Possible values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>round</code></p>
<dd> Use same timestamp rounding method as used for other frames.</p>
<dt><code>pass</code></p>
<dd> Pass through last frame if input duration has not been reached yet.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default is <tt>&quot;round&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> Alternatively, the options can be specified as a flat string: <i>fps</i>[:<i>start_time</i>[:<i>round</i>]]. </p>
<p> See also the <code>setpts</code> filter. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> A typical usage in order to set the fps to 25: </p>
<pre>
        fps=fps=25
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Sets the fps to 24, using abbreviation and rounding method to round to nearest: </p>
<pre>
        fps=fps=film:round=near
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>framepack</h3>
<p> Pack two different video streams into a stereoscopic video, setting proper metadata on supported codecs. The two views should have the same size and framerate and processing will stop when the shorter video ends. Please note that you may conveniently adjust view properties with the <code>scale</code> and <code>fps</code> filters. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> The desired packing format. Supported values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>sbs</code></p>
<dd> The views are next to each other (default).</p>
<dt><code>tab</code></p>
<dd> The views are on top of each other.</p>
<dt><code>lines</code></p>
<dd> The views are packed by line.</p>
<dt><code>columns</code></p>
<dd> The views are packed by column.</p>
<dt><code>frameseq</code></p>
<dd> The views are temporally interleaved.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> Some examples: </p>
<p>#Convert left and right views into a frame &#8211; sequential video<br />
ffmpeg &#8211; i LEFT &#8211; i RIGHT &#8211; filter_complex framepack = frameseq OUTPUT</p>
<p>#Convert views into a side &#8211; by &#8211; side video with the same output resolution as the input<br />
                                                           ffmpeg &#8211;<br />
                                                       i LEFT &#8211; i RIGHT &#8211; filter_complex [0:v] scale = w = iw / 2 [left],<br />
                                           [1:v] scale = w = iw / 2 [right], [left][right] framepack = sbs OUTPUT</p>
<h3>framerate</h3>
<p> Change the frame rate by interpolating new video output frames from the source frames. </p>
<p> This filter is not designed to function correctly with interlaced media. If you wish to change the frame rate of interlaced media then you are required to deinterlace before this filter and re-interlace after this filter. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted options follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>fps</code></p>
<dd> Specify the output frames per second. This option can also be specified as a value alone. The default is <tt>50</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>interp_start</code></p>
<dd> Specify the start of a range where the output frame will be created as a linear interpolation of two frames. The range is [<tt>0</tt>&#8211;<tt>255</tt>], the default is <tt>15</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>interp_end</code></p>
<dd> Specify the end of a range where the output frame will be created as a linear interpolation of two frames. The range is [<tt>0</tt>&#8211;<tt>255</tt>], the default is <tt>240</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>scene</code></p>
<dd> Specify the level at which a scene change is detected as a value between 0 and 100 to indicate a new scene; a low value reflects a low probability for the current frame to introduce a new scene, while a higher value means the current frame is more likely to be one. The default is <tt>8.2</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>flags</code></p>
<dd> Specify flags influencing the filter process. </p>
<p> Available value for <i>flags</i> is:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>scene_change_detect, scd</code></p>
<dd> Enable scene change detection using the value of the option <i>scene</i>. This flag is enabled by default.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<h3>framestep</h3>
<p> Select one frame every N-th frame. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>step</code></p>
<dd> Select frame after every <tt>&quot;step&quot;</tt> frames. Allowed values are positive integers higher than 0. Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</dl>
<h3>freezedetect</h3>
<p> Detect frozen video. </p>
<p> This filter logs a message and sets frame metadata when it detects that the input video has no significant change in content during a specified duration. Video freeze detection calculates the mean average absolute difference of all the components of video frames and compares it to a noise floor. </p>
<p> The printed times and duration are expressed in seconds. The <tt>&quot;lavfi.freezedetect.freeze_start&quot;</tt> metadata key is set on the first frame whose timestamp equals or exceeds the detection duration and it contains the timestamp of the first frame of the freeze. The <tt>&quot;lavfi.freezedetect.freeze_duration&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;lavfi.freezedetect.freeze_end&quot;</tt> metadata keys are set on the first frame after the freeze. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>noise, n</code></p>
<dd> Set noise tolerance. Can be specified in dB (in case &#8220;dB&#8221; is appended to the specified value) or as a difference ratio between 0 and 1. Default is -60dB, or 0.001.</p>
<dt><code>duration, d</code></p>
<dd> Set freeze duration until notification (default is 2 seconds).</dl>
<h3>freezeframes</h3>
<p> Freeze video frames. </p>
<p> This filter freezes video frames using frame from 2nd input. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>first</code></p>
<dd> Set number of first frame from which to start freeze.</p>
<dt><code>last</code></p>
<dd> Set number of last frame from which to end freeze.</p>
<dt><code>replace</code></p>
<dd> Set number of frame from 2nd input which will be used instead of replaced frames.</dl>
<h3>frei0r</h3>
<p> Apply a frei0r effect to the input video. </p>
<p> To enable the compilation of this filter, you need to install the frei0r header and configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-frei0r&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filter_name</code></p>
<dd> The name of the frei0r effect to load. If the environment variable <font SIZE="-1"><code>FREI0R_PATH</code></font> is defined, the frei0r effect is searched for in each of the directories specified by the colon-separated list in <font SIZE="-1"><code>FREI0R_PATH</code></font>. Otherwise, the standard frei0r paths are searched, in this order: <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>HOME/</i></font><i>.frei0r-1/lib/</i>, <i>/usr/local/lib/frei0r-1/</i>, <i>/usr/lib/frei0r-1/</i>.</p>
<dt><code>filter_params</code></p>
<dd> A &#8216;|&#8217;-separated list of parameters to pass to the frei0r effect.</dl>
<p> A frei0r effect parameter can be a boolean (its value is either &#8220;y&#8221; or &#8220;n&#8221;), a double, a color (specified as <i>R</i>/<i>G</i>/<i>B</i>, where <i>R</i>, <i>G</i>, and <i>B</i> are floating point numbers between 0.0 and 1.0, inclusive) or a color description as specified in the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>, a position (specified as <i>X</i>/<i>Y</i>, where <i>X</i> and <i>Y</i> are floating point numbers) and/or a string. </p>
<p> The number and types of parameters depend on the loaded effect. If an effect parameter is not specified, the default value is set. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply the distort0r effect, setting the first two double parameters: </p>
<pre>
        frei0r=filter_name=distort0r:filter_params=0.5|0.01
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply the colordistance effect, taking a color as the first parameter: </p>
<pre>
        frei0r=colordistance:0.2/0.3/0.4
        frei0r=colordistance:violet
        frei0r=colordistance:0x112233
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply the perspective effect, specifying the top left and top right image positions: </p>
<pre>
        frei0r=perspective:0.2/0.2|0.8/0.2
</pre>
</dl>
<p> For more information, see &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://frei0r.dyne.org">http://frei0r.dyne.org</a></code>&gt; </p>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the <code>filter_params</code> option as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>fspp</h3>
<p> Apply fast and simple postprocessing. It is a faster version of <code>spp</code>. </p>
<p> It splits (I)DCT into horizontal/vertical passes. Unlike the simple post- processing filter, one of them is performed once per block, not per pixel. This allows for much higher speed. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>quality</code></p>
<dd> Set quality. This option defines the number of levels for averaging. It accepts an integer in the range 4-5. Default value is <tt>4</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>qp</code></p>
<dd> Force a constant quantization parameter. It accepts an integer in range 0-63. If not set, the filter will use the <font SIZE="-1">QP</font> from the video stream (if available).</p>
<dt><code>strength</code></p>
<dd> Set filter strength. It accepts an integer in range -15 to 32. Lower values mean more details but also more artifacts, while higher values make the image smoother but also blurrier. Default value is <tt>0</tt> X <font SIZE="-1">PSNR</font> optimal.</p>
<dt><code>use_bframe_qp</code></p>
<dd> Enable the use of the <font SIZE="-1">QP</font> from the B-Frames if set to <tt>1</tt>. Using this option may cause flicker since the B-Frames have often larger <font SIZE="-1">QP.</font> Default is <tt>0</tt> (not enabled).</dl>
<h3>gblur</h3>
<p> Apply Gaussian blur filter. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>sigma</code></p>
<dd> Set horizontal sigma, standard deviation of Gaussian blur. Default is <tt>0.5</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>steps</code></p>
<dd> Set number of steps for Gaussian approximation. Default is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to filter. By default all planes are filtered.</p>
<dt><code>sigmaV</code></p>
<dd> Set vertical sigma, if negative it will be same as <tt>&quot;sigma&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same commands as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</p>
<h3>geq</h3>
<p> Apply generic equation to each pixel. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>lum_expr, lum</code></p>
<dd> Set the luminance expression.</p>
<dt><code>cb_expr, cb</code></p>
<dd> Set the chrominance blue expression.</p>
<dt><code>cr_expr, cr</code></p>
<dd> Set the chrominance red expression.</p>
<dt><code>alpha_expr, a</code></p>
<dd> Set the alpha expression.</p>
<dt><code>red_expr, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the red expression.</p>
<dt><code>green_expr, g</code></p>
<dd> Set the green expression.</p>
<dt><code>blue_expr, b</code></p>
<dd> Set the blue expression.</dl>
<p> The colorspace is selected according to the specified options. If one of the <code>lum_expr</code>, <code>cb_expr</code>, or <code>cr_expr</code> options is specified, the filter will automatically select a YCbCr colorspace. If one of the <code>red_expr</code>, <code>green_expr</code>, or <code>blue_expr</code> options is specified, it will select an <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> colorspace. </p>
<p> If one of the chrominance expression is not defined, it falls back on the other one. If no alpha expression is specified it will evaluate to opaque value. If none of chrominance expressions are specified, they will evaluate to the luminance expression. </p>
<p> The expressions can use the following variables and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>N</code></p>
<dd> The sequential number of the filtered frame, starting from <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>X</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>Y</code></p>
<dd> The coordinates of the current sample.</p>
<dt><code>W</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>H</code></p>
<dd> The width and height of the image.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SW</code></font></p>
<dd>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SH</code></font></p>
<dd> Width and height scale depending on the currently filtered plane. It is the ratio between the corresponding luma plane number of pixels and the current plane ones. E.g. for <font SIZE="-1">YUV4:2:0</font> the values are <tt>&quot;1,1&quot;</tt> for the luma plane, and <tt>&quot;0.5,0.5&quot;</tt> for chroma planes.</p>
<dt><code>T</code></p>
<dd> Time of the current frame, expressed in seconds.</p>
<dt><code>p(x, y)</code></p>
<dd> Return the value of the pixel at location (<i>x</i>,<i>y</i>) of the current plane.</p>
<dt><code>lum(x, y)</code></p>
<dd> Return the value of the pixel at location (<i>x</i>,<i>y</i>) of the luminance plane.</p>
<dt><code>cb(x, y)</code></p>
<dd> Return the value of the pixel at location (<i>x</i>,<i>y</i>) of the blue-difference chroma plane. Return 0 if there is no such plane.</p>
<dt><code>cr(x, y)</code></p>
<dd> Return the value of the pixel at location (<i>x</i>,<i>y</i>) of the red-difference chroma plane. Return 0 if there is no such plane.</p>
<dt><code>r(x, y)</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>g(x, y)</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>b(x, y)</code></p>
<dd> Return the value of the pixel at location (<i>x</i>,<i>y</i>) of the red/green/blue component. Return 0 if there is no such component.</p>
<dt><code>alpha(x, y)</code></p>
<dd> Return the value of the pixel at location (<i>x</i>,<i>y</i>) of the alpha plane. Return 0 if there is no such plane.</p>
<dt><code>psum(x,y), lumsum(x, y), cbsum(x,y), crsum(x,y), rsum(x,y), gsum(x,y), bsum(x,y), alphasum(x,y)</code></p>
<dd> Sum of sample values in the rectangle from (0,0) to (x,y), this allows obtaining sums of samples within a rectangle. See the functions without the sum postfix.</p>
<dt><code>interpolation</code></p>
<dd> Set one of interpolation methods:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>nearest, n</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bilinear, b</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is bilinear.</dl>
</dl>
<p> For functions, if <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> are outside the area, the value will be automatically clipped to the closer edge. </p>
<p> Please note that this filter can use multiple threads in which case each slice will have its own expression state. If you want to use only a single expression state because your expressions depend on previous state then you should limit the number of filter threads to 1. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Flip the image horizontally: </p>
<pre>
        geq=p(W-X\,Y)
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a bidimensional sine wave, with angle <tt>&quot;PI/3&quot;</tt> and a wavelength of 100 pixels: </p>
<pre>
        geq=128 + 100*sin(2*(PI/100)*(cos(PI/3)*(X-50*T) + sin(PI/3)*Y)):128:128
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a fancy enigmatic moving light: </p>
<pre>
        nullsrc=s=256x256,geq=<a href="//www.systutorials.com/docs/linux/man/1-random/">random</a>(1)/hypot(X-cos(N*0.07)*W/2-W/2\,Y-sin(N*0.09)*H/2-H/2)^2*1000000*sin(N*0.02):128:128
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a quick emboss effect: </p>
<pre>
        format=gray,geq=lum_expr='(p(X,Y)+(256-p(X-4,Y-4)))/2'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Modify <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> components depending on pixel position: </p>
<pre>
        geq=r='X/W*r(X,Y)':g='(1-X/W)*g(X,Y)':b='(H-Y)/H*b(X,Y)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Create a radial gradient that is the same size as the input (also see the <code>vignette</code> filter): </p>
<pre>
        geq=lum=255*gauss((X/W-0.5)*3)*gauss((Y/H-0.5)*3)/gauss(0)/gauss(0),format=gray
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>gradfun</h3>
<p> Fix the banding artifacts that are sometimes introduced into nearly flat regions by truncation to 8-bit color depth. Interpolate the gradients that should go where the bands are, and dither them. </p>
<p> It is designed for playback only. Do not use it prior to lossy compression, because compression tends to lose the dither and bring back the bands. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>strength</code></p>
<dd> The maximum amount by which the filter will change any one pixel. This is also the threshold for detecting nearly flat regions. Acceptable values range from .51 to 64; the default value is 1.2. Out-of-range values will be clipped to the valid range.</p>
<dt><code>radius</code></p>
<dd> The neighborhood to fit the gradient to. A larger radius makes for smoother gradients, but also prevents the filter from modifying the pixels near detailed regions. Acceptable values are 8-32; the default value is 16. Out-of-range values will be clipped to the valid range.</dl>
<p> Alternatively, the options can be specified as a flat string: <i>strength</i>[:<i>radius</i>] </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply the filter with a <tt>3.5</tt> strength and radius of <tt>8</tt>: </p>
<pre>
        gradfun=3.5:8
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Specify radius, omitting the strength (which will fall-back to the default value): </p>
<pre>
        gradfun=radius=8
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>graphmonitor</h3>
<p> Show various filtergraph stats. </p>
<p> With this filter one can debug complete filtergraph. Especially issues with links filling with queued frames. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Set video output size. Default is <i>hd720</i>.</p>
<dt><code>opacity, o</code></p>
<dd> Set video opacity. Default is <i>0.9</i>. Allowed range is from <i>0</i> to <i>1</i>.</p>
<dt><code>mode, m</code></p>
<dd> Set output mode, can be <i>fulll</i> or <i>compact</i>. In <i>compact</i> mode only filters with some queued frames have displayed stats.</p>
<dt><code>flags, f</code></p>
<dd> Set flags which enable which stats are shown in video. </p>
<p> Available values for flags are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>queue</code></p>
<dd> Display number of queued frames in each link.</p>
<dt><code>frame_count_in</code></p>
<dd> Display number of frames taken from filter.</p>
<dt><code>frame_count_out</code></p>
<dd> Display number of frames given out from filter.</p>
<dt><code>pts</code></p>
<dd> Display current filtered frame pts.</p>
<dt><code>time</code></p>
<dd> Display current filtered frame time.</p>
<dt><code>timebase</code></p>
<dd> Display time base for filter link.</p>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> Display used format for filter link.</p>
<dt><code>size</code></p>
<dd> Display video size or number of audio channels in case of audio used by filter link.</p>
<dt><code>rate</code></p>
<dd> Display video frame rate or sample rate in case of audio used by filter link.</p>
<dt><code>eof</code></p>
<dd> Display link output status.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set upper limit for video rate of output stream, Default value is <i>25</i>. This guarantee that output video frame rate will not be higher than this value.</dl>
<h3>greyedge</h3>
<p> A color constancy variation filter which estimates scene illumination via grey edge algorithm and corrects the scene colors accordingly. </p>
<p> See: &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://staff.science.uva.nl/th.gevers/pub/GeversTIP07.pdf">https://staff.science.uva.nl/th.gevers/pub/GeversTIP07.pdf</a></code>&gt; </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>difford</code></p>
<dd> The order of differentiation to be applied on the scene. Must be chosen in the range [0,2] and default value is 1.</p>
<dt><code>minknorm</code></p>
<dd> The Minkowski parameter to be used for calculating the Minkowski distance. Must be chosen in the range [0,20] and default value is 1. Set to 0 for getting max value instead of calculating Minkowski distance.</p>
<dt><code>sigma</code></p>
<dd> The standard deviation of Gaussian blur to be applied on the scene. Must be chosen in the range [0,1024.0] and default value = 1. floor( <i>sigma</i> * <code><a href="/docs/linux/man/3-break_off_sigma/">break_off_sigma</a></code>(3) ) can&#8217;t be equal to 0 if <i>difford</i> is greater than 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Grey Edge: </p>
<pre>
        greyedge=difford=1:minknorm=5:sigma=2
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Max Edge: </p>
<pre>
        greyedge=difford=1:minknorm=0:sigma=2
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>haldclut</h3>
<p> Apply a Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> to a video stream. </p>
<p> First input is the video stream to process, and second one is the Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT.</font> The Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> input can be a simple picture or a complete video stream. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>shortest</code></p>
<dd> Force termination when the shortest input terminates. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>repeatlast</code></p>
<dd> Continue applying the last <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> after the end of the stream. A value of <tt>0</tt> disable the filter after the last frame of the <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> is reached. Default is <tt>1</tt>.</dl>
<p> <tt>&quot;haldclut&quot;</tt> also has the same interpolation options as <code>lut3d</code> (both filters share the same internals). </p>
<p> This filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options. </p>
<p> More information about the Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> can be found on Eskil Steenberg&#8217;s website (Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> author) at &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://www.quelsolaar.com/technology/clut.html">http://www.quelsolaar.com/technology/clut.html</a></code>&gt;. </p>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the <tt>&quot;interp&quot;</tt> option as <code>commands</code>. </p>
<p> <i>Workflow examples</i> </p>
<p> Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> video stream </p>
<p> Generate an identity Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> stream altered with various effects: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -f lavfi -i B&lt;haldclutsrc&gt;=8 -vf &quot;hue=H=2*PI*t:s=sin(2*PI*t)+1, curves=cross_process&quot; -t 10 -c:v ffv1 clut.nut
</pre>
<p> Note: make sure you use a lossless codec. </p>
<p> Then use it with <tt>&quot;haldclut&quot;</tt> to apply it on some random stream: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; f lavfi &#8211; i mandelbrot &#8211; i clut.nut &#8211; filter_complex &#8216;[0][1] haldclut&#8217; &#8211; t 20 mandelclut.mkv</p>
<p> The Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> will be applied to the 10 first seconds (duration of <i>clut.nut</i>), then the latest picture of that <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> stream will be applied to the remaining frames of the <tt>&quot;mandelbrot&quot;</tt> stream. </p>
<p> Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> with preview </p>
<p> A Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> is supposed to be a squared image of <tt>&quot;Level*Level*Level&quot;</tt> by <tt>&quot;Level*Level*Level&quot;</tt> pixels. For a given Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT,</font> FFmpeg will select the biggest possible square starting at the top left of the picture. The remaining padding pixels (bottom or right) will be ignored. This area can be used to add a preview of the Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT.</font> </p>
<p> Typically, the following generated Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> will be supported by the <tt>&quot;haldclut&quot;</tt> filter: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; f lavfi &#8211; i B &lt; haldclutsrc &gt;= 8 &#8211; vf &#8221;<br />
                                            pad = iw + 320 [padded_clut];<br />
smptebars = s = 320&#215;256, split[a][b];<br />
[padded_clut][a] overlay = W &#8211; 320 : h, curves = color_negative[main];<br />
[main][b] overlay = W &#8211; 320 &#8221; -frames:v 1 clut.png</p>
<p> It contains the original and a preview of the effect of the <font SIZE="-1">CLUT: SMPTE</font> color bars are displayed on the right-top, and below the same color bars processed by the color changes. </p>
<p> Then, the effect of this Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT</font> can be visualized with: </p>
<pre>
        ffplay input.mkv -vf &quot;movie=clut.png, [in] haldclut&quot;
</pre>
<h3>hflip</h3>
<p> Flip the input video horizontally. </p>
<p> For example, to horizontally flip the input video with <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i in.avi -vf &quot;hflip&quot; out.avi
</pre>
<h3>histeq</h3>
<p> This filter applies a global color histogram equalization on a per-frame basis. </p>
<p> It can be used to correct video that has a compressed range of pixel intensities. The filter redistributes the pixel intensities to equalize their distribution across the intensity range. It may be viewed as an &#8220;automatically adjusting contrast filter&#8221;. This filter is useful only for correcting degraded or poorly captured source video. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>strength</code></p>
<dd> Determine the amount of equalization to be applied. As the strength is reduced, the distribution of pixel intensities more-and-more approaches that of the input frame. The value must be a float number in the range [0,1] and defaults to 0.200.</p>
<dt><code>intensity</code></p>
<dd> Set the maximum intensity that can generated and scale the output values appropriately. The strength should be set as desired and then the intensity can be limited if needed to avoid washing-out. The value must be a float number in the range [0,1] and defaults to 0.210.</p>
<dt><code>antibanding</code></p>
<dd> Set the antibanding level. If enabled the filter will randomly vary the luminance of output pixels by a small amount to avoid banding of the histogram. Possible values are <tt>&quot;none&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;weak&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;strong&quot;</tt>. It defaults to <tt>&quot;none&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<h3>histogram</h3>
<p> Compute and draw a color distribution histogram for the input video. </p>
<p> The computed histogram is a representation of the color component distribution in an image. </p>
<p> Standard histogram displays the color components distribution in an image. Displays color graph for each color component. Shows distribution of the Y, U, V, A or R, G, B components, depending on input format, in the current frame. Below each graph a color component scale meter is shown. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>level_height</code></p>
<dd> Set height of level. Default value is <tt>200</tt>. Allowed range is [50, 2048].</p>
<dt><code>scale_height</code></p>
<dd> Set height of color scale. Default value is <tt>12</tt>. Allowed range is [0, 40].</p>
<dt><code>display_mode</code></p>
<dd> Set display mode. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>stack</code></p>
<dd> Per color component graphs are placed below each other.</p>
<dt><code>parade</code></p>
<dd> Per color component graphs are placed side by side.</p>
<dt><code>overlay</code></p>
<dd> Presents information identical to that in the <tt>&quot;parade&quot;</tt>, except that the graphs representing color components are superimposed directly over one another.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;stack&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>levels_mode</code></p>
<dd> Set mode. Can be either <tt>&quot;linear&quot;</tt>, or <tt>&quot;logarithmic&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;linear&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>components</code></p>
<dd> Set what color components to display. Default is <tt>7</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>fgopacity</code></p>
<dd> Set foreground opacity. Default is <tt>0.7</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>bgopacity</code></p>
<dd> Set background opacity. Default is <tt>0.5</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Calculate and draw histogram: </p>
<pre>
        ffplay -i input -vf histogram
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>hqdn3d</h3>
<p> This is a high precision/quality 3d denoise filter. It aims to reduce image noise, producing smooth images and making still images really still. It should enhance compressibility. </p>
<p> It accepts the following optional parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>luma_spatial</code></p>
<dd> A non-negative floating point number which specifies spatial luma strength. It defaults to 4.0.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_spatial</code></p>
<dd> A non-negative floating point number which specifies spatial chroma strength. It defaults to 3.0*<i>luma_spatial</i>/4.0.</p>
<dt><code>luma_tmp</code></p>
<dd> A floating point number which specifies luma temporal strength. It defaults to 6.0*<i>luma_spatial</i>/4.0.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_tmp</code></p>
<dd> A floating point number which specifies chroma temporal strength. It defaults to <i>luma_tmp</i>*<i>chroma_spatial</i>/<i>luma_spatial</i>.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</p>
<h3>hwdownload</h3>
<p> Download hardware frames to system memory. </p>
<p> The input must be in hardware frames, and the output a non-hardware format. Not all formats will be supported on the output &#8211; it may be necessary to insert an additional <code>format</code> filter immediately following in the graph to get the output in a supported format.</p>
<h3>hwmap</h3>
<p> Map hardware frames to system memory or to another device. </p>
<p> This filter has several different modes of operation; which one is used depends on the input and output formats:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Hardware frame input, normal frame output </p>
<p> Map the input frames to system memory and pass them to the output. If the original hardware frame is later required (for example, after overlaying something else on part of it), the <code>hwmap</code> filter can be used again in the next mode to retrieve it.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Normal frame input, hardware frame output </p>
<p> If the input is actually a software-mapped hardware frame, then unmap it &#8211; that is, return the original hardware frame. </p>
<p> Otherwise, a device must be provided. Create new hardware surfaces on that device for the output, then map them back to the software format at the input and give those frames to the preceding filter. This will then act like the <code>hwupload</code> filter, but may be able to avoid an additional copy when the input is already in a compatible format.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Hardware frame input and output </p>
<p> A device must be supplied for the output, either directly or with the <code>derive_device</code> option. The input and output devices must be of different types and compatible &#8211; the exact meaning of this is system-dependent, but typically it means that they must refer to the same underlying hardware context (for example, refer to the same graphics card). </p>
<p> If the input frames were originally created on the output device, then unmap to retrieve the original frames. </p>
<p> Otherwise, map the frames to the output device &#8211; create new hardware frames on the output corresponding to the frames on the input.</dl>
<p> The following additional parameters are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set the frame mapping mode. Some combination of:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><i>read</i></p>
<dd> The mapped frame should be readable.</p>
<dt><i>write</i></p>
<dd> The mapped frame should be writeable.</p>
<dt><i>overwrite</i></p>
<dd> The mapping will always overwrite the entire frame. </p>
<p> This may improve performance in some cases, as the original contents of the frame need not be loaded.</p>
<dt><i>direct</i></p>
<dd> The mapping must not involve any copying. </p>
<p> Indirect mappings to copies of frames are created in some cases where either direct mapping is not possible or it would have unexpected properties. Setting this flag ensures that the mapping is direct and will fail if that is not possible.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Defaults to <i>read+write</i> if not specified.</dl>
<dt><code>derive_device</code> <i>type</i></p>
<dd> Rather than using the device supplied at initialisation, instead derive a new device of type <i>type</i> from the device the input frames exist on.</p>
<dt><code>reverse</code></p>
<dd> In a hardware to hardware mapping, map in reverse &#8211; create frames in the sink and map them back to the source. This may be necessary in some cases where a mapping in one direction is required but only the opposite direction is supported by the devices being used. </p>
<p> This option is dangerous &#8211; it may break the preceding filter in undefined ways if there are any additional constraints on that filter&#8217;s output. Do not use it without fully understanding the implications of its use.</dl>
<h3>hwupload</h3>
<p> Upload system memory frames to hardware surfaces. </p>
<p> The device to upload to must be supplied when the filter is initialised. If using ffmpeg, select the appropriate device with the <code>-filter_hw_device</code> option or with the <code>derive_device</code> option. The input and output devices must be of different types and compatible &#8211; the exact meaning of this is system-dependent, but typically it means that they must refer to the same underlying hardware context (for example, refer to the same graphics card). </p>
<p> The following additional parameters are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>derive_device</code> <i>type</i></p>
<dd> Rather than using the device supplied at initialisation, instead derive a new device of type <i>type</i> from the device the input frames exist on.</dl>
<h3>hwupload_cuda</h3>
<p> Upload system memory frames to a <font SIZE="-1">CUDA</font> device. </p>
<p> It accepts the following optional parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>device</code></p>
<dd> The number of the <font SIZE="-1">CUDA</font> device to use</dl>
<h3>hqx</h3>
<p> Apply a high-quality magnification filter designed for pixel art. This filter was originally created by Maxim Stepin. </p>
<p> It accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> Set the scaling dimension: <tt>2</tt> for <tt>&quot;hq2x&quot;</tt>, <tt>3</tt> for <tt>&quot;hq3x&quot;</tt> and <tt>4</tt> for <tt>&quot;hq4x&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>3</tt>.</dl>
<h3>hstack</h3>
<p> Stack input videos horizontally. </p>
<p> All streams must be of same pixel format and of same height. </p>
<p> Note that this filter is faster than using <code>overlay</code> and <code>pad</code> filter to create same output. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>inputs</code></p>
<dd> Set number of input streams. Default is 2.</p>
<dt><code>shortest</code></p>
<dd> If set to 1, force the output to terminate when the shortest input terminates. Default value is 0.</dl>
<h3>hue</h3>
<p> Modify the hue and/or the saturation of the input. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> Specify the hue angle as a number of degrees. It accepts an expression, and defaults to &#8220;0&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the saturation in the [-10,10] range. It accepts an expression and defaults to &#8220;1&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>H</code></p>
<dd> Specify the hue angle as a number of radians. It accepts an expression, and defaults to &#8220;0&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd> Specify the brightness in the [-10,10] range. It accepts an expression and defaults to &#8220;0&#8221;.</dl>
<p> <code>h</code> and <code>H</code> are mutually exclusive, and can&#8217;t be specified at the same time. </p>
<p> The <code>b</code>, <code>h</code>, <code>H</code> and <code>s</code> option values are expressions containing the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> frame count of the input frame starting from 0</p>
<dt><code>pts</code></p>
<dd> presentation timestamp of the input frame expressed in time base units</p>
<dt><code>r</code></p>
<dd> frame rate of the input video, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input frame rate is unknown</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> timestamp expressed in seconds, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input timestamp is unknown</p>
<dt><code>tb</code></p>
<dd> time base of the input video</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set the hue to 90 degrees and the saturation to 1.0: </p>
<pre>
        hue=h=90:s=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Same command but expressing the hue in radians: </p>
<pre>
        hue=H=PI/2:s=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Rotate hue and make the saturation swing between 0 and 2 over a period of 1 second: </p>
<pre>
        hue=&quot;H=2*PI*t: s=sin(2*PI*t)+1&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply a 3 seconds saturation fade-in effect starting at 0: </p>
<pre>
        hue=&quot;s=min(t/3\,1)&quot;
</pre>
<p> The general fade-in expression can be written as: </p>
<pre>
        hue=&quot;s=min(0\, max((t-START)/DURATION\, 1))&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply a 3 seconds saturation fade-out effect starting at 5 seconds: </p>
<pre>
        hue=&quot;s=max(0\, min(1\, (8-t)/3))&quot;
</pre>
<p> The general fade-out expression can be written as: </p>
<pre>
        hue=&quot;s=max(0\, min(1\, (START+DURATION-t)/DURATION))&quot;
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>s</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>H</code></p>
<dd> Modify the hue and/or the saturation and/or brightness of the input video. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</dl>
<h3>hysteresis</h3>
<p> Grow first stream into second stream by connecting components. This makes it possible to build more robust edge masks. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be copied from first stream. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</p>
<dt><code>threshold</code></p>
<dd> Set threshold which is used in filtering. If pixel component value is higher than this value filter algorithm for connecting components is activated. By default value is 0.</dl>
<p> The <tt>&quot;hysteresis&quot;</tt> filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options.</p>
<h3>identity</h3>
<p> Obtain the identity score between two input videos. </p>
<p> This filter takes two input videos. </p>
<p> Both input videos must have the same resolution and pixel format for this filter to work correctly. Also it assumes that both inputs have the same number of frames, which are compared one by one. </p>
<p> The obtained per component, average, min and max identity score is printed through the logging system. </p>
<p> The filter stores the calculated identity scores of each frame in frame metadata. </p>
<p> In the below example the input file <i>main.mpg</i> being processed is compared with the reference file <i>ref.mpg</i>. </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi identity -f null -
</pre>
<h3>idet</h3>
<p> Detect video interlacing type. </p>
<p> This filter tries to detect if the input frames are interlaced, progressive, top or bottom field first. It will also try to detect fields that are repeated between adjacent frames (a sign of telecine). </p>
<p> Single frame detection considers only immediately adjacent frames when classifying each frame. Multiple frame detection incorporates the classification history of previous frames. </p>
<p> The filter will log these metadata values:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>single.current_frame</code></p>
<dd> Detected type of current frame using single-frame detection. One of: &#8220;tff&#8221; (top field first), &#8220;bff&#8221; (bottom field first), &#8220;progressive&#8221;, or &#8220;undetermined&#8221;</p>
<dt><code>single.tff</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames detected as top field first using single-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>multiple.tff</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames detected as top field first using multiple-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>single.bff</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames detected as bottom field first using single-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>multiple.current_frame</code></p>
<dd> Detected type of current frame using multiple-frame detection. One of: &#8220;tff&#8221; (top field first), &#8220;bff&#8221; (bottom field first), &#8220;progressive&#8221;, or &#8220;undetermined&#8221;</p>
<dt><code>multiple.bff</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames detected as bottom field first using multiple-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>single.progressive</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames detected as progressive using single-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>multiple.progressive</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames detected as progressive using multiple-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>single.undetermined</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames that could not be classified using single-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>multiple.undetermined</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames that could not be classified using multiple-frame detection.</p>
<dt><code>repeated.current_frame</code></p>
<dd> Which field in the current frame is repeated from the last. One of &#8220;neither&#8221;, &#8220;top&#8221;, or &#8220;bottom&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>repeated.neither</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames with no repeated field.</p>
<dt><code>repeated.top</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames with the top field repeated from the previous frame&#8217;s top field.</p>
<dt><code>repeated.bottom</code></p>
<dd> Cumulative number of frames with the bottom field repeated from the previous frame&#8217;s bottom field.</dl>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>intl_thres</code></p>
<dd> Set interlacing threshold.</p>
<dt><code>prog_thres</code></p>
<dd> Set progressive threshold.</p>
<dt><code>rep_thres</code></p>
<dd> Threshold for repeated field detection.</p>
<dt><code>half_life</code></p>
<dd> Number of frames after which a given frame&#8217;s contribution to the statistics is halved (i.e., it contributes only 0.5 to its classification). The default of 0 means that all frames seen are given full weight of 1.0 forever.</p>
<dt><code>analyze_interlaced_flag</code></p>
<dd> When this is not 0 then idet will use the specified number of frames to determine if the interlaced flag is accurate, it will not count undetermined frames. If the flag is found to be accurate it will be used without any further computations, if it is found to be inaccurate it will be cleared without any further computations. This allows inserting the idet filter as a low computational method to clean up the interlaced flag</dl>
<h3>il</h3>
<p> Deinterleave or interleave fields. </p>
<p> This filter allows one to process interlaced images fields without deinterlacing them. Deinterleaving splits the input frame into 2 fields (so called half pictures). Odd lines are moved to the top half of the output image, even lines to the bottom half. You can process (filter) them independently and then re-interleave them. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>luma_mode, l</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>chroma_mode, c</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>alpha_mode, a</code></p>
<dd> Available values for <i>luma_mode</i>, <i>chroma_mode</i> and <i>alpha_mode</i> are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> Do nothing.</p>
<dt><code>deinterleave, d</code></p>
<dd> Deinterleave fields, placing one above the other.</p>
<dt><code>interleave, i</code></p>
<dd> Interleave fields. Reverse the effect of deinterleaving.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;none&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>luma_swap, ls</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>chroma_swap, cs</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>alpha_swap, as</code></p>
<dd> Swap luma/chroma/alpha fields. Exchange even &amp; odd lines. Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>inflate</h3>
<p> Apply inflate effect to the video. </p>
<p> This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3&#215;3) average by taking into account only values higher than the pixel. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>threshold3</code></p>
<dd> Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535. If 0, plane will remain unchanged.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>interlace</h3>
<p> Simple interlacing filter from progressive contents. This interleaves upper (or lower) lines from odd frames with lower (or upper) lines from even frames, halving the frame rate and preserving image height. </p>
<p>Original Original New Frame<br />
        Frame &#8216;j&#8217; Frame &#8216;j+1&#8242;(tff) == ==<br />
    == == == == == == == ==<br />
    = == == == == == == == == ==<br />
          Line 0 &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &gt; Frame &#8216;j&#8217; Line 0 Line 1 Line 1 &#8212; &#8212; &gt; Frame &#8216;j+1&#8217; Line 1 Line 2 &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212;&gt;Frame &#8216;j&#8217; Line 2 Line 3 Line 3 &#8212; &#8212; &gt; Frame &#8216;j+1&#8217; Line 3 &#8230;&#8230;&#8230; New Frame + 1 will be generated by Frame &#8216;j+2&#8217; and<br />
      Frame &#8216;j+3&#8217; and so on</p>
<p> It accepts the following optional parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>scan</code></p>
<dd> This determines whether the interlaced frame is taken from the even (tff &#8211; default) or odd (bff) lines of the progressive frame.</p>
<dt><code>lowpass</code></p>
<dd> Vertical lowpass filter to avoid twitter interlacing and reduce moire patterns.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>0, off</code></p>
<dd> Disable vertical lowpass filter</p>
<dt><code>1, linear</code></p>
<dd> Enable linear filter (default)</p>
<dt><code>2, complex</code></p>
<dd> Enable complex filter. This will slightly less reduce twitter and moire but better retain detail and subjective sharpness impression.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<h3>kerndeint</h3>
<p> Deinterlace input video by applying Donald Graft&#8217;s adaptive kernel deinterling. Work on interlaced parts of a video to produce progressive frames. </p>
<p> The description of the accepted parameters follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>thresh</code></p>
<dd> Set the threshold which affects the filter&#8217;s tolerance when determining if a pixel line must be processed. It must be an integer in the range [0,255] and defaults to 10. A value of 0 will result in applying the process on every pixels.</p>
<dt><code>map</code></p>
<dd> Paint pixels exceeding the threshold value to white if set to 1. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>order</code></p>
<dd> Set the fields order. Swap fields if set to 1, leave fields alone if 0. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>sharp</code></p>
<dd> Enable additional sharpening if set to 1. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>twoway</code></p>
<dd> Enable twoway sharpening if set to 1. Default is 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply default values: </p>
<pre>
        kerndeint=thresh=10:map=0:order=0:sharp=0:twoway=0
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Enable additional sharpening: </p>
<pre>
        kerndeint=sharp=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Paint processed pixels in white: </p>
<pre>
        kerndeint=map=1
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>kirsch</h3>
<p> Apply kirsch operator to input video stream. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be multiplied with filtered result.</p>
<dt><code>delta</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be added to filtered result.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>lagfun</h3>
<p> Slowly update darker pixels. </p>
<p> This filter makes short flashes of light appear longer. This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>decay</code></p>
<dd> Set factor for decaying. Default is .95. Allowed range is from 0 to 1.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to filter. Default is all. Allowed range is from 0 to 15.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>lenscorrection</h3>
<p> Correct radial lens distortion </p>
<p> This filter can be used to correct for radial distortion as can result from the use of wide angle lenses, and thereby re-rectify the image. To find the right parameters one can use tools available for example as part of opencv or simply trial-and-error. To use opencv use the calibration sample (under samples/cpp) from the opencv sources and extract the k1 and k2 coefficients from the resulting matrix. </p>
<p> Note that effectively the same filter is available in the open-source tools Krita and Digikam from the <font SIZE="-1">KDE</font> project. </p>
<p> In contrast to the <code>vignette</code> filter, which can also be used to compensate lens errors, this filter corrects the distortion of the image, whereas <code>vignette</code> corrects the brightness distribution, so you may want to use both filters together in certain cases, though you will have to take care of ordering, i.e. whether vignetting should be applied before or after lens correction. </p>
<p> <i>Options</i> </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>cx</code></p>
<dd> Relative x-coordinate of the focal point of the image, and thereby the center of the distortion. This value has a range [0,1] and is expressed as fractions of the image width. Default is 0.5.</p>
<dt><code>cy</code></p>
<dd> Relative y-coordinate of the focal point of the image, and thereby the center of the distortion. This value has a range [0,1] and is expressed as fractions of the image height. Default is 0.5.</p>
<dt><code>k1</code></p>
<dd> Coefficient of the quadratic correction term. This value has a range [-1,1]. 0 means no correction. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>k2</code></p>
<dd> Coefficient of the double quadratic correction term. This value has a range [-1,1]. 0 means no correction. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>i</code></p>
<dd> Set interpolation type. Can be <tt>&quot;nearest&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;bilinear&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;nearest&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>fc</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the unmapped pixels. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default color is <tt>&quot;black@0&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> The formula that generates the correction is: </p>
<p> <i>r_src</i> = <i>r_tgt</i> * (1 + <i>k1</i> * (<i>r_tgt</i> / <i>r_0</i>)^2 + <i>k2</i> * (<i>r_tgt</i> / <i>r_0</i>)^4) </p>
<p> where <i>r_0</i> is halve of the image diagonal and <i>r_src</i> and <i>r_tgt</i> are the distances from the focal point in the source and target images, respectively. </p>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>lensfun</h3>
<p> Apply lens correction via the lensfun library (&lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://lensfun.sourceforge.net/">http://lensfun.sourceforge.net/</a></code>&gt;). </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;lensfun&quot;</tt> filter requires the camera make, camera model, and lens model to apply the lens correction. The filter will load the lensfun database and query it to find the corresponding camera and lens entries in the database. As long as these entries can be found with the given options, the filter can perform corrections on frames. Note that incomplete strings will result in the filter choosing the best match with the given options, and the filter will output the chosen camera and lens models (logged with level &#8220;info&#8221;). You must provide the make, camera model, and lens model as they are required. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>make</code></p>
<dd> The make of the camera (for example, &#8220;Canon&#8221;). This option is required.</p>
<dt><code>model</code></p>
<dd> The model of the camera (for example, &#8220;Canon <font SIZE="-1">EOS 100D&#8221;</font>). This option is required.</p>
<dt><code>lens_model</code></p>
<dd> The model of the lens (for example, &#8220;Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 <font SIZE="-1">IS STM&#8221;</font>). This option is required.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> The type of correction to apply. The following values are valid options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>vignetting</code></p>
<dd> Enables fixing lens vignetting.</p>
<dt><code>geometry</code></p>
<dd> Enables fixing lens geometry. This is the default.</p>
<dt><code>subpixel</code></p>
<dd> Enables fixing chromatic aberrations.</p>
<dt><code>vig_geo</code></p>
<dd> Enables fixing lens vignetting and lens geometry.</p>
<dt><code>vig_subpixel</code></p>
<dd> Enables fixing lens vignetting and chromatic aberrations.</p>
<dt><code>distortion</code></p>
<dd> Enables fixing both lens geometry and chromatic aberrations.</p>
<dt><code>all</code></p>
<dd> Enables all possible corrections.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>focal_length</code></p>
<dd> The focal length of the image/video (zoom; expected constant for video). For example, a 18&#8211;55mm lens has focal length range of [18&#8211;55], so a value in that range should be chosen when using that lens. Default 18.</p>
<dt><code>aperture</code></p>
<dd> The aperture of the image/video (expected constant for video). Note that aperture is only used for vignetting correction. Default 3.5.</p>
<dt><code>focus_distance</code></p>
<dd> The focus distance of the image/video (expected constant for video). Note that focus distance is only used for vignetting and only slightly affects the vignetting correction process. If unknown, leave it at the default value (which is 1000).</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> The scale factor which is applied after transformation. After correction the video is no longer necessarily rectangular. This parameter controls how much of the resulting image is visible. The value 0 means that a value will be chosen automatically such that there is little or no unmapped area in the output image. 1.0 means that no additional scaling is done. Lower values may result in more of the corrected image being visible, while higher values may avoid unmapped areas in the output.</p>
<dt><code>target_geometry</code></p>
<dd> The target geometry of the output image/video. The following values are valid options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>rectilinear (default)</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fisheye</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>panoramic</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>equirectangular</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fisheye_orthographic</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fisheye_stereographic</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fisheye_equisolid</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fisheye_thoby</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>reverse</code></p>
<dd> Apply the reverse of image correction (instead of correcting distortion, apply it).</p>
<dt><code>interpolation</code></p>
<dd> The type of interpolation used when correcting distortion. The following values are valid options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>nearest</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>linear (default)</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>lanczos</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply lens correction with make &#8220;Canon&#8221;, camera model &#8220;Canon <font SIZE="-1">EOS 100D&#8221;,</font> and lens model &#8220;Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 <font SIZE="-1">IS STM&#8221;</font> with focal length of &#8220;18&#8221; and aperture of &#8220;8.0&#8221;. </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.mov -vf lensfun=make=Canon:model=&quot;Canon EOS 100D&quot;:lens_model=&quot;Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 IS STM&quot;:focal_length=18:aperture=8 -c:v h264 -b:v 8000k output.mov
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply the same as before, but only for the first 5 seconds of video. </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.mov -vf lensfun=make=Canon:model=&quot;Canon EOS 100D&quot;:lens_model=&quot;Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 IS STM&quot;:focal_length=18:aperture=8:enable='lte(t\,5)' -c:v h264 -b:v 8000k output.mov
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>libvmaf</h3>
<p> Obtain the <font SIZE="-1">VMAF</font> (Video Multi-Method Assessment Fusion) score between two input videos. </p>
<p> The obtained <font SIZE="-1">VMAF</font> score is printed through the logging system. </p>
<p> It requires Netflix&#8217;s vmaf library (libvmaf) as a pre-requisite. After installing the library it can be enabled using: <tt>&quot;./configure --enable-libvmaf&quot;</tt>. If no model path is specified it uses the default model: <tt>&quot;vmaf_v0.6.1.pkl&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> The filter has following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>model_path</code></p>
<dd> Set the model path which is to be used for <font SIZE="-1">SVM.</font> Default value: <tt>&quot;/usr/local/share/model/vmaf_v0.6.1.pkl&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>log_path</code></p>
<dd> Set the file path to be used to store logs.</p>
<dt><code>log_fmt</code></p>
<dd> Set the format of the log file (csv, json or xml).</p>
<dt><code>enable_transform</code></p>
<dd> This option can enable/disable the <tt>&quot;score_transform&quot;</tt> applied to the final predicted <font SIZE="-1">VMAF</font> score, if you have specified score_transform option in the input parameter file passed to <tt>&quot;run_vmaf_training.py&quot;</tt> Default value: <tt>&quot;false&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>phone_model</code></p>
<dd> Invokes the phone model which will generate <font SIZE="-1">VMAF</font> scores higher than in the regular model, which is more suitable for laptop, <font SIZE="-1">TV,</font> etc. viewing conditions. Default value: <tt>&quot;false&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>psnr</code></p>
<dd> Enables computing psnr along with vmaf. Default value: <tt>&quot;false&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>ssim</code></p>
<dd> Enables computing ssim along with vmaf. Default value: <tt>&quot;false&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>ms_ssim</code></p>
<dd> Enables computing ms_ssim along with vmaf. Default value: <tt>&quot;false&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>pool</code></p>
<dd> Set the pool method to be used for computing vmaf. Options are <tt>&quot;min&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;harmonic_mean&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;mean&quot;</tt> (default).</p>
<dt><code>n_threads</code></p>
<dd> Set number of threads to be used when computing vmaf. Default value: <tt>0</tt>, which makes use of all available logical processors.</p>
<dt><code>n_subsample</code></p>
<dd> Set interval for frame subsampling used when computing vmaf. Default value: <tt>1</tt></p>
<dt><code>enable_conf_interval</code></p>
<dd> Enables confidence interval. Default value: <tt>&quot;false&quot;</tt></dl>
<p> This filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> On the below examples the input file <i>main.mpg</i> being processed is compared with the reference file <i>ref.mpg</i>. </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi libvmaf -f null -
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Example with options: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi libvmaf=&quot;psnr=1:log_fmt=json&quot; -f null -
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Example with options and different containers: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i main.mpg &#8211; i ref.mkv &#8211; lavfi &#8220;[0:v]settb=AVTB,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS[main];[1:v]settb=AVTB,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS[ref];[main][ref]libvmaf=psnr=1:log_fmt=json&#8221; &#8211; f null &#8211;
</dl>
<h3>limiter</h3>
<p> Limits the pixel components values to the specified range [min, max]. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>min</code></p>
<dd> Lower bound. Defaults to the lowest allowed value for the input.</p>
<dt><code>max</code></p>
<dd> Upper bound. Defaults to the highest allowed value for the input.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Specify which planes will be processed. Defaults to all available.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>loop</h3>
<p> Loop video frames. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>loop</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of loops. Setting this value to -1 will result in infinite loops. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>size</code></p>
<dd> Set maximal size in number of frames. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>start</code></p>
<dd> Set first frame of loop. Default is 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Loop single first frame infinitely: </p>
<pre>
        loop=loop=-1:size=1:start=0
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Loop single first frame 10 times: </p>
<pre>
        loop=loop=10:size=1:start=0
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Loop 10 first frames 5 times: </p>
<pre>
        loop=loop=5:size=10:start=0
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>lut1d</h3>
<p> Apply a 1D <font SIZE="-1">LUT</font> to an input video. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>file</code></p>
<dd> Set the 1D <font SIZE="-1">LUT</font> file name. </p>
<p> Currently supported formats:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>cube</code></p>
<dd> Iridas</p>
<dt><code>csp</code></p>
<dd> cineSpace</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>interp</code></p>
<dd> Select interpolation mode. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>nearest</code></p>
<dd> Use values from the nearest defined point.</p>
<dt><code>linear</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using the linear interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>cosine</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using the cosine interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>cubic</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using the cubic interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>spline</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using the spline interpolation.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>lut3d</h3>
<p> Apply a 3D <font SIZE="-1">LUT</font> to an input video. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>file</code></p>
<dd> Set the 3D <font SIZE="-1">LUT</font> file name. </p>
<p> Currently supported formats:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>3dl</code></p>
<dd> AfterEffects</p>
<dt><code>cube</code></p>
<dd> Iridas</p>
<dt><code>dat</code></p>
<dd> DaVinci</p>
<dt><code>m3d</code></p>
<dd> Pandora</p>
<dt><code>csp</code></p>
<dd> cineSpace</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>interp</code></p>
<dd> Select interpolation mode. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>nearest</code></p>
<dd> Use values from the nearest defined point.</p>
<dt><code>trilinear</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using the 8 points defining a cube.</p>
<dt><code>tetrahedral</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using a tetrahedron.</p>
<dt><code>pyramid</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using a pyramid.</p>
<dt><code>prism</code></p>
<dd> Interpolate values using a prism.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the <tt>&quot;interp&quot;</tt> option as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>lumakey</h3>
<p> Turn certain luma values into transparency. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma which will be used as base for transparency. Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>tolerance</code></p>
<dd> Set the range of luma values to be keyed out. Default value is <tt>0.01</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>softness</code></p>
<dd> Set the range of softness. Default value is <tt>0</tt>. Use this to control gradual transition from zero to full transparency.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</p>
<h3>lut, lutrgb, lutyuv</h3>
<p> Compute a look-up table for binding each pixel component input value to an output value, and apply it to the input video. </p>
<p> <i>lutyuv</i> applies a lookup table to a <font SIZE="-1">YUV</font> input video, <i>lutrgb</i> to an <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> input video. </p>
<p> These filters accept the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>c0</code></p>
<dd> set first pixel component expression</p>
<dt><code>c1</code></p>
<dd> set second pixel component expression</p>
<dt><code>c2</code></p>
<dd> set third pixel component expression</p>
<dt><code>c3</code></p>
<dd> set fourth pixel component expression, corresponds to the alpha component</p>
<dt><code>r</code></p>
<dd> set red component expression</p>
<dt><code>g</code></p>
<dd> set green component expression</p>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd> set blue component expression</p>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> alpha component expression</p>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> set Y/luminance component expression</p>
<dt><code>u</code></p>
<dd> set U/Cb component expression</p>
<dt><code>v</code></p>
<dd> set V/Cr component expression</dl>
<p> Each of them specifies the expression to use for computing the lookup table for the corresponding pixel component values. </p>
<p> The exact component associated to each of the <i>c*</i> options depends on the format in input. </p>
<p> The <i>lut</i> filter requires either <font SIZE="-1">YUV</font> or <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> pixel formats in input, <i>lutrgb</i> requires <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> pixel formats in input, and <i>lutyuv</i> requires <font SIZE="-1">YUV.</font> </p>
<p> The expressions can contain the following constants and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> The input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>val</code></p>
<dd> The input value for the pixel component.</p>
<dt><code>clipval</code></p>
<dd> The input value, clipped to the <i>minval</i>&#8211;<i>maxval</i> range.</p>
<dt><code>maxval</code></p>
<dd> The maximum value for the pixel component.</p>
<dt><code>minval</code></p>
<dd> The minimum value for the pixel component.</p>
<dt><code>negval</code></p>
<dd> The negated value for the pixel component value, clipped to the <i>minval</i>&#8211;<i>maxval</i> range; it corresponds to the expression &#8220;maxval-clipval+minval&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>clip(val)</code></p>
<dd> The computed value in <i>val</i>, clipped to the <i>minval</i>&#8211;<i>maxval</i> range.</p>
<dt><code>gammaval(gamma)</code></p>
<dd> The computed gamma correction value of the pixel component value, clipped to the <i>minval</i>&#8211;<i>maxval</i> range. It corresponds to the expression &quot;pow((clipval-minval)/(maxval-minval)\,<i>gamma</i>)*(maxval-minval)+minval&quot;</dl>
<p> All expressions default to &#8220;val&#8221;. </p>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Negate input video: </p>
<pre>
        lutrgb=&quot;r=maxval+minval-val:g=maxval+minval-val:b=maxval+minval-val&quot;
        lutyuv=&quot;y=maxval+minval-val:u=maxval+minval-val:v=maxval+minval-val&quot;
</pre>
<p> The above is the same as: </p>
<pre>
        lutrgb=&quot;r=negval:g=negval:b=negval&quot;
        lutyuv=&quot;y=negval:u=negval:v=negval&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Negate luminance: </p>
<pre>
        lutyuv=y=negval
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Remove chroma components, turning the video into a graytone image: </p>
<pre>
        lutyuv=&quot;u=128:v=128&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply a luma burning effect: </p>
<pre>
        lutyuv=&quot;y=2*val&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Remove green and blue components: </p>
<pre>
        lutrgb=&quot;g=0:b=0&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set a constant alpha channel value on input: </p>
<pre>
