        format=rgba,lutrgb=a=&quot;maxval-minval/2&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Correct luminance gamma by a factor of 0.5: </p>
<pre>
        lutyuv=y=gammaval(0.5)
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Discard least significant bits of luma: </p>
<pre>
        lutyuv=y='bitand(val, 128+64+32)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Technicolor like effect: </p>
<pre>
        lutyuv=u='(val-maxval/2)*2+maxval/2':v='(val-maxval/2)*2+maxval/2'
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>lut2, tlut2</h3>
<p> The <tt>&quot;lut2&quot;</tt> filter takes two input streams and outputs one stream. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;tlut2&quot;</tt> (time lut2) filter takes two consecutive frames from one single stream. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>c0</code></p>
<dd> set first pixel component expression</p>
<dt><code>c1</code></p>
<dd> set second pixel component expression</p>
<dt><code>c2</code></p>
<dd> set third pixel component expression</p>
<dt><code>c3</code></p>
<dd> set fourth pixel component expression, corresponds to the alpha component</p>
<dt><code>d</code></p>
<dd> set output bit depth, only available for <tt>&quot;lut2&quot;</tt> filter. By default is 0, which means bit depth is automatically picked from first input format.</dl>
<p> The <tt>&quot;lut2&quot;</tt> filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options. </p>
<p> Each of them specifies the expression to use for computing the lookup table for the corresponding pixel component values. </p>
<p> The exact component associated to each of the <i>c*</i> options depends on the format in inputs. </p>
<p> The expressions can contain the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> The input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd> The first input value for the pixel component.</p>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The second input value for the pixel component.</p>
<dt><code>bdx</code></p>
<dd> The first input video bit depth.</p>
<dt><code>bdy</code></p>
<dd> The second input video bit depth.</dl>
<p> All expressions default to &#8220;x&#8221;. </p>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code> except option <tt>&quot;d&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Highlight differences between two <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> video streams: </p>
<pre>
        lut2='ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Highlight differences between two <font SIZE="-1">YUV</font> video streams: </p>
<pre>
        lut2='ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,pow(2,bdx-1),pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,pow(2,bdx-1),pow(2,bdx)-1)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Show max difference between two video streams: </p>
<p>lut2 = &#8216;if(lt(x,y),0,if(gt(x,y),pow(2,bdx)-1,pow(2,bdx-1))):if(lt(x,y),0,if(gt(x,y),pow(2,bdx)-1,pow(2,bdx-1))):if(lt(x,y),0,if(gt(x,y),pow(2,bdx)-1,pow(2,bdx-1)))&#8217;
</dl>
<h3>maskedclamp</h3>
<p> Clamp the first input stream with the second input and third input stream. </p>
<p> Returns the value of first stream to be between second input stream &#8211; <tt>&quot;undershoot&quot;</tt> and third input stream + <tt>&quot;overshoot&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>undershoot</code></p>
<dd> Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>overshoot</code></p>
<dd> Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be copied from first stream. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>maskedmax</h3>
<p> Merge the second and third input stream into output stream using absolute differences between second input stream and first input stream and absolute difference between third input stream and first input stream. The picked value will be from second input stream if second absolute difference is greater than first one or from third input stream otherwise. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be copied from first stream. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>maskedmerge</h3>
<p> Merge the first input stream with the second input stream using per pixel weights in the third input stream. </p>
<p> A value of 0 in the third stream pixel component means that pixel component from first stream is returned unchanged, while maximum value (eg. 255 for 8-bit videos) means that pixel component from second stream is returned unchanged. Intermediate values define the amount of merging between both input stream&#8217;s pixel components. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be copied from first stream. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>maskedmin</h3>
<p> Merge the second and third input stream into output stream using absolute differences between second input stream and first input stream and absolute difference between third input stream and first input stream. The picked value will be from second input stream if second absolute difference is less than first one or from third input stream otherwise. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be copied from first stream. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>maskedthreshold</h3>
<p> Pick pixels comparing absolute difference of two video streams with fixed threshold. </p>
<p> If absolute difference between pixel component of first and second video stream is equal or lower than user supplied threshold than pixel component from first video stream is picked, otherwise pixel component from second video stream is picked. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold</code></p>
<dd> Set threshold used when picking pixels from absolute difference from two input video streams.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be copied from second stream. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>maskfun</h3>
<p> Create mask from input video. </p>
<p> For example it is useful to create motion masks after <tt>&quot;tblend&quot;</tt> filter. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>low</code></p>
<dd> Set low threshold. Any pixel component lower or exact than this value will be set to 0.</p>
<dt><code>high</code></p>
<dd> Set high threshold. Any pixel component higher than this value will be set to max value allowed for current pixel format.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set planes to filter, by default all available planes are filtered.</p>
<dt><code>fill</code></p>
<dd> Fill all frame pixels with this value.</p>
<dt><code>sum</code></p>
<dd> Set max average pixel value for frame. If sum of all pixel components is higher that this average, output frame will be completely filled with value set by <i>fill</i> option. Typically useful for scene changes when used in combination with <tt>&quot;tblend&quot;</tt> filter.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>mcdeint</h3>
<p> Apply motion-compensation deinterlacing. </p>
<p> It needs one field per frame as input and must thus be used together with yadif=1/3 or equivalent. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set the deinterlacing mode. </p>
<p> It accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>fast</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>medium</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>slow</code></p>
<dd> use iterative motion estimation</p>
<dt><code>extra_slow</code></p>
<dd> like <code>slow</code>, but use multiple reference frames.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>fast</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>parity</code></p>
<dd> Set the picture field parity assumed for the input video. It must be one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>0, tff</code></p>
<dd> assume top field first</p>
<dt><code>1, bff</code></p>
<dd> assume bottom field first</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>bff</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>qp</code></p>
<dd> Set per-block quantization parameter (<font SIZE="-1">QP</font>) used by the internal encoder. </p>
<p> Higher values should result in a smoother motion vector field but less optimal individual vectors. Default value is 1.</dl>
<h3>median</h3>
<p> Pick median pixel from certain rectangle defined by radius. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>radius</code></p>
<dd> Set horizontal radius size. Default value is <tt>1</tt>. Allowed range is integer from 1 to 127.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to process. Default is <tt>15</tt>, which is all available planes.</p>
<dt><code>radiusV</code></p>
<dd> Set vertical radius size. Default value is <tt>0</tt>. Allowed range is integer from 0 to 127. If it is 0, value will be picked from horizontal <tt>&quot;radius&quot;</tt> option.</p>
<dt><code>percentile</code></p>
<dd> Set median percentile. Default value is <tt>0.5</tt>. Default value of <tt>0.5</tt> will pick always median values, while <tt>0</tt> will pick minimum values, and <tt>1</tt> maximum values.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</p>
<h3>mergeplanes</h3>
<p> Merge color channel components from several video streams. </p>
<p> The filter accepts up to 4 input streams, and merge selected input planes to the output video. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mapping</code></p>
<dd> Set input to output plane mapping. Default is <tt>0</tt>. </p>
<p> The mappings is specified as a bitmap. It should be specified as a hexadecimal number in the form 0xAa[Bb[Cc[Dd]]]. &#8216;Aa&#8217; describes the mapping for the first plane of the output stream. &#8216;A&#8217; sets the number of the input stream to use (from 0 to 3), and &#8216;a&#8217; the plane number of the corresponding input to use (from 0 to 3). The rest of the mappings is similar, &#8216;Bb&#8217; describes the mapping for the output stream second plane, &#8216;Cc&#8217; describes the mapping for the output stream third plane and &#8216;Dd&#8217; describes the mapping for the output stream fourth plane.</p>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> Set output pixel format. Default is <tt>&quot;yuva444p&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Merge three gray video streams of same width and height into single video stream: </p>
<p>[a0][a1][a2] mergeplanes = 0x001020 : yuv444p</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Merge 1st yuv444p stream and 2nd gray video stream into yuva444p video stream: </p>
<p>[a0][a1] mergeplanes = 0x00010210 : yuva444p</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Swap Y and A plane in yuva444p stream: </p>
<pre>
        format=yuva444p,mergeplanes=0x03010200:yuva444p
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Swap U and V plane in yuv420p stream: </p>
<pre>
        format=yuv420p,mergeplanes=0x000201:yuv420p
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Cast a rgb24 clip to yuv444p: </p>
<pre>
        format=rgb24,mergeplanes=0x000102:yuv444p
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>mestimate</h3>
<p> Estimate and export motion vectors using block matching algorithms. Motion vectors are stored in frame side data to be used by other filters. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>method</code></p>
<dd> Specify the motion estimation method. Accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>esa</code></p>
<dd> Exhaustive search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>tss</code></p>
<dd> Three step search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>tdls</code></p>
<dd> Two dimensional logarithmic search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>ntss</code></p>
<dd> New three step search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>fss</code></p>
<dd> Four step search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>ds</code></p>
<dd> Diamond search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>hexbs</code></p>
<dd> Hexagon-based search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>epzs</code></p>
<dd> Enhanced predictive zonal search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>umh</code></p>
<dd> Uneven multi-hexagon search algorithm.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>esa</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>mb_size</code></p>
<dd> Macroblock size. Default <tt>16</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>search_param</code></p>
<dd> Search parameter. Default <tt>7</tt>.</dl>
<h3>midequalizer</h3>
<p> Apply Midway Image Equalization effect using two video streams. </p>
<p> Midway Image Equalization adjusts a pair of images to have the same histogram, while maintaining their dynamics as much as possible. It&#8217;s useful for e.g. matching exposures from a pair of stereo cameras. </p>
<p> This filter has two inputs and one output, which must be of same pixel format, but may be of different sizes. The output of filter is first input adjusted with midway histogram of both inputs. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to process. Default is <tt>15</tt>, which is all available planes.</dl>
<h3>minterpolate</h3>
<p> Convert the video to specified frame rate using motion interpolation. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>fps</code></p>
<dd> Specify the output frame rate. This can be rational e.g. <tt>&quot;60000/1001&quot;</tt>. Frames are dropped if <i>fps</i> is lower than source fps. Default <tt>60</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>mi_mode</code></p>
<dd> Motion interpolation mode. Following values are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>dup</code></p>
<dd> Duplicate previous or next frame for interpolating new ones.</p>
<dt><code>blend</code></p>
<dd> Blend source frames. Interpolated frame is mean of previous and next frames.</p>
<dt><code>mci</code></p>
<dd> Motion compensated interpolation. Following options are effective when this mode is selected:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>mc_mode</code></p>
<dd> Motion compensation mode. Following values are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>obmc</code></p>
<dd> Overlapped block motion compensation.</p>
<dt><code>aobmc</code></p>
<dd> Adaptive overlapped block motion compensation. Window weighting coefficients are controlled adaptively according to the reliabilities of the neighboring motion vectors to reduce oversmoothing.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default mode is <code>obmc</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>me_mode</code></p>
<dd> Motion estimation mode. Following values are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>bidir</code></p>
<dd> Bidirectional motion estimation. Motion vectors are estimated for each source frame in both forward and backward directions.</p>
<dt><code>bilat</code></p>
<dd> Bilateral motion estimation. Motion vectors are estimated directly for interpolated frame.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default mode is <code>bilat</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>me</code></p>
<dd> The algorithm to be used for motion estimation. Following values are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>esa</code></p>
<dd> Exhaustive search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>tss</code></p>
<dd> Three step search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>tdls</code></p>
<dd> Two dimensional logarithmic search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>ntss</code></p>
<dd> New three step search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>fss</code></p>
<dd> Four step search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>ds</code></p>
<dd> Diamond search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>hexbs</code></p>
<dd> Hexagon-based search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>epzs</code></p>
<dd> Enhanced predictive zonal search algorithm.</p>
<dt><code>umh</code></p>
<dd> Uneven multi-hexagon search algorithm.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default algorithm is <code>epzs</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>mb_size</code></p>
<dd> Macroblock size. Default <tt>16</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>search_param</code></p>
<dd> Motion estimation search parameter. Default <tt>32</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>vsbmc</code></p>
<dd> Enable variable-size block motion compensation. Motion estimation is applied with smaller block sizes at object boundaries in order to make the them less blur. Default is <tt>0</tt> (disabled).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>scd</code></p>
<dd> Scene change detection method. Scene change leads motion vectors to be in random direction. Scene change detection replace interpolated frames by duplicate ones. May not be needed for other modes. Following values are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> Disable scene change detection.</p>
<dt><code>fdiff</code></p>
<dd> Frame difference. Corresponding pixel values are compared and if it satisfies <i>scd_threshold</i> scene change is detected.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default method is <code>fdiff</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>scd_threshold</code></p>
<dd> Scene change detection threshold. Default is <tt>10.</tt>.</dl>
<h3>mix</h3>
<p> Mix several video input streams into one video stream. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted options follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>nb_inputs</code></p>
<dd> The number of inputs. If unspecified, it defaults to 2.</p>
<dt><code>weights</code></p>
<dd> Specify weight of each input video stream as sequence. Each weight is separated by space. If number of weights is smaller than number of <i>frames</i> last specified weight will be used for all remaining unset weights.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Specify scale, if it is set it will be multiplied with sum of each weight multiplied with pixel values to give final destination pixel value. By default <i>scale</i> is auto scaled to sum of weights.</p>
<dt><code>duration</code></p>
<dd> Specify how end of stream is determined.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>longest</code></p>
<dd> The duration of the longest input. (default)</p>
<dt><code>shortest</code></p>
<dd> The duration of the shortest input.</p>
<dt><code>first</code></p>
<dd> The duration of the first input.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>weights</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Syntax is same as option with same name.</dl>
<h3>monochrome</h3>
<p> Convert video to gray using custom color filter. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted options follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>cb</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma blue spot. Allowed range is from -1 to 1. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>cr</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma red spot. Allowed range is from -1 to 1. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>size</code></p>
<dd> Set the color filter size. Allowed range is from .1 to 10. Default value is 1.</p>
<dt><code>high</code></p>
<dd> Set the highlights strength. Allowed range is from 0 to 1. Default value is 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>mpdecimate</h3>
<p> Drop frames that do not differ greatly from the previous frame in order to reduce frame rate. </p>
<p> The main use of this filter is for very-low-bitrate encoding (e.g. streaming over dialup modem), but it could in theory be used for fixing movies that were inverse-telecined incorrectly. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted options follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>max</code></p>
<dd> Set the maximum number of consecutive frames which can be dropped (if positive), or the minimum interval between dropped frames (if negative). If the value is 0, the frame is dropped disregarding the number of previous sequentially dropped frames. </p>
<p> Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>hi</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>lo</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>frac</code></p>
<dd> Set the dropping threshold values. </p>
<p> Values for <code>hi</code> and <code>lo</code> are for 8&#215;8 pixel blocks and represent actual pixel value differences, so a threshold of 64 corresponds to 1 unit of difference for each pixel, or the same spread out differently over the block. </p>
<p> A frame is a candidate for dropping if no 8&#215;8 blocks differ by more than a threshold of <code>hi</code>, and if no more than <code>frac</code> blocks (1 meaning the whole image) differ by more than a threshold of <code>lo</code>. </p>
<p> Default value for <code>hi</code> is 64*12, default value for <code>lo</code> is 64*5, and default value for <code>frac</code> is 0.33.</dl>
<h3>msad</h3>
<p> Obtain the <font SIZE="-1">MSAD</font> (Mean Sum of Absolute Differences) between two input videos. </p>
<p> This filter takes two input videos. </p>
<p> Both input videos must have the same resolution and pixel format for this filter to work correctly. Also it assumes that both inputs have the same number of frames, which are compared one by one. </p>
<p> The obtained per component, average, min and max <font SIZE="-1">MSAD</font> is printed through the logging system. </p>
<p> The filter stores the calculated <font SIZE="-1">MSAD</font> of each frame in frame metadata. </p>
<p> In the below example the input file <i>main.mpg</i> being processed is compared with the reference file <i>ref.mpg</i>. </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi msad -f null -
</pre>
<h3>negate</h3>
<p> Negate (invert) the input video. </p>
<p> It accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>negate_alpha</code></p>
<dd> With value 1, it negates the alpha component, if present. Default value is 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options.</p>
<h3>nlmeans</h3>
<p> Denoise frames using Non-Local Means algorithm. </p>
<p> Each pixel is adjusted by looking for other pixels with similar contexts. This context similarity is defined by comparing their surrounding patches of size <code>p</code>x<code>p</code>. Patches are searched in an area of <code>r</code>x<code>r</code> around the pixel. </p>
<p> Note that the research area defines centers for patches, which means some patches will be made of pixels outside that research area. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>s</code></p>
<dd> Set denoising strength. Default is 1.0. Must be in range [1.0, 30.0].</p>
<dt><code>p</code></p>
<dd> Set patch size. Default is 7. Must be odd number in range [0, 99].</p>
<dt><code>pc</code></p>
<dd> Same as <code>p</code> but for chroma planes. </p>
<p> The default value is <i>0</i> and means automatic.</p>
<dt><code>r</code></p>
<dd> Set research size. Default is 15. Must be odd number in range [0, 99].</p>
<dt><code>rc</code></p>
<dd> Same as <code>r</code> but for chroma planes. </p>
<p> The default value is <i>0</i> and means automatic.</dl>
<h3>nnedi</h3>
<p> Deinterlace video using neural network edge directed interpolation. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>weights</code></p>
<dd> Mandatory option, without binary file filter can not work. Currently file can be found here: <a rel="nofollow" href="https://github.com/dubhater/vapoursynth-nnedi3/blob/master/src/nnedi3_weights.bin">https://github.com/dubhater/vapoursynth-nnedi3/blob/master/src/nnedi3_weights.bin</a></p>
<dt><code>deint</code></p>
<dd> Set which frames to deinterlace, by default it is <tt>&quot;all&quot;</tt>. Can be <tt>&quot;all&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;interlaced&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>field</code></p>
<dd> Set mode of operation. </p>
<p> Can be one of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>af</code></p>
<dd> Use frame flags, both fields.</p>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> Use frame flags, single field.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> Use top field only.</p>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd> Use bottom field only.</p>
<dt><code>tf</code></p>
<dd> Use both fields, top first.</p>
<dt><code>bf</code></p>
<dd> Use both fields, bottom first.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to process, by default filter process all frames.</p>
<dt><code>nsize</code></p>
<dd> Set size of local neighborhood around each pixel, used by the predictor neural network. </p>
<p> Can be one of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>s8x6</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>s16x6</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>s32x6</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>s48x6</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>s8x4</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>s16x4</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>s32x4</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>nns</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of neurons in predictor neural network. Can be one of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>n16</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>n32</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>n64</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>n128</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>n256</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>qual</code></p>
<dd> Controls the number of different neural network predictions that are blended together to compute the final output value. Can be <tt>&quot;fast&quot;</tt>, default or <tt>&quot;slow&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>etype</code></p>
<dd> Set which set of weights to use in the predictor. Can be one of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>a, abs</code></p>
<dd> weights trained to minimize absolute error</p>
<dt><code>s, mse</code></p>
<dd> weights trained to minimize squared error</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>pscrn</code></p>
<dd> Controls whether or not the prescreener neural network is used to decide which pixels should be processed by the predictor neural network and which can be handled by simple cubic interpolation. The prescreener is trained to know whether cubic interpolation will be sufficient for a pixel or whether it should be predicted by the predictor nn. The computational complexity of the prescreener nn is much less than that of the predictor nn. Since most pixels can be handled by cubic interpolation, using the prescreener generally results in much faster processing. The prescreener is pretty accurate, so the difference between using it and not using it is almost always unnoticeable. </p>
<p> Can be one of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>original</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>new</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>new2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>new3</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;new&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options, excluding <i>weights</i> option.</p>
<h3>noformat</h3>
<p> Force libavfilter not to use any of the specified pixel formats for the input to the next filter. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>pix_fmts</code></p>
<dd> A &#8216;|&#8217;-separated list of pixel format names, such as pix_fmts=yuv420p|monow|rgb24&quot;.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Force libavfilter to use a format different from <i>yuv420p</i> for the input to the vflip filter: </p>
<pre>
        noformat=pix_fmts=yuv420p,vflip
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Convert the input video to any of the formats not contained in the list: </p>
<pre>
        noformat=yuv420p|yuv444p|yuv410p
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>noise</h3>
<p> Add noise on video input frame. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>all_seed</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c0_seed</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c1_seed</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c2_seed</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c3_seed</code></p>
<dd> Set noise seed for specific pixel component or all pixel components in case of <i>all_seed</i>. Default value is <tt>123457</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>all_strength, alls</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c0_strength, c0s</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c1_strength, c1s</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c2_strength, c2s</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c3_strength, c3s</code></p>
<dd> Set noise strength for specific pixel component or all pixel components in case <i>all_strength</i>. Default value is <tt>0</tt>. Allowed range is [0, 100].</p>
<dt><code>all_flags, allf</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c0_flags, c0f</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c1_flags, c1f</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c2_flags, c2f</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c3_flags, c3f</code></p>
<dd> Set pixel component flags or set flags for all components if <i>all_flags</i>. Available values for component flags are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> averaged temporal noise (smoother)</p>
<dt><code>p</code></p>
<dd> mix random noise with a (semi)regular pattern</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> temporal noise (noise pattern changes between frames)</p>
<dt><code>u</code></p>
<dd> uniform noise (gaussian otherwise)</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i> </p>
<p> Add temporal and uniform noise to input video: </p>
<pre>
        noise=alls=20:allf=t+u
</pre>
<h3>normalize</h3>
<p> Normalize <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> video (aka histogram stretching, contrast stretching). See: <a rel="nofollow" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normalization_(image_processing)">https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normalization_(image_processing)</a> </p>
<p> For each channel of each frame, the filter computes the input range and maps it linearly to the user-specified output range. The output range defaults to the full dynamic range from pure black to pure white. </p>
<p> Temporal smoothing can be used on the input range to reduce flickering (rapid changes in brightness) caused when small dark or bright objects enter or leave the scene. This is similar to the auto-exposure (automatic gain control) on a video camera, and, like a video camera, it may cause a period of over- or under-exposure of the video. </p>
<p> The R,G,B channels can be normalized independently, which may cause some color shifting, or linked together as a single channel, which prevents color shifting. Linked normalization preserves hue. Independent normalization does not, so it can be used to remove some color casts. Independent and linked normalization can be combined in any ratio. </p>
<p> The normalize filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>blackpt</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>whitept</code></p>
<dd> Colors which define the output range. The minimum input value is mapped to the <i>blackpt</i>. The maximum input value is mapped to the <i>whitept</i>. The defaults are black and white respectively. Specifying white for <i>blackpt</i> and black for <i>whitept</i> will give color-inverted, normalized video. Shades of grey can be used to reduce the dynamic range (contrast). Specifying saturated colors here can create some interesting effects.</p>
<dt><code>smoothing</code></p>
<dd> The number of previous frames to use for temporal smoothing. The input range of each channel is smoothed using a rolling average over the current frame and the <i>smoothing</i> previous frames. The default is 0 (no temporal smoothing).</p>
<dt><code>independence</code></p>
<dd> Controls the ratio of independent (color shifting) channel normalization to linked (color preserving) normalization. 0.0 is fully linked, 1.0 is fully independent. Defaults to 1.0 (fully independent).</p>
<dt><code>strength</code></p>
<dd> Overall strength of the filter. 1.0 is full strength. 0.0 is a rather expensive no-op. Defaults to 1.0 (full strength).</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options, excluding <i>smoothing</i> option. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i> </p>
<p> Stretch video contrast to use the full dynamic range, with no temporal smoothing; may flicker depending on the source content: </p>
<pre>
        normalize=blackpt=black:whitept=white:smoothing=0
</pre>
<p> As above, but with 50 frames of temporal smoothing; flicker should be reduced, depending on the source content: </p>
<pre>
        normalize=blackpt=black:whitept=white:smoothing=50
</pre>
<p> As above, but with hue-preserving linked channel normalization: </p>
<pre>
        normalize=blackpt=black:whitept=white:smoothing=50:independence=0
</pre>
<p> As above, but with half strength: </p>
<pre>
        normalize=blackpt=black:whitept=white:smoothing=50:independence=0:strength=0.5
</pre>
<p> Map the darkest input color to red, the brightest input color to cyan: </p>
<pre>
        normalize=blackpt=red:whitept=cyan
</pre>
<h3>null</h3>
<p> Pass the video source unchanged to the output.</p>
<h3>ocr</h3>
<p> Optical Character Recognition </p>
<p> This filter uses Tesseract for optical character recognition. To enable compilation of this filter, you need to configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libtesseract&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>datapath</code></p>
<dd> Set datapath to tesseract data. Default is to use whatever was set at installation.</p>
<dt><code>language</code></p>
<dd> Set language, default is &#8220;eng&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>whitelist</code></p>
<dd> Set character whitelist.</p>
<dt><code>blacklist</code></p>
<dd> Set character blacklist.</dl>
<p> The filter exports recognized text as the frame metadata <tt>&quot;lavfi.ocr.text&quot;</tt>. The filter exports confidence of recognized words as the frame metadata <tt>&quot;lavfi.ocr.confidence&quot;</tt>.</p>
<h3>ocv</h3>
<p> Apply a video transform using libopencv. </p>
<p> To enable this filter, install the libopencv library and headers and configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libopencv&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filter_name</code></p>
<dd> The name of the libopencv filter to apply.</p>
<dt><code>filter_params</code></p>
<dd> The parameters to pass to the libopencv filter. If not specified, the default values are assumed.</dl>
<p> Refer to the official libopencv documentation for more precise information: &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://docs.opencv.org/master/modules/imgproc/doc/filtering.html">http://docs.opencv.org/master/modules/imgproc/doc/filtering.html</a></code>&gt; </p>
<p> Several libopencv filters are supported; see the following subsections. </p>
<p> <i>dilate</i> </p>
<p> Dilate an image by using a specific structuring element. It corresponds to the libopencv function <tt>&quot;cvDilate&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> It accepts the parameters: <i>struct_el</i>|<i>nb_iterations</i>. </p>
<p> <i>struct_el</i> represents a structuring element, and has the syntax: <i>cols</i>x<i>rows</i>+<i>anchor_x</i>x<i>anchor_y</i>/<i>shape</i> </p>
<p> <i>cols</i> and <i>rows</i> represent the number of columns and rows of the structuring element, <i>anchor_x</i> and <i>anchor_y</i> the anchor point, and <i>shape</i> the shape for the structuring element. <i>shape</i> must be &#8220;rect&#8221;, &#8220;cross&#8221;, &#8220;ellipse&#8221;, or &#8220;custom&#8221;. </p>
<p> If the value for <i>shape</i> is &#8220;custom&#8221;, it must be followed by a string of the form &quot;=<i>filename</i>&quot;. The file with name <i>filename</i> is assumed to represent a binary image, with each printable character corresponding to a bright pixel. When a custom <i>shape</i> is used, <i>cols</i> and <i>rows</i> are ignored, the number or columns and rows of the read file are assumed instead. </p>
<p> The default value for <i>struct_el</i> is &#8220;3&#215;3+0x0/rect&#8221;. </p>
<p> <i>nb_iterations</i> specifies the number of times the transform is applied to the image, and defaults to 1. </p>
<p> Some examples: </p>
<pre>
        # Use the default values
        ocv=dilate
        
        # Dilate using a structuring element with a 5x5 cross, iterating two times
        ocv=filter_name=dilate:filter_params=5x5+2x2/cross|2
        
        # Read the shape from the file diamond.shape, iterating two times.
        # The file diamond.shape may contain a pattern of characters like this
        #   *
        #  ***
        # *****
        #  ***
        #   *
        # The specified columns and rows are ignored
        # but the anchor point coordinates are not
        ocv=dilate:0x0+2x2/custom=diamond.shape|2
</pre>
<p> <i>erode</i> </p>
<p> Erode an image by using a specific structuring element. It corresponds to the libopencv function <tt>&quot;cvErode&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> It accepts the parameters: <i>struct_el</i>:<i>nb_iterations</i>, with the same syntax and semantics as the <code>dilate</code> filter. </p>
<p> <i>smooth</i> </p>
<p> Smooth the input video. </p>
<p> The filter takes the following parameters: <i>type</i>|<i>param1</i>|<i>param2</i>|<i>param3</i>|<i>param4</i>. </p>
<p> <i>type</i> is the type of smooth filter to apply, and must be one of the following values: &#8220;blur&#8221;, &#8220;blur_no_scale&#8221;, &#8220;median&#8221;, &#8220;gaussian&#8221;, or &#8220;bilateral&#8221;. The default value is &#8220;gaussian&#8221;. </p>
<p> The meaning of <i>param1</i>, <i>param2</i>, <i>param3</i>, and <i>param4</i> depends on the smooth type. <i>param1</i> and <i>param2</i> accept integer positive values or 0. <i>param3</i> and <i>param4</i> accept floating point values. </p>
<p> The default value for <i>param1</i> is 3. The default value for the other parameters is 0. </p>
<p> These parameters correspond to the parameters assigned to the libopencv function <tt>&quot;cvSmooth&quot;</tt>.</p>
<h3>oscilloscope</h3>
<p> 2D Video Oscilloscope. </p>
<p> Useful to measure spatial impulse, step responses, chroma delays, etc. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd> Set scope center x position.</p>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Set scope center y position.</p>
<dt><code>s</code></p>
<dd> Set scope size, relative to frame diagonal.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> Set scope tilt/rotation.</p>
<dt><code>o</code></p>
<dd> Set trace opacity.</p>
<dt><code>tx</code></p>
<dd> Set trace center x position.</p>
<dt><code>ty</code></p>
<dd> Set trace center y position.</p>
<dt><code>tw</code></p>
<dd> Set trace width, relative to width of frame.</p>
<dt><code>th</code></p>
<dd> Set trace height, relative to height of frame.</p>
<dt><code>c</code></p>
<dd> Set which components to trace. By default it traces first three components.</p>
<dt><code>g</code></p>
<dd> Draw trace grid. By default is enabled.</p>
<dt><code>st</code></p>
<dd> Draw some statistics. By default is enabled.</p>
<dt><code>sc</code></p>
<dd> Draw scope. By default is enabled.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Inspect full first row of video frame. </p>
<pre>
        oscilloscope=x=0.5:y=0:s=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Inspect full last row of video frame. </p>
<pre>
        oscilloscope=x=0.5:y=1:s=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Inspect full 5th line of video frame of height 1080. </p>
<pre>
        oscilloscope=x=0.5:y=5/1080:s=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Inspect full last column of video frame. </p>
<pre>
        oscilloscope=x=1:y=0.5:s=1:t=1
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>overlay</h3>
<p> Overlay one video on top of another. </p>
<p> It takes two inputs and has one output. The first input is the &#8220;main&#8221; video on which the second input is overlaid. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters: </p>
<p> A description of the accepted options follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Set the expression for the x and y coordinates of the overlaid video on the main video. Default value is &#8220;0&#8221; for both expressions. In case the expression is invalid, it is set to a huge value (meaning that the overlay will not be displayed within the output visible area).</p>
<dt><code>eof_action</code></p>
<dd> See <code>framesync</code>.</p>
<dt><code>eval</code></p>
<dd> Set when the expressions for <code>x</code>, and <code>y</code> are evaluated. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>init</code></p>
<dd> only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization or when a command is processed</p>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> evaluate expressions for each incoming frame</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>frame</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>shortest</code></p>
<dd> See <code>framesync</code>.</p>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> Set the format for the output video. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>yuv420</code></p>
<dd> force <font SIZE="-1">YUV420</font> output</p>
<dt><code>yuv420p10</code></p>
<dd> force YUV420p10 output</p>
<dt><code>yuv422</code></p>
<dd> force <font SIZE="-1">YUV422</font> output</p>
<dt><code>yuv422p10</code></p>
<dd> force YUV422p10 output</p>
<dt><code>yuv444</code></p>
<dd> force <font SIZE="-1">YUV444</font> output</p>
<dt><code>rgb</code></p>
<dd> force packed <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> output</p>
<dt><code>gbrp</code></p>
<dd> force planar <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> output</p>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> automatically pick format</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>yuv420</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>repeatlast</code></p>
<dd> See <code>framesync</code>.</p>
<dt><code>alpha</code></p>
<dd> Set format of alpha of the overlaid video, it can be <i>straight</i> or <i>premultiplied</i>. Default is <i>straight</i>.</dl>
<p> The <code>x</code>, and <code>y</code> expressions can contain the following parameters.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>main_w, W</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>main_h, H</code></p>
<dd> The main input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>overlay_w, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>overlay_h, h</code></p>
<dd> The overlay input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The computed values for <i>x</i> and <i>y</i>. They are evaluated for each new frame.</p>
<dt><code>hsub</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>vsub</code></p>
<dd> horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values of the output format. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> the number of input frame, starting from 0</p>
<dt><code>pos</code></p>
<dd> the position in the file of the input frame, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if unknown</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> The timestamp, expressed in seconds. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input timestamp is unknown.</dl>
<p> This filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options. </p>
<p> Note that the <i>n</i>, <i>pos</i>, <i>t</i> variables are available only when evaluation is done <i>per frame</i>, and will evaluate to <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> when <code>eval</code> is set to <code>init</code>. </p>
<p> Be aware that frames are taken from each input video in timestamp order, hence, if their initial timestamps differ, it is a good idea to pass the two inputs through a <i>setpts=PTS-STARTPTS</i> filter to have them begin in the same zero timestamp, as the example for the <i>movie</i> filter does. </p>
<p> You can chain together more overlays but you should test the efficiency of such approach. </p>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Modify the x and y of the overlay input. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Draw the overlay at 10 pixels from the bottom right corner of the main video: </p>
<pre>
        overlay=main_w-overlay_w-10:main_h-overlay_h-10
</pre>
<p> Using named options the example above becomes: </p>
<pre>
        overlay=x=main_w-overlay_w-10:y=main_h-overlay_h-10
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Insert a transparent <font SIZE="-1">PNG</font> logo in the bottom left corner of the input, using the <code>ffmpeg</code> tool with the <tt>&quot;-filter_complex&quot;</tt> option: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input -i logo -filter_complex 'overlay=10:main_h-overlay_h-10' output
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Insert 2 different transparent <font SIZE="-1">PNG</font> logos (second logo on bottom right corner) using the <code>ffmpeg</code> tool: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input -i logo1 -i logo2 -filter_complex 'overlay=x=10:y=H-h-10,overlay=x=W-w-10:y=H-h-10' output
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Add a transparent color layer on top of the main video; <tt>&quot;WxH&quot;</tt> must specify the size of the main input to the overlay filter: </p>
<p>color = color = red @.3 : size = WxH[over];<br />
[in][over] overlay[out]</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Play an original video and a filtered version (here with the deshake filter) side by side using the <code>ffplay</code> tool: </p>
<p>ffplay input.avi &#8211; vf &#8216;split[a][b]; [a]pad=iw*2:ih[src]; [b]deshake[filt]; [src][filt]overlay=w&#8217;</p>
<p> The above command is the same as: </p>
<p>ffplay input.avi &#8211; vf &#8216;split[b], pad=iw*2[src], [b]deshake, [src]overlay=w&#8217;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Make a sliding overlay appearing from the left to the right top part of the screen starting since time 2: </p>
<pre>
        overlay=x='if(gte(t,2), -w+(t-2)*20, NAN)':y=0
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Compose output by putting two input videos side to side: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i left.avi &#8211; i right.avi &#8211; filter_complex &#8221;<br />
    nullsrc = size = 200&#215;100 [background];<br />
[0:v] setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS, scale = 100&#215;100 [left];<br />
[1:v] setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS, scale = 100&#215;100 [right];<br />
[background][left] overlay = shortest = 1 [background + left];<br />
[background + left][right] overlay = shortest = 1 : x = 100 [left + right] &#8221;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Mask 10-20 seconds of a video by applying the delogo filter to a section </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i test.avi &#8211; codec : v : 0 wmv2 &#8211; ar 11025 &#8211; b : v 9000k &#8211; vf &#8216;[in]split[split_main][split_delogo];[split_delogo]trim=start=360:end=371,delogo=0:0:640:480[delogoed];[split_main][delogoed]overlay=eof_action=pass[out]&#8217; masked.avi</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Chain several overlays in cascade: </p>
<p>nullsrc = s = 200&#215;200 [bg];<br />
testsrc = s = 100&#215;100, split = 4 [in0][in1][in2][in3];<br />
[in0] lutrgb = r = 0, [bg] overlay = 0 : 0 [mid0];<br />
[in1] lutrgb = g = 0, [mid0] overlay = 100 : 0 [mid1];<br />
[in2] lutrgb = b = 0, [mid1] overlay = 0 : 100 [mid2];<br />
[in3] null, [mid2] overlay = 100 : 100 [out0]
</dl>
<h3>overlay_cuda</h3>
<p> Overlay one video on top of another. </p>
<p> This is the <font SIZE="-1">CUDA</font> variant of the <code>overlay</code> filter. It only accepts <font SIZE="-1">CUDA</font> frames. The underlying input pixel formats have to match. </p>
<p> It takes two inputs and has one output. The first input is the &#8220;main&#8221; video on which the second input is overlaid. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Set the x and y coordinates of the overlaid video on the main video. Default value is &#8220;0&#8221; for both expressions.</p>
<dt><code>eof_action</code></p>
<dd> See <code>framesync</code>.</p>
<dt><code>shortest</code></p>
<dd> See <code>framesync</code>.</p>
<dt><code>repeatlast</code></p>
<dd> See <code>framesync</code>.</dl>
<p> This filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options.</p>
<h3>owdenoise</h3>
<p> Apply Overcomplete Wavelet denoiser. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>depth</code></p>
<dd> Set depth. </p>
<p> Larger depth values will denoise lower frequency components more, but slow down filtering. </p>
<p> Must be an int in the range 8-16, default is <tt>8</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>luma_strength, ls</code></p>
<dd> Set luma strength. </p>
<p> Must be a double value in the range 0-1000, default is <tt>1.0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_strength, cs</code></p>
<dd> Set chroma strength. </p>
<p> Must be a double value in the range 0-1000, default is <tt>1.0</tt>.</dl>
<h3>pad</h3>
<p> Add paddings to the input image, and place the original input at the provided <i>x</i>, <i>y</i> coordinates. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>height, h</code></p>
<dd> Specify an expression for the size of the output image with the paddings added. If the value for <i>width</i> or <i>height</i> is 0, the corresponding input size is used for the output. </p>
<p> The <i>width</i> expression can reference the value set by the <i>height</i> expression, and vice versa. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>width</i> and <i>height</i> is 0.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Specify the offsets to place the input image at within the padded area, with respect to the top/left border of the output image. </p>
<p> The <i>x</i> expression can reference the value set by the <i>y</i> expression, and vice versa. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> is 0. </p>
<p> If <i>x</i> or <i>y</i> evaluate to a negative number, they&#8217;ll be changed so the input image is centered on the padded area.</p>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the padded area. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>color</i> is &#8220;black&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>eval</code></p>
<dd> Specify when to evaluate <i>width</i>, <i>height</i>, <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> expression. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>init</code></p>
<dd> Only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization or when a command is processed.</p>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Evaluate expressions for each incoming frame.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>init</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>aspect</code></p>
<dd> Pad to aspect instead to a resolution.</dl>
<p> The value for the <i>width</i>, <i>height</i>, <i>x</i>, and <i>y</i> options are expressions containing the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>in_w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>in_h</code></p>
<dd> The input video width and height.</p>
<dt><code>iw</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>ih</code></p>
<dd> These are the same as <i>in_w</i> and <i>in_h</i>.</p>
<dt><code>out_w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_h</code></p>
<dd> The output width and height (the size of the padded area), as specified by the <i>width</i> and <i>height</i> expressions.</p>
<dt><code>ow</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>oh</code></p>
<dd> These are the same as <i>out_w</i> and <i>out_h</i>.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> The x and y offsets as specified by the <i>x</i> and <i>y</i> expressions, or <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if not yet specified.</p>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> same as <i>iw</i> / <i>ih</i></p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> input sample aspect ratio</p>
<dt><code>dar</code></p>
<dd> input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (<i>iw</i> / <i>ih</i>) * <i>sar</i></p>
<dt><code>hsub</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>vsub</code></p>
<dd> The horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Add paddings with the color &#8220;violet&#8221; to the input video. The output video size is 640&#215;480, and the top-left corner of the input video is placed at column 0, row 40 </p>
<pre>
        pad=640:480:0:40:violet
</pre>
<p> The example above is equivalent to the following command: </p>
<pre>
        pad=width=640:height=480:x=0:y=40:color=violet
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Pad the input to get an output with dimensions increased by 3/2, and put the input video at the center of the padded area: </p>
<pre>
        pad=&quot;3/2*iw:3/2*ih:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Pad the input to get a squared output with size equal to the maximum value between the input width and height, and put the input video at the center of the padded area: </p>
<pre>
        pad=&quot;max(iw\,ih):ow:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Pad the input to get a final w/h ratio of 16:9: </p>
<pre>
        pad=&quot;ih*16/9:ih:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> In case of anamorphic video, in order to set the output display aspect correctly, it is necessary to use <i>sar</i> in the expression, according to the relation: </p>
<pre>
        (ih * X / ih) * sar = output_dar
        X = output_dar / sar
</pre>
<p> Thus the previous example needs to be modified to: </p>
<pre>
        pad=&quot;ih*16/9/sar:ih:(ow-iw)/2:(oh-ih)/2&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Double the output size and put the input video in the bottom-right corner of the output padded area: </p>
<pre>
        pad=&quot;2*iw:2*ih:ow-iw:oh-ih&quot;
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>palettegen</h3>
<p> Generate one palette for a whole video stream. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>max_colors</code></p>
<dd> Set the maximum number of colors to quantize in the palette. Note: the palette will still contain 256 colors; the unused palette entries will be black.</p>
<dt><code>reserve_transparent</code></p>
<dd> Create a palette of 255 colors maximum and reserve the last one for transparency. Reserving the transparency color is useful for <font SIZE="-1">GIF</font> optimization. If not set, the maximum of colors in the palette will be 256. You probably want to disable this option for a standalone image. Set by default.</p>
<dt><code>transparency_color</code></p>
<dd> Set the color that will be used as background for transparency.</p>
<dt><code>stats_mode</code></p>
<dd> Set statistics mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>full</code></p>
<dd> Compute full frame histograms.</p>
<dt><code>diff</code></p>
<dd> Compute histograms only for the part that differs from previous frame. This might be relevant to give more importance to the moving part of your input if the background is static.</p>
<dt><code>single</code></p>
<dd> Compute new histogram for each frame.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <i>full</i>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> The filter also exports the frame metadata <tt>&quot;lavfi.color_quant_ratio&quot;</tt> (<tt>&quot;nb_color_in / nb_color_out&quot;</tt>) which you can use to evaluate the degree of color quantization of the palette. This information is also visible at <i>info</i> logging level. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a representative palette of a given video using <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.mkv -vf palettegen palette.png
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>paletteuse</h3>
<p> Use a palette to downsample an input video stream. </p>
<p> The filter takes two inputs: one video stream and a palette. The palette must be a 256 pixels image. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dither</code></p>
<dd> Select dithering mode. Available algorithms are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>bayer</code></p>
<dd> Ordered 8&#215;8 bayer dithering (deterministic)</p>
<dt><code>heckbert</code></p>
<dd> Dithering as defined by Paul Heckbert in 1982 (simple error diffusion). Note: this dithering is sometimes considered &#8220;wrong&#8221; and is included as a reference.</p>
<dt><code>floyd_steinberg</code></p>
<dd> Floyd and Steingberg dithering (error diffusion)</p>
<dt><code>sierra2</code></p>
<dd> Frankie Sierra dithering v2 (error diffusion)</p>
<dt><code>sierra2_4a</code></p>
<dd> Frankie Sierra dithering v2 &#8220;Lite&#8221; (error diffusion)</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <i>sierra2_4a</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>bayer_scale</code></p>
<dd> When <i>bayer</i> dithering is selected, this option defines the scale of the pattern (how much the crosshatch pattern is visible). A low value means more visible pattern for less banding, and higher value means less visible pattern at the cost of more banding. </p>
<p> The option must be an integer value in the range [0,5]. Default is <i>2</i>.</p>
<dt><code>diff_mode</code></p>
<dd> If set, define the zone to process</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>rectangle</code></p>
<dd> Only the changing rectangle will be reprocessed. This is similar to <font SIZE="-1">GIF</font> cropping/offsetting compression mechanism. This option can be useful for speed if only a part of the image is changing, and has use cases such as limiting the scope of the error diffusal <code>dither</code> to the rectangle that bounds the moving scene (it leads to more deterministic output if the scene doesn&#8217;t change much, and as a result less moving noise and better <font SIZE="-1">GIF</font> compression).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <i>none</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>new</code></p>
<dd> Take new palette for each output frame.</p>
<dt><code>alpha_threshold</code></p>
<dd> Sets the alpha threshold for transparency. Alpha values above this threshold will be treated as completely opaque, and values below this threshold will be treated as completely transparent. </p>
<p> The option must be an integer value in the range [0,255]. Default is <i>128</i>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Use a palette (generated for example with <code>palettegen</code>) to encode a <font SIZE="-1">GIF</font> using <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.mkv -i palette.png -lavfi paletteuse output.gif
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>perspective</h3>
<p> Correct perspective of video not recorded perpendicular to the screen. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted parameters follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>x1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>x2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>x3</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y3</code></p>
<dd> Set coordinates expression for top left, top right, bottom left and bottom right corners. Default values are <tt>&quot;0:0:W:0:0:H:W:H&quot;</tt> with which perspective will remain unchanged. If the <tt>&quot;sense&quot;</tt> option is set to <tt>&quot;source&quot;</tt>, then the specified points will be sent to the corners of the destination. If the <tt>&quot;sense&quot;</tt> option is set to <tt>&quot;destination&quot;</tt>, then the corners of the source will be sent to the specified coordinates. </p>
<p> The expressions can use the following variables:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>W</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>H</code></p>
<dd> the width and height of video frame.</p>
<dt><code>in</code></p>
<dd> Input frame count.</p>
<dt><code>on</code></p>
<dd> Output frame count.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>interpolation</code></p>
<dd> Set interpolation for perspective correction. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>linear</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cubic</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>linear</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>sense</code></p>
<dd> Set interpretation of coordinate options. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>0, source</code></p>
<dd> Send point in the source specified by the given coordinates to the corners of the destination.</p>
<dt><code>1, destination</code></p>
<dd> Send the corners of the source to the point in the destination specified by the given coordinates. </p>
<p> Default value is <code>source</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>eval</code></p>
<dd> Set when the expressions for coordinates <code>x0,y0,&#8230;x3,y3</code> are evaluated. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>init</code></p>
<dd> only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization or when a command is processed</p>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> evaluate expressions for each incoming frame</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>init</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<h3>phase</h3>
<p> Delay interlaced video by one field time so that the field order changes. </p>
<p> The intended use is to fix <font SIZE="-1">PAL</font> movies that have been captured with the opposite field order to the film-to-video transfer. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted parameters follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set phase mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> Capture field order top-first, transfer bottom-first. Filter will delay the bottom field.</p>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd> Capture field order bottom-first, transfer top-first. Filter will delay the top field.</p>
<dt><code>p</code></p>
<dd> Capture and transfer with the same field order. This mode only exists for the documentation of the other options to refer to, but if you actually select it, the filter will faithfully do nothing.</p>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> Capture field order determined automatically by field flags, transfer opposite. Filter selects among <code>t</code> and <code>b</code> modes on a frame by frame basis using field flags. If no field information is available, then this works just like <code>u</code>.</p>
<dt><code>u</code></p>
<dd> Capture unknown or varying, transfer opposite. Filter selects among <code>t</code> and <code>b</code> on a frame by frame basis by analyzing the images and selecting the alternative that produces best match between the fields.</p>
<dt><code>T</code></p>
<dd> Capture top-first, transfer unknown or varying. Filter selects among <code>t</code> and <code>p</code> using image analysis.</p>
<dt><code>B</code></p>
<dd> Capture bottom-first, transfer unknown or varying. Filter selects among <code>b</code> and <code>p</code> using image analysis.</p>
<dt><code>A</code></p>
<dd> Capture determined by field flags, transfer unknown or varying. Filter selects among <code>t</code>, <code>b</code> and <code>p</code> using field flags and image analysis. If no field information is available, then this works just like <code>U</code>. This is the default mode.</p>
<dt><code>U</code></p>
<dd> Both capture and transfer unknown or varying. Filter selects among <code>t</code>, <code>b</code> and <code>p</code> using image analysis only.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>photosensitivity</h3>
<p> Reduce various flashes in video, so to help users with epilepsy. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>frames, f</code></p>
<dd> Set how many frames to use when filtering. Default is 30.</p>
<dt><code>threshold, t</code></p>
<dd> Set detection threshold factor. Default is 1. Lower is stricter.</p>
<dt><code>skip</code></p>
<dd> Set how many pixels to skip when sampling frames. Default is 1. Allowed range is from 1 to 1024.</p>
<dt><code>bypass</code></p>
<dd> Leave frames unchanged. Default is disabled.</dl>
<h3>pixdesctest</h3>
<p> Pixel format descriptor test filter, mainly useful for internal testing. The output video should be equal to the input video. </p>
<p> For example: </p>
<pre>
        format=monow, pixdesctest
</pre>
<p> can be used to test the monowhite pixel format descriptor definition.</p>
<h3>pixscope</h3>
<p> Display sample values of color channels. Mainly useful for checking color and levels. Minimum supported resolution is 640&#215;480. </p>
<p> The filters accept the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd> Set scope X position, relative offset on X axis.</p>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Set scope Y position, relative offset on Y axis.</p>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd> Set scope width.</p>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> Set scope height.</p>
<dt><code>o</code></p>
<dd> Set window opacity. This window also holds statistics about pixel area.</p>
<dt><code>wx</code></p>
<dd> Set window X position, relative offset on X axis.</p>
<dt><code>wy</code></p>
<dd> Set window Y position, relative offset on Y axis.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options.</p>
<h3>pp</h3>
<p> Enable the specified chain of postprocessing subfilters using libpostproc. This library should be automatically selected with a <font SIZE="-1">GPL</font> build (<tt>&quot;--enable-gpl&quot;</tt>). Subfilters must be separated by &#8216;/&#8217; and can be disabled by prepending a &#8216;-&#8216;. Each subfilter and some options have a short and a long name that can be used interchangeably, i.e. dr/dering are the same. </p>
<p> The filters accept the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>subfilters</code></p>
<dd> Set postprocessing subfilters string.</dl>
<p> All subfilters share common options to determine their scope:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>a/autoq</code></p>
<dd> Honor the quality commands for this subfilter.</p>
<dt><code>c/chrom</code></p>
<dd> Do chrominance filtering, too (default).</p>
<dt><code>y/nochrom</code></p>
<dd> Do luminance filtering only (no chrominance).</p>
<dt><code>n/noluma</code></p>
<dd> Do chrominance filtering only (no luminance).</dl>
<p> These options can be appended after the subfilter name, separated by a &#8216;|&#8217;. </p>
<p> Available subfilters are:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>hb/hdeblock[|difference[|flatness]]</code></p>
<dd> Horizontal deblocking filter</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>difference</code></p>
<dd> Difference factor where higher values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>32</tt>).</p>
<dt><code>flatness</code></p>
<dd> Flatness threshold where lower values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>39</tt>).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>vb/vdeblock[|difference[|flatness]]</code></p>
<dd> Vertical deblocking filter</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>difference</code></p>
<dd> Difference factor where higher values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>32</tt>).</p>
<dt><code>flatness</code></p>
<dd> Flatness threshold where lower values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>39</tt>).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>ha/hadeblock[|difference[|flatness]]</code></p>
<dd> Accurate horizontal deblocking filter</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>difference</code></p>
<dd> Difference factor where higher values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>32</tt>).</p>
<dt><code>flatness</code></p>
<dd> Flatness threshold where lower values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>39</tt>).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>va/vadeblock[|difference[|flatness]]</code></p>
<dd> Accurate vertical deblocking filter</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>difference</code></p>
<dd> Difference factor where higher values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>32</tt>).</p>
<dt><code>flatness</code></p>
<dd> Flatness threshold where lower values mean more deblocking (default: <tt>39</tt>).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> The horizontal and vertical deblocking filters share the difference and flatness values so you cannot set different horizontal and vertical thresholds.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>h1/x1hdeblock</code></p>
<dd> Experimental horizontal deblocking filter</p>
<dt><code>v1/x1vdeblock</code></p>
<dd> Experimental vertical deblocking filter</p>
<dt><code>dr/dering</code></p>
<dd> Deringing filter</p>
<dt><code>tn/tmpnoise[|threshold1[|threshold2[|threshold3]]], temporal noise reducer</code></p>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold1</code></p>
<dd> larger -&gt; stronger filtering</p>
<dt><code>threshold2</code></p>
<dd> larger -&gt; stronger filtering</p>
<dt><code>threshold3</code></p>
<dd> larger -&gt; stronger filtering</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>al/autolevels[:f/fullyrange], automatic brightness / contrast correction</code></p>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>f/fullyrange</code></p>
<dd> Stretch luminance to <tt>&quot;0-255&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>lb/linblenddeint</code></p>
<dd> Linear blend deinterlacing filter that deinterlaces the given block by filtering all lines with a <tt>&quot;(1 2 1)&quot;</tt> filter.</p>
<dt><code>li/linipoldeint</code></p>
<dd> Linear interpolating deinterlacing filter that deinterlaces the given block by linearly interpolating every second line.</p>
<dt><code>ci/cubicipoldeint</code></p>
<dd> Cubic interpolating deinterlacing filter deinterlaces the given block by cubically interpolating every second line.</p>
<dt><code>md/mediandeint</code></p>
<dd> Median deinterlacing filter that deinterlaces the given block by applying a median filter to every second line.</p>
<dt><code>fd/ffmpegdeint</code></p>
<dd> FFmpeg deinterlacing filter that deinterlaces the given block by filtering every second line with a <tt>&quot;(-1 4 2 4 -1)&quot;</tt> filter.</p>
<dt><code>l5/lowpass5</code></p>
<dd> Vertically applied <font SIZE="-1">FIR</font> lowpass deinterlacing filter that deinterlaces the given block by filtering all lines with a <tt>&quot;(-1 2 6 2 -1)&quot;</tt> filter.</p>
<dt><code>fq/forceQuant[|quantizer]</code></p>
<dd> Overrides the quantizer table from the input with the constant quantizer you specify.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>quantizer</code></p>
<dd> Quantizer to use</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>de/default</code></p>
<dd> Default pp filter combination (<tt>&quot;hb|a,vb|a,dr|a&quot;</tt>)</p>
<dt><code>fa/fast</code></p>
<dd> Fast pp filter combination (<tt>&quot;h1|a,v1|a,dr|a&quot;</tt>)</p>
<dt><code>ac</code></p>
<dd> High quality pp filter combination (<tt>&quot;ha|a|128|7,va|a,dr|a&quot;</tt>)</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply horizontal and vertical deblocking, deringing and automatic brightness/contrast: </p>
<pre>
        pp=hb/vb/dr/al
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply default filters without brightness/contrast correction: </p>
<pre>
        pp=de/-al
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply default filters and temporal denoiser: </p>
<pre>
        pp=default/tmpnoise|1|2|3
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply deblocking on luminance only, and switch vertical deblocking on or off automatically depending on available <font SIZE="-1">CPU</font> time: </p>
<pre>
        pp=hb|y/vb|a
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>pp7</h3>
<p> Apply Postprocessing filter 7. It is variant of the <code>spp</code> filter, similar to spp = 6 with 7 point <font SIZE="-1">DCT,</font> where only the center sample is used after <font SIZE="-1">IDCT.</font> </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>qp</code></p>
<dd> Force a constant quantization parameter. It accepts an integer in range 0 to 63. If not set, the filter will use the <font SIZE="-1">QP</font> from the video stream (if available).</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set thresholding mode. Available modes are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>hard</code></p>
<dd> Set hard thresholding.</p>
<dt><code>soft</code></p>
<dd> Set soft thresholding (better de-ringing effect, but likely blurrier).</p>
<dt><code>medium</code></p>
<dd> Set medium thresholding (good results, default).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<h3>premultiply</h3>
<p> Apply alpha premultiply effect to input video stream using first plane of second stream as alpha. </p>
<p> Both streams must have same dimensions and same pixel format. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</p>
<dt><code>inplace</code></p>
<dd> Do not require 2nd input for processing, instead use alpha plane from input stream.</dl>
<h3>prewitt</h3>
<p> Apply prewitt operator to input video stream. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be multiplied with filtered result.</p>
<dt><code>delta</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be added to filtered result.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>pseudocolor</h3>
<p> Alter frame colors in video with pseudocolors. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>c0</code></p>
<dd> set pixel first component expression</p>
<dt><code>c1</code></p>
<dd> set pixel second component expression</p>
<dt><code>c2</code></p>
<dd> set pixel third component expression</p>
<dt><code>c3</code></p>
<dd> set pixel fourth component expression, corresponds to the alpha component</p>
<dt><code>index, i</code></p>
<dd> set component to use as base for altering colors</p>
<dt><code>preset, p</code></p>
<dd> Pick one of built-in LUTs. By default is set to none. </p>
<p> Available LUTs:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>magma</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>inferno</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>plasma</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>viridis</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>turbo</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cividis</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>range1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>range2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>shadows</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>highlights</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>opacity</code></p>
<dd> Set opacity of output colors. Allowed range is from 0 to 1. Default value is set to 1.</dl>
<p> Each of the expression options specifies the expression to use for computing the lookup table for the corresponding pixel component values. </p>
<p> The expressions can contain the following constants and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> The input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>val</code></p>
<dd> The input value for the pixel component.</p>
<dt><code>ymin, umin, vmin, amin</code></p>
<dd> The minimum allowed component value.</p>
<dt><code>ymax, umax, vmax, amax</code></p>
<dd> The maximum allowed component value.</dl>
<p> All expressions default to &#8220;val&#8221;. </p>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Change too high luma values to gradient: </p>
<pre>
        pseudocolor=&quot;'if(between(val,ymax,amax),lerp(ymin,ymax,(val-ymax)/(amax-ymax)),-1):if(between(val,ymax,amax),lerp(umax,umin,(val-ymax)/(amax-ymax)),-1):if(between(val,ymax,amax),lerp(vmin,vmax,(val-ymax)/(amax-ymax)),-1):-1'&quot;
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>psnr</h3>
<p> Obtain the average, maximum and minimum <font SIZE="-1">PSNR</font> (Peak Signal to Noise Ratio) between two input videos. </p>
<p> This filter takes in input two input videos, the first input is considered the &#8220;main&#8221; source and is passed unchanged to the output. The second input is used as a &#8220;reference&#8221; video for computing the <font SIZE="-1">PSNR.</font> </p>
<p> Both video inputs must have the same resolution and pixel format for this filter to work correctly. Also it assumes that both inputs have the same number of frames, which are compared one by one. </p>
<p> The obtained average <font SIZE="-1">PSNR</font> is printed through the logging system. </p>
<p> The filter stores the accumulated <font SIZE="-1">MSE</font> (mean squared error) of each frame, and at the end of the processing it is averaged across all frames equally, and the following formula is applied to obtain the <font SIZE="-1">PSNR:</font> </p>
<pre>
        PSNR = 10*log10(MAX^2/MSE)
</pre>
<p> Where <font SIZE="-1">MAX</font> is the average of the maximum values of each component of the image. </p>
<p> The description of the accepted parameters follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>stats_file, f</code></p>
<dd> If specified the filter will use the named file to save the <font SIZE="-1">PSNR</font> of each individual frame. When filename equals &#8220;-&#8221; the data is sent to standard output.</p>
<dt><code>stats_version</code></p>
<dd> Specifies which version of the stats file format to use. Details of each format are written below. Default value is 1.</p>
<dt><code>stats_add_max</code></p>
<dd> Determines whether the max value is output to the stats log. Default value is 0. Requires stats_version &gt;= 2. If this is set and stats_version &lt; 2, the filter will return an error.</dl>
<p> This filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options. </p>
<p> The file printed if <i>stats_file</i> is selected, contains a sequence of key/value pairs of the form <i>key</i>:<i>value</i> for each compared couple of frames. </p>
<p> If a <i>stats_version</i> greater than 1 is specified, a header line precedes the list of per-frame-pair stats, with key value pairs following the frame format with the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>psnr_log_version</code></p>
<dd> The version of the log file format. Will match <i>stats_version</i>.</p>
<dt><code>fields</code></p>
<dd> A comma separated list of the per-frame-pair parameters included in the log.</dl>
<p> A description of each shown per-frame-pair parameter follows:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> sequential number of the input frame, starting from 1</p>
<dt><code>mse_avg</code></p>
<dd> Mean Square Error pixel-by-pixel average difference of the compared frames, averaged over all the image components.</p>
<dt><code>mse_y, mse_u, mse_v, mse_r, mse_g, mse_b, mse_a</code></p>
<dd> Mean Square Error pixel-by-pixel average difference of the compared frames for the component specified by the suffix.</p>
<dt><code>psnr_y, psnr_u, psnr_v, psnr_r, psnr_g, psnr_b, psnr_a</code></p>
<dd> Peak Signal to Noise ratio of the compared frames for the component specified by the suffix.</p>
<dt><code>max_avg, max_y, max_u, max_v</code></p>
<dd> Maximum allowed value for each channel, and average over all channels.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> For example: </p>
<p>movie = ref_movie.mpg, setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS[main];<br />
[main][ref] psnr = &#8220;stats_file=stats.log&#8221;[out]</p>
<p> On this example the input file being processed is compared with the reference file <i>ref_movie.mpg</i>. The <font SIZE="-1">PSNR</font> of each individual frame is stored in <i>stats.log</i>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Another example with different containers: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i main.mpg &#8211; i ref.mkv &#8211; lavfi &#8220;[0:v]settb=AVTB,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS[main];[1:v]settb=AVTB,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS[ref];[main][ref]psnr&#8221; &#8211; f null &#8211;
</dl>
<h3>pullup</h3>
<p> Pulldown reversal (inverse telecine) filter, capable of handling mixed hard-telecine, 24000/1001 fps progressive, and 30000/1001 fps progressive content. </p>
<p> The pullup filter is designed to take advantage of future context in making its decisions. This filter is stateless in the sense that it does not lock onto a pattern to follow, but it instead looks forward to the following fields in order to identify matches and rebuild progressive frames. </p>
<p> To produce content with an even framerate, insert the fps filter after pullup, use <tt>&quot;fps=24000/1001&quot;</tt> if the input frame rate is 29.97fps, <tt>&quot;fps=24&quot;</tt> for 30fps and the (rare) telecined 25fps input. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>jl</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>jr</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>jt</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>jb</code></p>
