<dd> These options set the amount of &#8220;junk&#8221; to ignore at the left, right, top, and bottom of the image, respectively. Left and right are in units of 8 pixels, while top and bottom are in units of 2 lines. The default is 8 pixels on each side.</p>
<dt><code>sb</code></p>
<dd> Set the strict breaks. Setting this option to 1 will reduce the chances of filter generating an occasional mismatched frame, but it may also cause an excessive number of frames to be dropped during high motion sequences. Conversely, setting it to -1 will make filter match fields more easily. This may help processing of video where there is slight blurring between the fields, but may also cause there to be interlaced frames in the output. Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>mp</code></p>
<dd> Set the metric plane to use. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>l</code></p>
<dd> Use luma plane.</p>
<dt><code>u</code></p>
<dd> Use chroma blue plane.</p>
<dt><code>v</code></p>
<dd> Use chroma red plane.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> This option may be set to use chroma plane instead of the default luma plane for doing filter&#8217;s computations. This may improve accuracy on very clean source material, but more likely will decrease accuracy, especially if there is chroma noise (rainbow effect) or any grayscale video. The main purpose of setting <code>mp</code> to a chroma plane is to reduce <font SIZE="-1">CPU</font> load and make pullup usable in realtime on slow machines.</dl>
</dl>
<p> For best results (without duplicated frames in the output file) it is necessary to change the output frame rate. For example, to inverse telecine <font SIZE="-1">NTSC</font> input: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input -vf pullup -r 24000/1001 ...
</pre>
<h3>qp</h3>
<p> Change video quantization parameters (<font SIZE="-1">QP</font>). </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>qp</code></p>
<dd> Set expression for quantization parameter.</dl>
<p> The expression is evaluated through the eval <font SIZE="-1">API</font> and can contain, among others, the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><i>known</i></p>
<dd> 1 if index is not 129, 0 otherwise.</p>
<dt><i>qp</i></p>
<dd> Sequential index starting from -129 to 128.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Some equation like: </p>
<pre>
        qp=2+2*sin(PI*qp)
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>random</h3>
<p> Flush video frames from internal cache of frames into a random order. No frame is discarded. Inspired by <code>frei0r</code> nervous filter.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>frames</code></p>
<dd> Set size in number of frames of internal cache, in range from <tt>2</tt> to <tt>512</tt>. Default is <tt>30</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>seed</code></p>
<dd> Set seed for random number generator, must be an integer included between <tt>0</tt> and <tt>&quot;UINT32_MAX&quot;</tt>. If not specified, or if explicitly set to less than <tt>0</tt>, the filter will try to use a good random seed on a best effort basis.</dl>
<h3>readeia608</h3>
<p> Read closed captioning (<font SIZE="-1">EIA-608</font>) information from the top lines of a video frame. </p>
<p> This filter adds frame metadata for <tt>&quot;lavfi.readeia608.X.cc&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;lavfi.readeia608.X.line&quot;</tt>, where <tt>&quot;X&quot;</tt> is the number of the identified line with <font SIZE="-1">EIA-608</font> data (starting from 0). A description of each metadata value follows:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>lavfi.readeia608.X.cc</code></p>
<dd> The two bytes stored as <font SIZE="-1">EIA-608</font> data (printed in hexadecimal).</p>
<dt><code>lavfi.readeia608.X.line</code></p>
<dd> The number of the line on which the <font SIZE="-1">EIA-608</font> data was identified and read.</dl>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>scan_min</code></p>
<dd> Set the line to start scanning for <font SIZE="-1">EIA-608</font> data. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>scan_max</code></p>
<dd> Set the line to end scanning for <font SIZE="-1">EIA-608</font> data. Default is <tt>29</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>spw</code></p>
<dd> Set the ratio of width reserved for sync code detection. Default is <tt>0.27</tt>. Allowed range is <tt>&quot;[0.1 - 0.7]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>chp</code></p>
<dd> Enable checking the parity bit. In the event of a parity error, the filter will output <tt>0x00</tt> for that character. Default is false.</p>
<dt><code>lp</code></p>
<dd> Lowpass lines prior to further processing. Default is enabled.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Output a csv with presentation time and the first two lines of identified <font SIZE="-1">EIA-608</font> captioning data. </p>
<pre>
        ffprobe -f lavfi -i movie=captioned_video.mov,readeia608 -show_entries frame=pkt_pts_time:frame_tags=lavfi.readeia608.0.cc,lavfi.readeia608.1.cc -of csv
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>readvitc</h3>
<p> Read vertical interval timecode (<font SIZE="-1">VITC</font>) information from the top lines of a video frame. </p>
<p> The filter adds frame metadata key <tt>&quot;lavfi.readvitc.tc_str&quot;</tt> with the timecode value, if a valid timecode has been detected. Further metadata key <tt>&quot;lavfi.readvitc.found&quot;</tt> is set to 0/1 depending on whether timecode data has been found or not. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>scan_max</code></p>
<dd> Set the maximum number of lines to scan for <font SIZE="-1">VITC</font> data. If the value is set to <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt> the full video frame is scanned. Default is <tt>45</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>thr_b</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma threshold for black. Accepts float numbers in the range [0.0,1.0], default value is <tt>0.2</tt>. The value must be equal or less than <tt>&quot;thr_w&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>thr_w</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma threshold for white. Accepts float numbers in the range [0.0,1.0], default value is <tt>0.6</tt>. The value must be equal or greater than <tt>&quot;thr_b&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Detect and draw <font SIZE="-1">VITC</font> data onto the video frame; if no valid <font SIZE="-1">VITC</font> is detected, draw <tt>&quot;--:--:--:--&quot;</tt> as a placeholder: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i input.avi &#8211; filter : v &#8216;readvitc,drawtext=fontfile=FreeMono.ttf:text=%{metadata\:lavfi.readvitc.tc_str\:&#8211;\\\:&#8211;\\\:&#8211;\\\:&#8211;}:x=(w-tw)/2:y=400-ascent&#8217;
</dl>
<h3>remap</h3>
<p> Remap pixels using 2nd: Xmap and 3rd: Ymap input video stream. </p>
<p> Destination pixel at position (X, Y) will be picked from source (x, y) position where x = Xmap(X, Y) and y = Ymap(X, Y). If mapping values are out of range, zero value for pixel will be used for destination pixel. </p>
<p> Xmap and Ymap input video streams must be of same dimensions. Output video stream will have Xmap/Ymap video stream dimensions. Xmap and Ymap input video streams are 16bit depth, single channel.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> Specify pixel format of output from this filter. Can be <tt>&quot;color&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;gray&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;color&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>fill</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the unmapped pixels. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default color is <tt>&quot;black&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<h3>removegrain</h3>
<p> The removegrain filter is a spatial denoiser for progressive video.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>m0</code></p>
<dd> Set mode for the first plane.</p>
<dt><code>m1</code></p>
<dd> Set mode for the second plane.</p>
<dt><code>m2</code></p>
<dd> Set mode for the third plane.</p>
<dt><code>m3</code></p>
<dd> Set mode for the fourth plane.</dl>
<p> Range of mode is from 0 to 24. Description of each mode follows:</p>
<dl>
<dt><i>0</i></p>
<dd> Leave input plane unchanged. Default.</p>
<dt><i>1</i></p>
<dd> Clips the pixel with the minimum and maximum of the 8 neighbour pixels.</p>
<dt><i>2</i></p>
<dd> Clips the pixel with the second minimum and maximum of the 8 neighbour pixels.</p>
<dt><i>3</i></p>
<dd> Clips the pixel with the third minimum and maximum of the 8 neighbour pixels.</p>
<dt><i>4</i></p>
<dd> Clips the pixel with the fourth minimum and maximum of the 8 neighbour pixels. This is equivalent to a median filter.</p>
<dt><i>5</i></p>
<dd> Line-sensitive clipping giving the minimal change.</p>
<dt><i>6</i></p>
<dd> Line-sensitive clipping, intermediate.</p>
<dt><i>7</i></p>
<dd> Line-sensitive clipping, intermediate.</p>
<dt><i>8</i></p>
<dd> Line-sensitive clipping, intermediate.</p>
<dt><i>9</i></p>
<dd> Line-sensitive clipping on a line where the neighbours pixels are the closest.</p>
<dt><i>10</i></p>
<dd> Replaces the target pixel with the closest neighbour.</p>
<dt><i>11</i></p>
<dd> [1 2 1] horizontal and vertical kernel blur.</p>
<dt><i>12</i></p>
<dd> Same as mode 11.</p>
<dt><i>13</i></p>
<dd> Bob mode, interpolates top field from the line where the neighbours pixels are the closest.</p>
<dt><i>14</i></p>
<dd> Bob mode, interpolates bottom field from the line where the neighbours pixels are the closest.</p>
<dt><i>15</i></p>
<dd> Bob mode, interpolates top field. Same as 13 but with a more complicated interpolation formula.</p>
<dt><i>16</i></p>
<dd> Bob mode, interpolates bottom field. Same as 14 but with a more complicated interpolation formula.</p>
<dt><i>17</i></p>
<dd> Clips the pixel with the minimum and maximum of respectively the maximum and minimum of each pair of opposite neighbour pixels.</p>
<dt><i>18</i></p>
<dd> Line-sensitive clipping using opposite neighbours whose greatest distance from the current pixel is minimal.</p>
<dt><i>19</i></p>
<dd> Replaces the pixel with the average of its 8 neighbours.</p>
<dt><i>20</i></p>
<dd> Averages the 9 pixels ([1 1 1] horizontal and vertical blur).</p>
<dt><i>21</i></p>
<dd> Clips pixels using the averages of opposite neighbour.</p>
<dt><i>22</i></p>
<dd> Same as mode 21 but simpler and faster.</p>
<dt><i>23</i></p>
<dd> Small edge and halo removal, but reputed useless.</p>
<dt><i>24</i></p>
<dd> Similar as 23.</dl>
<h3>removelogo</h3>
<p> Suppress a <font SIZE="-1">TV</font> station logo, using an image file to determine which pixels comprise the logo. It works by filling in the pixels that comprise the logo with neighboring pixels. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filename, f</code></p>
<dd> Set the filter bitmap file, which can be any image format supported by libavformat. The width and height of the image file must match those of the video stream being processed.</dl>
<p> Pixels in the provided bitmap image with a value of zero are not considered part of the logo, non-zero pixels are considered part of the logo. If you use white (255) for the logo and black (0) for the rest, you will be safe. For making the filter bitmap, it is recommended to take a screen capture of a black frame with the logo visible, and then using a threshold filter followed by the erode filter once or twice. </p>
<p> If needed, little splotches can be fixed manually. Remember that if logo pixels are not covered, the filter quality will be much reduced. Marking too many pixels as part of the logo does not hurt as much, but it will increase the amount of blurring needed to cover over the image and will destroy more information than necessary, and extra pixels will slow things down on a large logo.</p>
<h3>repeatfields</h3>
<p> This filter uses the repeat_field flag from the Video <font SIZE="-1">ES</font> headers and hard repeats fields based on its value.</p>
<h3>reverse</h3>
<p> Reverse a video clip. </p>
<p> Warning: This filter requires memory to buffer the entire clip, so trimming is suggested. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Take the first 5 seconds of a clip, and reverse it. </p>
<pre>
        trim=end=5,reverse
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>rgbashift</h3>
<p> Shift R/G/B/A pixels horizontally and/or vertically. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>rh</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift red horizontally.</p>
<dt><code>rv</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift red vertically.</p>
<dt><code>gh</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift green horizontally.</p>
<dt><code>gv</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift green vertically.</p>
<dt><code>bh</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift blue horizontally.</p>
<dt><code>bv</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift blue vertically.</p>
<dt><code>ah</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift alpha horizontally.</p>
<dt><code>av</code></p>
<dd> Set amount to shift alpha vertically.</p>
<dt><code>edge</code></p>
<dd> Set edge mode, can be <i>smear</i>, default, or <i>warp</i>.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>roberts</h3>
<p> Apply roberts cross operator to input video stream. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be multiplied with filtered result.</p>
<dt><code>delta</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be added to filtered result.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>rotate</h3>
<p> Rotate video by an arbitrary angle expressed in radians. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options: </p>
<p> A description of the optional parameters follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>angle, a</code></p>
<dd> Set an expression for the angle by which to rotate the input video clockwise, expressed as a number of radians. A negative value will result in a counter-clockwise rotation. By default it is set to &#8220;0&#8221;. </p>
<p> This expression is evaluated for each frame.</p>
<dt><code>out_w, ow</code></p>
<dd> Set the output width expression, default value is &#8220;iw&#8221;. This expression is evaluated just once during configuration.</p>
<dt><code>out_h, oh</code></p>
<dd> Set the output height expression, default value is &#8220;ih&#8221;. This expression is evaluated just once during configuration.</p>
<dt><code>bilinear</code></p>
<dd> Enable bilinear interpolation if set to 1, a value of 0 disables it. Default value is 1.</p>
<dt><code>fillcolor, c</code></p>
<dd> Set the color used to fill the output area not covered by the rotated image. For the general syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. If the special value &#8220;none&#8221; is selected then no background is printed (useful for example if the background is never shown). </p>
<p> Default value is &#8220;black&#8221;.</dl>
<p> The expressions for the angle and the output size can contain the following constants and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> sequential number of the input frame, starting from 0. It is always <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> before the first frame is filtered.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> time in seconds of the input frame, it is set to 0 when the filter is configured. It is always <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> before the first frame is filtered.</p>
<dt><code>hsub</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>vsub</code></p>
<dd> horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><code>in_w, iw</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>in_h, ih</code></p>
<dd> the input video width and height</p>
<dt><code>out_w, ow</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_h, oh</code></p>
<dd> the output width and height, that is the size of the padded area as specified by the <i>width</i> and <i>height</i> expressions</p>
<dt><code>rotw(a)</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>roth(a)</code></p>
<dd> the minimal width/height required for completely containing the input video rotated by <i>a</i> radians. </p>
<p> These are only available when computing the <code>out_w</code> and <code>out_h</code> expressions.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Rotate the input by <font SIZE="-1">PI/6</font> radians clockwise: </p>
<pre>
        rotate=PI/6
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Rotate the input by <font SIZE="-1">PI/6</font> radians counter-clockwise: </p>
<pre>
        rotate=-PI/6
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Rotate the input by 45 degrees clockwise: </p>
<pre>
        rotate=45*PI/180
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply a constant rotation with period T, starting from an angle of <font SIZE="-1">PI/3:</font> </p>
<pre>
        rotate=PI/3+2*PI*t/T
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Make the input video rotation oscillating with a period of T seconds and an amplitude of A radians: </p>
<pre>
        rotate=A*sin(2*PI/T*t)
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Rotate the video, output size is chosen so that the whole rotating input video is always completely contained in the output: </p>
<pre>
        rotate='2*PI*t:ow=hypot(iw,ih):oh=ow'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Rotate the video, reduce the output size so that no background is ever shown: </p>
<pre>
        rotate=2*PI*t:ow='min(iw,ih)/<a href="//www.systutorials.com/docs/linux/man/2-sqrt/">sqrt</a>(2)':oh=ow:c=none
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> The filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>a, angle</code></p>
<dd> Set the angle expression. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</dl>
<h3>sab</h3>
<p> Apply Shape Adaptive Blur. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>luma_radius, lr</code></p>
<dd> Set luma blur filter strength, must be a value in range 0.1-4.0, default value is 1.0. A greater value will result in a more blurred image, and in slower processing.</p>
<dt><code>luma_pre_filter_radius, lpfr</code></p>
<dd> Set luma pre-filter radius, must be a value in the 0.1-2.0 range, default value is 1.0.</p>
<dt><code>luma_strength, ls</code></p>
<dd> Set luma maximum difference between pixels to still be considered, must be a value in the 0.1-100.0 range, default value is 1.0.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_radius, cr</code></p>
<dd> Set chroma blur filter strength, must be a value in range -0.9-4.0. A greater value will result in a more blurred image, and in slower processing.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_pre_filter_radius, cpfr</code></p>
<dd> Set chroma pre-filter radius, must be a value in the -0.9-2.0 range.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_strength, cs</code></p>
<dd> Set chroma maximum difference between pixels to still be considered, must be a value in the -0.9-100.0 range.</dl>
<p> Each chroma option value, if not explicitly specified, is set to the corresponding luma option value.</p>
<h3>scale</h3>
<p> Scale (resize) the input video, using the libswscale library. </p>
<p> The scale filter forces the output display aspect ratio to be the same of the input, by changing the output sample aspect ratio. </p>
<p> If the input image format is different from the format requested by the next filter, the scale filter will convert the input to the requested format. </p>
<p> <i>Options</i> </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options, or any of the options supported by the libswscale scaler. </p>
<p> See <code>the ffmpeg-scaler manual</code> for the complete list of scaler options.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>height, h</code></p>
<dd> Set the output video dimension expression. Default value is the input dimension. </p>
<p> If the <i>width</i> or <i>w</i> value is 0, the input width is used for the output. If the <i>height</i> or <i>h</i> value is 0, the input height is used for the output. </p>
<p> If one and only one of the values is -n with n &gt;= 1, the scale filter will use a value that maintains the aspect ratio of the input image, calculated from the other specified dimension. After that it will, however, make sure that the calculated dimension is divisible by n and adjust the value if necessary. </p>
<p> If both values are -n with n &gt;= 1, the behavior will be identical to both values being set to 0 as previously detailed. </p>
<p> See below for the list of accepted constants for use in the dimension expression.</p>
<dt><code>eval</code></p>
<dd> Specify when to evaluate <i>width</i> and <i>height</i> expression. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>init</code></p>
<dd> Only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization or when a command is processed.</p>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Evaluate expressions for each incoming frame.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>init</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>interl</code></p>
<dd> Set the interlacing mode. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>1</code></p>
<dd> Force interlaced aware scaling.</p>
<dt><code>0</code></p>
<dd> Do not apply interlaced scaling.</p>
<dt><code>-1</code></p>
<dd> Select interlaced aware scaling depending on whether the source frames are flagged as interlaced or not.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>0</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>flags</code></p>
<dd> Set libswscale scaling flags. See <code>the ffmpeg-scaler manual</code> for the complete list of values. If not explicitly specified the filter applies the default flags.</p>
<dt><code>param0, param1</code></p>
<dd> Set libswscale input parameters for scaling algorithms that need them. See <code>the ffmpeg-scaler manual</code> for the complete documentation. If not explicitly specified the filter applies empty parameters.</p>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Set the video size. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>.</p>
<dt><code>in_color_matrix</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_color_matrix</code></p>
<dd> Set in/output YCbCr color space type. </p>
<p> This allows the autodetected value to be overridden as well as allows forcing a specific value used for the output and encoder. </p>
<p> If not specified, the color space type depends on the pixel format. </p>
<p> Possible values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Choose automatically.</p>
<dt><code>bt709</code></p>
<dd> Format conforming to International Telecommunication Union (<font SIZE="-1">ITU</font>) Recommendation <font SIZE="-1">BT.709.</font></p>
<dt><code>fcc</code></p>
<dd> Set color space conforming to the United States Federal Communications Commission (<font SIZE="-1">FCC</font>) Code of Federal Regulations (<font SIZE="-1">CFR</font>) Title 47 (2003) 73.682 (a).</p>
<dt><code>bt601</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt470</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte170m</code></p>
<dd> Set color space conforming to:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">ITU</font> Radiocommunication Sector (ITU-R) Recommendation <font SIZE="-1">BT.601</font></p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> ITU-R Rec. <font SIZE="-1">BT.470-6</font> (1998) Systems B, B1, and G</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Society of Motion Picture and Television Engineers (<font SIZE="-1">SMPTE</font>) <font SIZE="-1">ST 170:2004</font></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>smpte240m</code></p>
<dd> Set color space conforming to <font SIZE="-1">SMPTE ST 240:1999.</font></p>
<dt><code>bt2020</code></p>
<dd> Set color space conforming to ITU-R <font SIZE="-1">BT.2020</font> non-constant luminance system.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>in_range</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_range</code></p>
<dd> Set in/output YCbCr sample range. </p>
<p> This allows the autodetected value to be overridden as well as allows forcing a specific value used for the output and encoder. If not specified, the range depends on the pixel format. Possible values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto/unknown</code></p>
<dd> Choose automatically.</p>
<dt><code>jpeg/full/pc</code></p>
<dd> Set full range (0-255 in case of 8-bit luma).</p>
<dt><code>mpeg/limited/tv</code></p>
<dd> Set &#8220;<font SIZE="-1">MPEG&#8221;</font> range (16-235 in case of 8-bit luma).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code></p>
<dd> Enable decreasing or increasing output video width or height if necessary to keep the original aspect ratio. Possible values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>disable</code></p>
<dd> Scale the video as specified and disable this feature.</p>
<dt><code>decrease</code></p>
<dd> The output video dimensions will automatically be decreased if needed.</p>
<dt><code>increase</code></p>
<dd> The output video dimensions will automatically be increased if needed.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> One useful instance of this option is that when you know a specific device&#8217;s maximum allowed resolution, you can use this to limit the output video to that, while retaining the aspect ratio. For example, device A allows 1280&#215;720 playback, and your video is 1920&#215;800. Using this option (set it to decrease) and specifying 1280&#215;720 to the command line makes the output 1280&#215;533. </p>
<p> Please note that this is a different thing than specifying -1 for <code>w</code> or <code>h</code>, you still need to specify the output resolution for this option to work.</dl>
<dt><code>force_divisible_by</code></p>
<dd> Ensures that both the output dimensions, width and height, are divisible by the given integer when used together with <code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code>. This works similar to using <tt>&quot;-n&quot;</tt> in the <code>w</code> and <code>h</code> options. </p>
<p> This option respects the value set for <code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code>, increasing or decreasing the resolution accordingly. The video&#8217;s aspect ratio may be slightly modified. </p>
<p> This option can be handy if you need to have a video fit within or exceed a defined resolution using <code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code> but also have encoder restrictions on width or height divisibility.</dl>
<p> The values of the <code>w</code> and <code>h</code> options are expressions containing the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><i>in_w</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>in_h</i></p>
<dd> The input width and height</p>
<dt><i>iw</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>ih</i></p>
<dd> These are the same as <i>in_w</i> and <i>in_h</i>.</p>
<dt><i>out_w</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>out_h</i></p>
<dd> The output (scaled) width and height</p>
<dt><i>ow</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>oh</i></p>
<dd> These are the same as <i>out_w</i> and <i>out_h</i></p>
<dt><i>a</i></p>
<dd> The same as <i>iw</i> / <i>ih</i></p>
<dt><i>sar</i></p>
<dd> input sample aspect ratio</p>
<dt><i>dar</i></p>
<dd> The input display aspect ratio. Calculated from <tt>&quot;(iw / ih) * sar&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><i>hsub</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>vsub</i></p>
<dd> horizontal and vertical input chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><i>ohsub</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>ovsub</i></p>
<dd> horizontal and vertical output chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><i>n</i></p>
<dd> The (sequential) number of the input frame, starting from 0. Only available with <tt>&quot;eval=frame&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><i>t</i></p>
<dd> The presentation timestamp of the input frame, expressed as a number of seconds. Only available with <tt>&quot;eval=frame&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><i>pos</i></p>
<dd> The position (byte offset) of the frame in the input stream, or NaN if this information is unavailable and/or meaningless (for example in case of synthetic video). Only available with <tt>&quot;eval=frame&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Scale the input video to a size of 200&#215;100 </p>
<pre>
        scale=w=200:h=100
</pre>
<p> This is equivalent to: </p>
<pre>
        scale=200:100
</pre>
<p> or: </p>
<pre>
        scale=200x100
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Specify a size abbreviation for the output size: </p>
<pre>
        scale=qcif
</pre>
<p> which can also be written as: </p>
<pre>
        scale=size=qcif
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Scale the input to 2x: </p>
<pre>
        scale=w=2*iw:h=2*ih
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> The above is the same as: </p>
<pre>
        scale=2*in_w:2*in_h
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Scale the input to 2x with forced interlaced scaling: </p>
<pre>
        scale=2*iw:2*ih:interl=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Scale the input to half size: </p>
<pre>
        scale=w=iw/2:h=ih/2
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Increase the width, and set the height to the same size: </p>
<pre>
        scale=3/2*iw:ow
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Seek Greek harmony: </p>
<pre>
        scale=iw:1/PHI*iw
        scale=ih*PHI:ih
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Increase the height, and set the width to 3/2 of the height: </p>
<pre>
        scale=w=3/2*oh:h=3/5*ih
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Increase the size, making the size a multiple of the chroma subsample values: </p>
<pre>
        scale=&quot;trunc(3/2*iw/hsub)*hsub:trunc(3/2*ih/vsub)*vsub&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Increase the width to a maximum of 500 pixels, keeping the same aspect ratio as the input: </p>
<pre>
        scale=w='min(500\, iw*3/2):h=-1'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Make pixels square by combining scale and setsar: </p>
<pre>
        scale='trunc(ih*dar):ih',setsar=1/1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Make pixels square by combining scale and setsar, making sure the resulting resolution is even (required by some codecs): </p>
<pre>
        scale='trunc(ih*dar/2)*2:trunc(ih/2)*2',setsar=1/1
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>height, h</code></p>
<dd> Set the output video dimension expression. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</dl>
<h3>scale_npp</h3>
<p> Use the <font SIZE="-1">NVIDIA</font> Performance Primitives (libnpp) to perform scaling and/or pixel format conversion on <font SIZE="-1">CUDA</font> video frames. Setting the output width and height works in the same way as for the <i>scale</i> filter. </p>
<p> The following additional options are accepted:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> The pixel format of the output <font SIZE="-1">CUDA</font> frames. If set to the string &#8220;same&#8221; (the default), the input format will be kept. Note that automatic format negotiation and conversion is not yet supported for hardware frames</p>
<dt><code>interp_algo</code></p>
<dd> The interpolation algorithm used for resizing. One of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>nn</code></p>
<dd> Nearest neighbour.</p>
<dt><code>linear</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cubic</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cubic2p_bspline</code></p>
<dd> 2-parameter cubic (B=1, C=0)</p>
<dt><code>cubic2p_catmullrom</code></p>
<dd> 2-parameter cubic (B=0, C=1/2)</p>
<dt><code>cubic2p_b05c03</code></p>
<dd> 2-parameter cubic (B=1/2, C=3/10)</p>
<dt><code>super</code></p>
<dd> Supersampling</p>
<dt><code>lanczos</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code></p>
<dd> Enable decreasing or increasing output video width or height if necessary to keep the original aspect ratio. Possible values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>disable</code></p>
<dd> Scale the video as specified and disable this feature.</p>
<dt><code>decrease</code></p>
<dd> The output video dimensions will automatically be decreased if needed.</p>
<dt><code>increase</code></p>
<dd> The output video dimensions will automatically be increased if needed.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> One useful instance of this option is that when you know a specific device&#8217;s maximum allowed resolution, you can use this to limit the output video to that, while retaining the aspect ratio. For example, device A allows 1280&#215;720 playback, and your video is 1920&#215;800. Using this option (set it to decrease) and specifying 1280&#215;720 to the command line makes the output 1280&#215;533. </p>
<p> Please note that this is a different thing than specifying -1 for <code>w</code> or <code>h</code>, you still need to specify the output resolution for this option to work.</dl>
<dt><code>force_divisible_by</code></p>
<dd> Ensures that both the output dimensions, width and height, are divisible by the given integer when used together with <code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code>. This works similar to using <tt>&quot;-n&quot;</tt> in the <code>w</code> and <code>h</code> options. </p>
<p> This option respects the value set for <code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code>, increasing or decreasing the resolution accordingly. The video&#8217;s aspect ratio may be slightly modified. </p>
<p> This option can be handy if you need to have a video fit within or exceed a defined resolution using <code>force_original_aspect_ratio</code> but also have encoder restrictions on width or height divisibility.</dl>
<h3>scale2ref</h3>
<p> Scale (resize) the input video, based on a reference video. </p>
<p> See the scale filter for available options, scale2ref supports the same but uses the reference video instead of the main input as basis. scale2ref also supports the following additional constants for the <code>w</code> and <code>h</code> options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><i>main_w</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>main_h</i></p>
<dd> The main input video&#8217;s width and height</p>
<dt><i>main_a</i></p>
<dd> The same as <i>main_w</i> / <i>main_h</i></p>
<dt><i>main_sar</i></p>
<dd> The main input video&#8217;s sample aspect ratio</p>
<dt><i>main_dar, mdar</i></p>
<dd> The main input video&#8217;s display aspect ratio. Calculated from <tt>&quot;(main_w / main_h) * main_sar&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><i>main_hsub</i></p>
<dd>
<dt><i>main_vsub</i></p>
<dd> The main input video&#8217;s horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</p>
<dt><i>main_n</i></p>
<dd> The (sequential) number of the main input frame, starting from 0. Only available with <tt>&quot;eval=frame&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><i>main_t</i></p>
<dd> The presentation timestamp of the main input frame, expressed as a number of seconds. Only available with <tt>&quot;eval=frame&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><i>main_pos</i></p>
<dd> The position (byte offset) of the frame in the main input stream, or NaN if this information is unavailable and/or meaningless (for example in case of synthetic video). Only available with <tt>&quot;eval=frame&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Scale a subtitle stream (b) to match the main video (a) in size before overlaying </p>
<p>&#8216;scale2ref[b][a];[a][b]overlay&#8217;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Scale a logo to 1/10th the height of a video, while preserving its display aspect ratio. </p>
<p>[logo &#8211; in] scale2ref = w = oh *mdar : h = ih / 10 [logo &#8211; out]
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>height, h</code></p>
<dd> Set the output video dimension expression. The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option. </p>
<p> If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current value.</dl>
<h3>scroll</h3>
<p> Scroll input video horizontally and/or vertically by constant speed. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>horizontal, h</code></p>
<dd> Set the horizontal scrolling speed. Default is 0. Allowed range is from -1 to 1. Negative values changes scrolling direction.</p>
<dt><code>vertical, v</code></p>
<dd> Set the vertical scrolling speed. Default is 0. Allowed range is from -1 to 1. Negative values changes scrolling direction.</p>
<dt><code>hpos</code></p>
<dd> Set the initial horizontal scrolling position. Default is 0. Allowed range is from 0 to 1.</p>
<dt><code>vpos</code></p>
<dd> Set the initial vertical scrolling position. Default is 0. Allowed range is from 0 to 1.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following <code>commands</code>:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>horizontal, h</code></p>
<dd> Set the horizontal scrolling speed.</p>
<dt><code>vertical, v</code></p>
<dd> Set the vertical scrolling speed.</dl>
<h3>scdet</h3>
<p> Detect video scene change. </p>
<p> This filter sets frame metadata with mafd between frame, the scene score, and forward the frame to the next filter, so they can use these metadata to detect scene change or others. </p>
<p> In addition, this filter logs a message and sets frame metadata when it detects a scene change by <code>threshold</code>. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;lavfi.scd.mafd&quot;</tt> metadata keys are set with mafd for every frame. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;lavfi.scd.score&quot;</tt> metadata keys are set with scene change score for every frame to detect scene change. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;lavfi.scd.time&quot;</tt> metadata keys are set with current filtered frame time which detect scene change with <code>threshold</code>. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold, t</code></p>
<dd> Set the scene change detection threshold as a percentage of maximum change. Good values are in the <tt>&quot;[8.0, 14.0]&quot;</tt> range. The range for <code>threshold</code> is <tt>&quot;[0., 100.]&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> Default value is <tt>10.</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>sc_pass, s</code></p>
<dd> Set the flag to pass scene change frames to the next filter. Default value is <tt>0</tt> You can enable it if you want to get snapshot of scene change frames only.</dl>
<h3>selectivecolor</h3>
<p> Adjust cyan, magenta, yellow and black (<font SIZE="-1">CMYK</font>) to certain ranges of colors (such as &#8220;reds&#8221;, &#8220;yellows&#8221;, &#8220;greens&#8221;, &#8220;cyans&#8221;, &#8230;). The adjustment range is defined by the &#8220;purity&#8221; of the color (that is, how saturated it already is). </p>
<p> This filter is similar to the Adobe Photoshop Selective Color tool. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>correction_method</code></p>
<dd> Select color correction method. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>absolute</code></p>
<dd> Specified adjustments are applied &#8220;as-is&#8221; (added/subtracted to original pixel component value).</p>
<dt><code>relative</code></p>
<dd> Specified adjustments are relative to the original component value.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;absolute&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>reds</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for red pixels (pixels where the red component is the maximum)</p>
<dt><code>yellows</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for yellow pixels (pixels where the blue component is the minimum)</p>
<dt><code>greens</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for green pixels (pixels where the green component is the maximum)</p>
<dt><code>cyans</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for cyan pixels (pixels where the red component is the minimum)</p>
<dt><code>blues</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for blue pixels (pixels where the blue component is the maximum)</p>
<dt><code>magentas</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for magenta pixels (pixels where the green component is the minimum)</p>
<dt><code>whites</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for white pixels (pixels where all components are greater than 128)</p>
<dt><code>neutrals</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for all pixels except pure black and pure white</p>
<dt><code>blacks</code></p>
<dd> Adjustments for black pixels (pixels where all components are lesser than 128)</p>
<dt><code>psfile</code></p>
<dd> Specify a Photoshop selective color file (<tt>&quot;.asv&quot;</tt>) to import the settings from.</dl>
<p> All the adjustment settings (<code>reds</code>, <code>yellows</code>, &#8230;) accept up to 4 space separated floating point adjustment values in the [-1,1] range, respectively to adjust the amount of cyan, magenta, yellow and black for the pixels of its range. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Increase cyan by 50% and reduce yellow by 33% in every green areas, and increase magenta by 27% in blue areas: </p>
<pre>
        selectivecolor=greens=.5 0 -.33 0:blues=0 .27
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Use a Photoshop selective color preset: </p>
<pre>
        selectivecolor=psfile=MySelectiveColorPresets/Misty.asv
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>separatefields</h3>
<p> The <tt>&quot;separatefields&quot;</tt> takes a frame-based video input and splits each frame into its components fields, producing a new half height clip with twice the frame rate and twice the frame count. </p>
<p> This filter use field-dominance information in frame to decide which of each pair of fields to place first in the output. If it gets it wrong use <code>setfield</code> filter before <tt>&quot;separatefields&quot;</tt> filter.</p>
<h3>setdar, setsar</h3>
<p> The <tt>&quot;setdar&quot;</tt> filter sets the Display Aspect Ratio for the filter output video. </p>
<p> This is done by changing the specified Sample (aka Pixel) Aspect Ratio, according to the following equation: </p>
<pre>
        &lt;DAR&gt; = &lt;HORIZONTAL_RESOLUTION&gt; / &lt;VERTICAL_RESOLUTION&gt; * &lt;SAR&gt;
</pre>
<p> Keep in mind that the <tt>&quot;setdar&quot;</tt> filter does not modify the pixel dimensions of the video frame. Also, the display aspect ratio set by this filter may be changed by later filters in the filterchain, e.g. in case of scaling or if another &#8220;setdar&#8221; or a &#8220;setsar&#8221; filter is applied. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;setsar&quot;</tt> filter sets the Sample (aka Pixel) Aspect Ratio for the filter output video. </p>
<p> Note that as a consequence of the application of this filter, the output display aspect ratio will change according to the equation above. </p>
<p> Keep in mind that the sample aspect ratio set by the <tt>&quot;setsar&quot;</tt> filter may be changed by later filters in the filterchain, e.g. if another &#8220;setsar&#8221; or a &#8220;setdar&#8221; filter is applied. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>r, ratio, dar (</code>&quot;setdar&quot;<code> only), sar (</code>&quot;setsar&quot;<code> only)</code></p>
<dd> Set the aspect ratio used by the filter. </p>
<p> The parameter can be a floating point number string, an expression, or a string of the form <i>num</i>:<i>den</i>, where <i>num</i> and <i>den</i> are the numerator and denominator of the aspect ratio. If the parameter is not specified, it is assumed the value &#8220;0&#8221;. In case the form &quot;<i>num</i>:<i>den</i>&quot; is used, the <tt>&quot;:&quot;</tt> character should be escaped.</p>
<dt><code>max</code></p>
<dd> Set the maximum integer value to use for expressing numerator and denominator when reducing the expressed aspect ratio to a rational. Default value is <tt>100</tt>.</dl>
<p> The parameter <i>sar</i> is an expression containing the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>E, </code><font SIZE="-1"><code>PI, PHI</code></font></p>
<dd> These are approximated values for the mathematical constants e (Euler&#8217;s number), pi (Greek pi), and phi (the golden ratio).</p>
<dt><code>w, h</code></p>
<dd> The input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> These are the same as <i>w</i> / <i>h</i>.</p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> The input sample aspect ratio.</p>
<dt><code>dar</code></p>
<dd> The input display aspect ratio. It is the same as (<i>w</i> / <i>h</i>) * <i>sar</i>.</p>
<dt><code>hsub, vsub</code></p>
<dd> Horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example, for the pixel format &#8220;yuv422p&#8221; <i>hsub</i> is 2 and <i>vsub</i> is 1.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> To change the display aspect ratio to 16:9, specify one of the following: </p>
<pre>
        setdar=dar=1.77777
        setdar=dar=16/9
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> To change the sample aspect ratio to 10:11, specify: </p>
<pre>
        setsar=sar=10/11
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> To set a display aspect ratio of 16:9, and specify a maximum integer value of 1000 in the aspect ratio reduction, use the command: </p>
<pre>
        setdar=ratio=16/9:max=1000
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>setfield</h3>
<p> Force field for the output video frame. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;setfield&quot;</tt> filter marks the interlace type field for the output frames. It does not change the input frame, but only sets the corresponding property, which affects how the frame is treated by following filters (e.g. <tt>&quot;fieldorder&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;yadif&quot;</tt>). </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Keep the same field property.</p>
<dt><code>bff</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as bottom-field-first.</p>
<dt><code>tff</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as top-field-first.</p>
<dt><code>prog</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as progressive.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<h3>setparams</h3>
<p> Force frame parameter for the output video frame. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;setparams&quot;</tt> filter marks interlace and color range for the output frames. It does not change the input frame, but only sets the corresponding property, which affects how the frame is treated by filters/encoders.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>field_mode</code></p>
<dd> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Keep the same field property (default).</p>
<dt><code>bff</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as bottom-field-first.</p>
<dt><code>tff</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as top-field-first.</p>
<dt><code>prog</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as progressive.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>range</code></p>
<dd> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Keep the same color range property (default).</p>
<dt><code>unspecified, unknown</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as unspecified color range.</p>
<dt><code>limited, tv, mpeg</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as limited range.</p>
<dt><code>full, pc, jpeg</code></p>
<dd> Mark the frame as full range.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>color_primaries</code></p>
<dd> Set the color primaries. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Keep the same color primaries property (default).</p>
<dt><code>bt709</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>unknown</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt470m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt470bg</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte170m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte240m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>film</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt2020</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte428</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte431</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte432</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>jedec-p22</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>color_trc</code></p>
<dd> Set the color transfer. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Keep the same color trc property (default).</p>
<dt><code>bt709</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>unknown</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt470m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt470bg</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte170m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte240m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>linear</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>log100</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>log316</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iec61966-2-4</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt1361e</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iec61966-2-1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt2020-10</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt2020-12</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte2084</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte428</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>arib-std-b67</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>colorspace</code></p>
<dd> Set the colorspace. Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Keep the same colorspace property (default).</p>
<dt><code>gbr</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt709</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>unknown</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fcc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt470bg</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte170m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte240m</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>ycgco</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt2020nc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bt2020c</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>smpte2085</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>chroma-derived-nc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>chroma-derived-c</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>ictcp</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<h3>shear</h3>
<p> Apply shear transform to input video. </p>
<p> This filter supports the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>shx</code></p>
<dd> Shear factor in X-direction. Default value is 0. Allowed range is from -2 to 2.</p>
<dt><code>shy</code></p>
<dd> Shear factor in Y-direction. Default value is 0. Allowed range is from -2 to 2.</p>
<dt><code>fillcolor, c</code></p>
<dd> Set the color used to fill the output area not covered by the transformed video. For the general syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. If the special value &#8220;none&#8221; is selected then no background is printed (useful for example if the background is never shown). </p>
<p> Default value is &#8220;black&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>interp</code></p>
<dd> Set interpolation type. Can be <tt>&quot;bilinear&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;nearest&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;bilinear&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>showinfo</h3>
<p> Show a line containing various information for each input video frame. The input video is not modified. </p>
<p> This filter supports the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>checksum</code></p>
<dd> Calculate checksums of each plane. By default enabled.</dl>
<p> The shown line contains a sequence of key/value pairs of the form <i>key</i>:<i>value</i>. </p>
<p> The following values are shown in the output:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> The (sequential) number of the input frame, starting from 0.</p>
<dt><code>pts</code></p>
<dd> The Presentation TimeStamp of the input frame, expressed as a number of time base units. The time base unit depends on the filter input pad.</p>
<dt><code>pts_time</code></p>
<dd> The Presentation TimeStamp of the input frame, expressed as a number of seconds.</p>
<dt><code>pos</code></p>
<dd> The position of the frame in the input stream, or -1 if this information is unavailable and/or meaningless (for example in case of synthetic video).</p>
<dt><code>fmt</code></p>
<dd> The pixel format name.</p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> The sample aspect ratio of the input frame, expressed in the form <i>num</i>/<i>den</i>.</p>
<dt><code>s</code></p>
<dd> The size of the input frame. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>.</p>
<dt><code>i</code></p>
<dd> The type of interlaced mode (&#8220;P&#8221; for &#8220;progressive&#8221;, &#8220;T&#8221; for top field first, &#8220;B&#8221; for bottom field first).</p>
<dt><code>iskey</code></p>
<dd> This is 1 if the frame is a key frame, 0 otherwise.</p>
<dt><code>type</code></p>
<dd> The picture type of the input frame (&#8220;I&#8221; for an I-frame, &#8220;P&#8221; for a P-frame, &#8220;B&#8221; for a B-frame, or &#8220;?&#8221; for an unknown type). Also refer to the documentation of the <tt>&quot;AVPictureType&quot;</tt> enum and of the <tt>&quot;av_get_picture_type_char&quot;</tt> function defined in <i>libavutil/avutil.h</i>.</p>
<dt><code>checksum</code></p>
<dd> The Adler-32 checksum (printed in hexadecimal) of all the planes of the input frame.</p>
<dt><code>plane_checksum</code></p>
<dd> The Adler-32 checksum (printed in hexadecimal) of each plane of the input frame, expressed in the form &quot;[<i>c0</i> <i>c1</i> <i>c2</i> <i>c3</i>]&quot;.</p>
<dt><code>mean</code></p>
<dd> The mean value of pixels in each plane of the input frame, expressed in the form &quot;[<i>mean0</i> <i>mean1</i> <i>mean2</i> <i>mean3</i>]&quot;.</p>
<dt><code>stdev</code></p>
<dd> The standard deviation of pixel values in each plane of the input frame, expressed in the form &quot;[<i>stdev0</i> <i>stdev1</i> <i>stdev2</i> <i>stdev3</i>]&quot;.</dl>
<h3>showpalette</h3>
<p> Displays the 256 colors palette of each frame. This filter is only relevant for <i>pal8</i> pixel format frames. </p>
<p> It accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>s</code></p>
<dd> Set the size of the box used to represent one palette color entry. Default is <tt>30</tt> (for a <tt>&quot;30x30&quot;</tt> pixel box).</dl>
<h3>shuffleframes</h3>
<p> Reorder and/or duplicate and/or drop video frames. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mapping</code></p>
<dd> Set the destination indexes of input frames. This is space or &#8216;|&#8217; separated list of indexes that maps input frames to output frames. Number of indexes also sets maximal value that each index may have. &#8216;-1&#8217; index have special meaning and that is to drop frame.</dl>
<p> The first frame has the index 0. The default is to keep the input unchanged. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Swap second and third frame of every three frames of the input: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf &quot;shuffleframes=0 2 1&quot; OUTPUT
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Swap 10th and 1st frame of every ten frames of the input: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf &quot;shuffleframes=9 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 0&quot; OUTPUT
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>shufflepixels</h3>
<p> Reorder pixels in video frames. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>direction, d</code></p>
<dd> Set shuffle direction. Can be forward or inverse direction. Default direction is forward.</p>
<dt><code>mode, m</code></p>
<dd> Set shuffle mode. Can be horizontal, vertical or block mode.</p>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>height, h</code></p>
<dd> Set shuffle block_size. In case of horizontal shuffle mode only width part of size is used, and in case of vertical shuffle mode only height part of size is used.</p>
<dt><code>seed, s</code></p>
<dd> Set random seed used with shuffling pixels. Mainly useful to set to be able to reverse filtering process to get original input. For example, to reverse forward shuffle you need to use same parameters and exact same seed and to set direction to inverse.</dl>
<h3>shuffleplanes</h3>
<p> Reorder and/or duplicate video planes. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>map0</code></p>
<dd> The index of the input plane to be used as the first output plane.</p>
<dt><code>map1</code></p>
<dd> The index of the input plane to be used as the second output plane.</p>
<dt><code>map2</code></p>
<dd> The index of the input plane to be used as the third output plane.</p>
<dt><code>map3</code></p>
<dd> The index of the input plane to be used as the fourth output plane.</dl>
<p> The first plane has the index 0. The default is to keep the input unchanged. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Swap the second and third planes of the input: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf shuffleplanes=0:2:1:3 OUTPUT
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>signalstats</h3>
<p> Evaluate various visual metrics that assist in determining issues associated with the digitization of analog video media. </p>
<p> By default the filter will log these metadata values:</p>
<dl>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>YMIN</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the minimal Y value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>YLOW</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the Y value at the 10% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>YAVG</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average Y value within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>YHIGH</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the Y value at the 90% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>YMAX</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the maximum Y value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>UMIN</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the minimal U value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>ULOW</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the U value at the 10% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>UAVG</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average U value within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>UHIGH</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the U value at the 90% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>UMAX</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the maximum U value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VMIN</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the minimal V value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VLOW</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the V value at the 10% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VAVG</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average V value within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VHIGH</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the V value at the 90% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VMAX</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the maximum V value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SATMIN</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the minimal saturation value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SATLOW</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the saturation value at the 10% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SATAVG</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average saturation value within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SATHIGH</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the saturation value at the 90% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SATMAX</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the maximum saturation value contained within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>HUEMED</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the median value for hue within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-360].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>HUEAVG</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average value for hue within the input frame. Expressed in range of [0-360].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>YDIF</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average of sample value difference between all values of the Y plane in the current frame and corresponding values of the previous input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>UDIF</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average of sample value difference between all values of the U plane in the current frame and corresponding values of the previous input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VDIF</code></font></p>
<dd> Display the average of sample value difference between all values of the V plane in the current frame and corresponding values of the previous input frame. Expressed in range of [0-255].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>YBITDEPTH</code></font></p>
<dd> Display bit depth of Y plane in current frame. Expressed in range of [0-16].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>UBITDEPTH</code></font></p>
<dd> Display bit depth of U plane in current frame. Expressed in range of [0-16].</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VBITDEPTH</code></font></p>
<dd> Display bit depth of V plane in current frame. Expressed in range of [0-16].</dl>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>stat</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out</code></p>
<dd> <code>stat</code> specify an additional form of image analysis. <code>out</code> output video with the specified type of pixel highlighted. </p>
<p> Both options accept the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>tout</code></p>
<dd> Identify <i>temporal outliers</i> pixels. A <i>temporal outlier</i> is a pixel unlike the neighboring pixels of the same field. Examples of temporal outliers include the results of video dropouts, head clogs, or tape tracking issues.</p>
<dt><code>vrep</code></p>
<dd> Identify <i>vertical line repetition</i>. Vertical line repetition includes similar rows of pixels within a frame. In born-digital video vertical line repetition is common, but this pattern is uncommon in video digitized from an analog source. When it occurs in video that results from the digitization of an analog source it can indicate concealment from a dropout compensator.</p>
<dt><code>brng</code></p>
<dd> Identify pixels that fall outside of legal broadcast range.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>color, c</code></p>
<dd> Set the highlight color for the <code>out</code> option. The default color is yellow.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Output data of various video metrics: </p>
<pre>
        ffprobe -f lavfi movie=example.mov,signalstats=&quot;stat=tout+vrep+brng&quot; -show_frames
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Output specific data about the minimum and maximum values of the Y plane per frame: </p>
<pre>
        ffprobe -f lavfi movie=example.mov,signalstats -show_entries frame_tags=lavfi.signalstats.YMAX,lavfi.signalstats.YMIN
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Playback video while highlighting pixels that are outside of broadcast range in red. </p>
<pre>
        ffplay example.mov -vf signalstats=&quot;out=brng:color=red&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Playback video with signalstats metadata drawn over the frame. </p>
<pre>
        ffplay example.mov -vf signalstats=stat=brng+vrep+tout,drawtext=fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:textfile=signalstat_drawtext.txt
</pre>
<p> The contents of signalstat_drawtext.txt used in the command are: </p>
<p>time % {pts : hms} Y(% {metadata : lavfi.signalstats.YMIN} &#8211; % {metadata : lavfi.signalstats.YMAX}) U(% {metadata : lavfi.signalstats.UMIN} &#8211; % {metadata : lavfi.signalstats.UMAX}) V(% {metadata : lavfi.signalstats.VMIN} &#8211; % {metadata : lavfi.signalstats.VMAX}) saturation maximum : %<br />
{<br />
metadata:<br />
    lavfi.signalstats.SATMAX<br />
}
</dl>
<h3>signature</h3>
<p> Calculates the <font SIZE="-1">MPEG-7</font> Video Signature. The filter can handle more than one input. In this case the matching between the inputs can be calculated additionally. The filter always passes through the first input. The signature of each stream can be written into a file. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>detectmode</code></p>
<dd> Enable or disable the matching process. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>off</code></p>
<dd> Disable the calculation of a matching (default).</p>
<dt><code>full</code></p>
<dd> Calculate the matching for the whole video and output whether the whole video matches or only parts.</p>
<dt><code>fast</code></p>
<dd> Calculate only until a matching is found or the video ends. Should be faster in some cases.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>nb_inputs</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of inputs. The option value must be a non negative integer. Default value is 1.</p>
<dt><code>filename</code></p>
<dd> Set the path to which the output is written. If there is more than one input, the path must be a prototype, i.e. must contain <tt>%d</tt> or <tt>%0nd</tt> (where n is a positive integer), that will be replaced with the input number. If no filename is specified, no output will be written. This is the default.</p>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> Choose the output format. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>binary</code></p>
<dd> Use the specified binary representation (default).</p>
<dt><code>xml</code></p>
<dd> Use the specified xml representation.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>th_d</code></p>
<dd> Set threshold to detect one word as similar. The option value must be an integer greater than zero. The default value is 9000.</p>
<dt><code>th_dc</code></p>
<dd> Set threshold to detect all words as similar. The option value must be an integer greater than zero. The default value is 60000.</p>
<dt><code>th_xh</code></p>
<dd> Set threshold to detect frames as similar. The option value must be an integer greater than zero. The default value is 116.</p>
<dt><code>th_di</code></p>
<dd> Set the minimum length of a sequence in frames to recognize it as matching sequence. The option value must be a non negative integer value. The default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>th_it</code></p>
<dd> Set the minimum relation, that matching frames to all frames must have. The option value must be a double value between 0 and 1. The default value is 0.5.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> To calculate the signature of an input video and store it in signature.bin: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.mkv -vf signature=filename=signature.bin -map 0:v -f null -
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> To detect whether two videos match and store the signatures in <font SIZE="-1">XML</font> format in signature0.xml and signature1.xml: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i input1.mkv &#8211; i input2.mkv &#8211; filter_complex &#8220;[0:v][1:v] signature=nb_inputs=2:detectmode=full:format=xml:filename=signature%d.xml&#8221; &#8211; map : v &#8211; f null &#8211;
</dl>
<h3>smartblur</h3>
<p> Blur the input video without impacting the outlines. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>luma_radius, lr</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma radius. The option value must be a float number in the range [0.1,5.0] that specifies the variance of the gaussian filter used to blur the image (slower if larger). Default value is 1.0.</p>
<dt><code>luma_strength, ls</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma strength. The option value must be a float number in the range [-1.0,1.0] that configures the blurring. A value included in [0.0,1.0] will blur the image whereas a value included in [-1.0,0.0] will sharpen the image. Default value is 1.0.</p>
<dt><code>luma_threshold, lt</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma threshold used as a coefficient to determine whether a pixel should be blurred or not. The option value must be an integer in the range [-30,30]. A value of 0 will filter all the image, a value included in [0,30] will filter flat areas and a value included in [-30,0] will filter edges. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_radius, cr</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma radius. The option value must be a float number in the range [0.1,5.0] that specifies the variance of the gaussian filter used to blur the image (slower if larger). Default value is <code>luma_radius</code>.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_strength, cs</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma strength. The option value must be a float number in the range [-1.0,1.0] that configures the blurring. A value included in [0.0,1.0] will blur the image whereas a value included in [-1.0,0.0] will sharpen the image. Default value is <code>luma_strength</code>.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_threshold, ct</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma threshold used as a coefficient to determine whether a pixel should be blurred or not. The option value must be an integer in the range [-30,30]. A value of 0 will filter all the image, a value included in [0,30] will filter flat areas and a value included in [-30,0] will filter edges. Default value is <code>luma_threshold</code>.</dl>
<p> If a chroma option is not explicitly set, the corresponding luma value is set.</p>
<h3>sobel</h3>
<p> Apply sobel operator to input video stream. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be multiplied with filtered result.</p>
<dt><code>delta</code></p>
<dd> Set value which will be added to filtered result.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>spp</h3>
<p> Apply a simple postprocessing filter that compresses and decompresses the image at several (or &#8211; in the case of <code>quality</code> level <tt>6</tt> &#8211; all) shifts and average the results. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>quality</code></p>
<dd> Set quality. This option defines the number of levels for averaging. It accepts an integer in the range 0-6. If set to <tt>0</tt>, the filter will have no effect. A value of <tt>6</tt> means the higher quality. For each increment of that value the speed drops by a factor of approximately 2. Default value is <tt>3</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>qp</code></p>
<dd> Force a constant quantization parameter. If not set, the filter will use the <font SIZE="-1">QP</font> from the video stream (if available).</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set thresholding mode. Available modes are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>hard</code></p>
<dd> Set hard thresholding (default).</p>
<dt><code>soft</code></p>
<dd> Set soft thresholding (better de-ringing effect, but likely blurrier).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>use_bframe_qp</code></p>
<dd> Enable the use of the <font SIZE="-1">QP</font> from the B-Frames if set to <tt>1</tt>. Using this option may cause flicker since the B-Frames have often larger <font SIZE="-1">QP.</font> Default is <tt>0</tt> (not enabled).</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>quality, level</code></p>
<dd> Set quality level. The value <tt>&quot;max&quot;</tt> can be used to set the maximum level, currently <tt>6</tt>.</dl>
<h3>sr</h3>
<p> Scale the input by applying one of the super-resolution methods based on convolutional neural networks. Supported models:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Super-Resolution Convolutional Neural Network model (<font SIZE="-1">SRCNN</font>). See &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.00092">https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.00092</a></code>&gt;.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Efficient Sub-Pixel Convolutional Neural Network model (<font SIZE="-1">ESPCN</font>). See &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1609.05158">https://arxiv.org/abs/1609.05158</a></code>&gt;.</dl>
<p> Training scripts as well as scripts for model file (.pb) saving can be found at &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://github.com/XueweiMeng/sr/tree/sr_dnn_native">https://github.com/XueweiMeng/sr/tree/sr_dnn_native</a></code>&gt;. Original repository is at &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://github.com/HighVoltageRocknRoll/sr.git">https://github.com/HighVoltageRocknRoll/sr.git</a></code>&gt;. </p>
<p> Native model files (.model) can be generated from TensorFlow model files (.pb) by using tools/python/convert.py </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dnn_backend</code></p>
<dd> Specify which <font SIZE="-1">DNN</font> backend to use for model loading and execution. This option accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>native</code></p>
<dd> Native implementation of <font SIZE="-1">DNN</font> loading and execution.</p>
<dt><code>tensorflow</code></p>
<dd> TensorFlow backend. To enable this backend you need to install the TensorFlow for C library (see &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="https://www.tensorflow.org/install/install_c">https://www.tensorflow.org/install/install_c</a></code>&gt;) and configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libtensorflow&quot;</tt></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>native</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>model</code></p>
<dd> Set path to model file specifying network architecture and its parameters. Note that different backends use different file formats. TensorFlow backend can load files for both formats, while native backend can load files for only its format.</p>
<dt><code>scale_factor</code></p>
<dd> Set scale factor for <font SIZE="-1">SRCNN</font> model. Allowed values are <tt>2</tt>, <tt>3</tt> and <tt>4</tt>. Default value is <tt>2</tt>. Scale factor is necessary for <font SIZE="-1">SRCNN</font> model, because it accepts input upscaled using bicubic upscaling with proper scale factor.</dl>
<p> This feature can also be finished with <code>dnn_processing</code> filter.</p>
<h3>ssim</h3>
<p> Obtain the <font SIZE="-1">SSIM</font> (Structural SImilarity Metric) between two input videos. </p>
<p> This filter takes in input two input videos, the first input is considered the &#8220;main&#8221; source and is passed unchanged to the output. The second input is used as a &#8220;reference&#8221; video for computing the <font SIZE="-1">SSIM.</font> </p>
<p> Both video inputs must have the same resolution and pixel format for this filter to work correctly. Also it assumes that both inputs have the same number of frames, which are compared one by one. </p>
<p> The filter stores the calculated <font SIZE="-1">SSIM</font> of each frame. </p>
<p> The description of the accepted parameters follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>stats_file, f</code></p>
<dd> If specified the filter will use the named file to save the <font SIZE="-1">SSIM</font> of each individual frame. When filename equals &#8220;-&#8221; the data is sent to standard output.</dl>
<p> The file printed if <i>stats_file</i> is selected, contains a sequence of key/value pairs of the form <i>key</i>:<i>value</i> for each compared couple of frames. </p>
<p> A description of each shown parameter follows:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> sequential number of the input frame, starting from 1</p>
<dt><code>Y, U, V, R, G, B</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">SSIM</font> of the compared frames for the component specified by the suffix.</p>
<dt><code>All</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">SSIM</font> of the compared frames for the whole frame.</p>
<dt><code>dB</code></p>
<dd> Same as above but in dB representation.</dl>
<p> This filter also supports the <code>framesync</code> options. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> For example: </p>
<p>movie = ref_movie.mpg, setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS[main];<br />
[main][ref] ssim = &#8220;stats_file=stats.log&#8221;[out]</p>
<p> On this example the input file being processed is compared with the reference file <i>ref_movie.mpg</i>. The <font SIZE="-1">SSIM</font> of each individual frame is stored in <i>stats.log</i>.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Another example with both psnr and ssim at same time: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i main.mpg &#8211; i ref.mpg &#8211; lavfi &#8220;ssim;[0:v][1:v]psnr&#8221; &#8211; f null &#8211;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Another example with different containers: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i main.mpg &#8211; i ref.mkv &#8211; lavfi &#8220;[0:v]settb=AVTB,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS[main];[1:v]settb=AVTB,setpts=PTS-STARTPTS[ref];[main][ref]ssim&#8221; &#8211; f null &#8211;
</dl>
<h3>stereo3d</h3>
<p> Convert between different stereoscopic image formats. </p>
<p> The filters accept the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>in</code></p>
<dd> Set stereoscopic image format of input. </p>
<p> Available values for input image formats are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>sbsl</code></p>
<dd> side by side parallel (left eye left, right eye right)</p>
<dt><code>sbsr</code></p>
<dd> side by side crosseye (right eye left, left eye right)</p>
<dt><code>sbs2l</code></p>
<dd> side by side parallel with half width resolution (left eye left, right eye right)</p>
<dt><code>sbs2r</code></p>
<dd> side by side crosseye with half width resolution (right eye left, left eye right)</p>
<dt><code>abl</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tbl</code></p>
<dd> above-below (left eye above, right eye below)</p>
<dt><code>abr</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tbr</code></p>
<dd> above-below (right eye above, left eye below)</p>
<dt><code>ab2l</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tb2l</code></p>
<dd> above-below with half height resolution (left eye above, right eye below)</p>
<dt><code>ab2r</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tb2r</code></p>
<dd> above-below with half height resolution (right eye above, left eye below)</p>
<dt><code>al</code></p>
<dd> alternating frames (left eye first, right eye second)</p>
<dt><code>ar</code></p>
<dd> alternating frames (right eye first, left eye second)</p>
<dt><code>irl</code></p>
<dd> interleaved rows (left eye has top row, right eye starts on next row)</p>
<dt><code>irr</code></p>
<dd> interleaved rows (right eye has top row, left eye starts on next row)</p>
<dt><code>icl</code></p>
<dd> interleaved columns, left eye first</p>
<dt><code>icr</code></p>
<dd> interleaved columns, right eye first </p>
<p> Default value is <code>sbsl</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>out</code></p>
<dd> Set stereoscopic image format of output.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>sbsl</code></p>
<dd> side by side parallel (left eye left, right eye right)</p>
<dt><code>sbsr</code></p>
<dd> side by side crosseye (right eye left, left eye right)</p>
<dt><code>sbs2l</code></p>
<dd> side by side parallel with half width resolution (left eye left, right eye right)</p>
<dt><code>sbs2r</code></p>
<dd> side by side crosseye with half width resolution (right eye left, left eye right)</p>
<dt><code>abl</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tbl</code></p>
<dd> above-below (left eye above, right eye below)</p>
<dt><code>abr</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tbr</code></p>
<dd> above-below (right eye above, left eye below)</p>
<dt><code>ab2l</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tb2l</code></p>
<dd> above-below with half height resolution (left eye above, right eye below)</p>
<dt><code>ab2r</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tb2r</code></p>
<dd> above-below with half height resolution (right eye above, left eye below)</p>
<dt><code>al</code></p>
<dd> alternating frames (left eye first, right eye second)</p>
<dt><code>ar</code></p>
<dd> alternating frames (right eye first, left eye second)</p>
<dt><code>irl</code></p>
<dd> interleaved rows (left eye has top row, right eye starts on next row)</p>
<dt><code>irr</code></p>
<dd> interleaved rows (right eye has top row, left eye starts on next row)</p>
<dt><code>arbg</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph red/blue gray (red filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>argg</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph red/green gray (red filter on left eye, green filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>arcg</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph red/cyan gray (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>arch</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph red/cyan half colored (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>arcc</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph red/cyan color (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>arcd</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph red/cyan color optimized with the least squares projection of dubois (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>agmg</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph green/magenta gray (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>agmh</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph green/magenta half colored (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>agmc</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph green/magenta colored (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>agmd</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph green/magenta color optimized with the least squares projection of dubois (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>aybg</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph yellow/blue gray (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>aybh</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph yellow/blue half colored (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>aybc</code></p>
<dd> anaglyph yellow/blue colored (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>aybd</code></p>
