<dd> anaglyph yellow/blue color optimized with the least squares projection of dubois (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)</p>
<dt><code>ml</code></p>
<dd> mono output (left eye only)</p>
<dt><code>mr</code></p>
<dd> mono output (right eye only)</p>
<dt><code>chl</code></p>
<dd> checkerboard, left eye first</p>
<dt><code>chr</code></p>
<dd> checkerboard, right eye first</p>
<dt><code>icl</code></p>
<dd> interleaved columns, left eye first</p>
<dt><code>icr</code></p>
<dd> interleaved columns, right eye first</p>
<dt><code>hdmi</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">HDMI</font> frame pack</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>arcd</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Convert input video from side by side parallel to anaglyph yellow/blue dubois: </p>
<pre>
        stereo3d=sbsl:aybd
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Convert input video from above below (left eye above, right eye below) to side by side crosseye. </p>
<pre>
        stereo3d=abl:sbsr
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>streamselect, astreamselect</h3>
<p> Select video or audio streams. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>inputs</code></p>
<dd> Set number of inputs. Default is 2.</p>
<dt><code>map</code></p>
<dd> Set input indexes to remap to outputs.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;streamselect&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;astreamselect&quot;</tt> filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>map</code></p>
<dd> Set input indexes to remap to outputs.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select first 5 seconds 1st stream and rest of time 2nd stream: </p>
<pre>
        sendcmd='5.0 streamselect map 1',streamselect=inputs=2:map=0
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Same as above, but for audio: </p>
<pre>
        asendcmd='5.0 astreamselect map 1',astreamselect=inputs=2:map=0
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>subtitles</h3>
<p> Draw subtitles on top of input video using the libass library. </p>
<p> To enable compilation of this filter you need to configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libass&quot;</tt>. This filter also requires a build with libavcodec and libavformat to convert the passed subtitles file to <font SIZE="-1">ASS</font> (Advanced Substation Alpha) subtitles format. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filename, f</code></p>
<dd> Set the filename of the subtitle file to read. It must be specified.</p>
<dt><code>original_size</code></p>
<dd> Specify the size of the original video, the video for which the <font SIZE="-1">ASS</font> file was composed. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Due to a misdesign in <font SIZE="-1">ASS</font> aspect ratio arithmetic, this is necessary to correctly scale the fonts if the aspect ratio has been changed.</p>
<dt><code>fontsdir</code></p>
<dd> Set a directory path containing fonts that can be used by the filter. These fonts will be used in addition to whatever the font provider uses.</p>
<dt><code>alpha</code></p>
<dd> Process alpha channel, by default alpha channel is untouched.</p>
<dt><code>charenc</code></p>
<dd> Set subtitles input character encoding. <tt>&quot;subtitles&quot;</tt> filter only. Only useful if not <font SIZE="-1">UTF-8.</font></p>
<dt><code>stream_index, si</code></p>
<dd> Set subtitles stream index. <tt>&quot;subtitles&quot;</tt> filter only.</p>
<dt><code>force_style</code></p>
<dd> Override default style or script info parameters of the subtitles. It accepts a string containing <font SIZE="-1">ASS</font> style format <tt>&quot;KEY=VALUE&quot;</tt> couples separated by &#8220;,&#8221;.</dl>
<p> If the first key is not specified, it is assumed that the first value specifies the <code>filename</code>. </p>
<p> For example, to render the file <i>sub.srt</i> on top of the input video, use the command: </p>
<pre>
        subtitles=sub.srt
</pre>
<p> which is equivalent to: </p>
<pre>
        subtitles=filename=sub.srt
</pre>
<p> To render the default subtitles stream from file <i>video.mkv</i>, use: </p>
<pre>
        subtitles=video.mkv
</pre>
<p> To render the second subtitles stream from that file, use: </p>
<pre>
        subtitles=video.mkv:si=1
</pre>
<p> To make the subtitles stream from <i>sub.srt</i> appear in 80% transparent blue <tt>&quot;DejaVu Serif&quot;</tt>, use: </p>
<pre>
        subtitles=sub.srt:force_style='Fontname=DejaVu Serif,PrimaryColour=&amp;HCCFF0000'
</pre>
<h3>super2xsai</h3>
<p> Scale the input by 2x and smooth using the Super2xSaI (Scale and Interpolate) pixel art scaling algorithm. </p>
<p> Useful for enlarging pixel art images without reducing sharpness.</p>
<h3>swaprect</h3>
<p> Swap two rectangular objects in video. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd> Set object width.</p>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> Set object height.</p>
<dt><code>x1</code></p>
<dd> Set 1st rect x coordinate.</p>
<dt><code>y1</code></p>
<dd> Set 1st rect y coordinate.</p>
<dt><code>x2</code></p>
<dd> Set 2nd rect x coordinate.</p>
<dt><code>y2</code></p>
<dd> Set 2nd rect y coordinate. </p>
<p> All expressions are evaluated once for each frame.</dl>
<p> The all options are expressions containing the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> The input width and height.</p>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> same as <i>w</i> / <i>h</i></p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> input sample aspect ratio</p>
<dt><code>dar</code></p>
<dd> input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (<i>w</i> / <i>h</i>) * <i>sar</i></p>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> The number of the input frame, starting from 0.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> The timestamp expressed in seconds. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input timestamp is unknown.</p>
<dt><code>pos</code></p>
<dd> the position in the file of the input frame, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if unknown</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>swapuv</h3>
<p> Swap U &amp; V plane.</p>
<h3>tblend</h3>
<p> Blend successive video frames. </p>
<p> See <code>blend</code></p>
<h3>telecine</h3>
<p> Apply telecine process to the video. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>first_field</code></p>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>top, t</code></p>
<dd> top field first</p>
<dt><code>bottom, b</code></p>
<dd> bottom field first The default value is <tt>&quot;top&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>pattern</code></p>
<dd> A string of numbers representing the pulldown pattern you wish to apply. The default value is <tt>23</tt>.</dl>
<pre>
        Some typical patterns:
        
        NTSC output (30i):
        27.5p: 32222
        24p: 23 (classic)
        24p: 2332 (preferred)
        20p: 33
        18p: 334
        16p: 3444
        
        PAL output (25i):
        27.5p: 12222
        24p: 222222222223 (&quot;Euro pulldown&quot;)
        16.67p: 33
        16p: 33333334
</pre>
<h3>thistogram</h3>
<p> Compute and draw a color distribution histogram for the input video across time. </p>
<p> Unlike <code>histogram</code> video filter which only shows histogram of single input frame at certain time, this filter shows also past histograms of number of frames defined by <tt>&quot;width&quot;</tt> option. </p>
<p> The computed histogram is a representation of the color component distribution in an image. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>width, w</code></p>
<dd> Set width of single color component output. Default value is <tt>0</tt>. Value of <tt>0</tt> means width will be picked from input video. This also set number of passed histograms to keep. Allowed range is [0, 8192].</p>
<dt><code>display_mode, d</code></p>
<dd> Set display mode. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>stack</code></p>
<dd> Per color component graphs are placed below each other.</p>
<dt><code>parade</code></p>
<dd> Per color component graphs are placed side by side.</p>
<dt><code>overlay</code></p>
<dd> Presents information identical to that in the <tt>&quot;parade&quot;</tt>, except that the graphs representing color components are superimposed directly over one another.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;stack&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>levels_mode, m</code></p>
<dd> Set mode. Can be either <tt>&quot;linear&quot;</tt>, or <tt>&quot;logarithmic&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;linear&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>components, c</code></p>
<dd> Set what color components to display. Default is <tt>7</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>bgopacity, b</code></p>
<dd> Set background opacity. Default is <tt>0.9</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>envelope, e</code></p>
<dd> Show envelope. Default is disabled.</p>
<dt><code>ecolor, ec</code></p>
<dd> Set envelope color. Default is <tt>&quot;gold&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>slide</code></p>
<dd> Set slide mode. </p>
<p> Available values for slide is:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Draw new frame when right border is reached.</p>
<dt><code>replace</code></p>
<dd> Replace old columns with new ones.</p>
<dt><code>scroll</code></p>
<dd> Scroll from right to left.</p>
<dt><code>rscroll</code></p>
<dd> Scroll from left to right.</p>
<dt><code>picture</code></p>
<dd> Draw single picture.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;replace&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<h3>threshold</h3>
<p> Apply threshold effect to video stream. </p>
<p> This filter needs four video streams to perform thresholding. First stream is stream we are filtering. Second stream is holding threshold values, third stream is holding min values, and last, fourth stream is holding max values. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.</dl>
<p> For example if first stream pixel&#8217;s component value is less then threshold value of pixel component from 2nd threshold stream, third stream value will picked, otherwise fourth stream pixel component value will be picked. </p>
<p> Using color source filter one can perform various types of thresholding: </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Binary threshold, using gray color as threshold: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -f lavfi -i color=black -f lavfi -i color=white -lavfi threshold output.avi
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Inverted binary threshold, using gray color as threshold: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -f lavfi -i color=white -f lavfi -i color=black -lavfi threshold output.avi
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Truncate binary threshold, using gray color as threshold: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -lavfi threshold output.avi
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Threshold to zero, using gray color as threshold: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -f lavfi -i color=white -i 320x240.avi -lavfi threshold output.avi
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Inverted threshold to zero, using gray color as threshold: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=white -lavfi threshold output.avi
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>thumbnail</h3>
<p> Select the most representative frame in a given sequence of consecutive frames. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> Set the frames batch size to analyze; in a set of <i>n</i> frames, the filter will pick one of them, and then handle the next batch of <i>n</i> frames until the end. Default is <tt>100</tt>.</dl>
<p> Since the filter keeps track of the whole frames sequence, a bigger <i>n</i> value will result in a higher memory usage, so a high value is not recommended. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Extract one picture each 50 frames: </p>
<pre>
        thumbnail=50
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Complete example of a thumbnail creation with <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i in.avi -vf thumbnail,scale=300:200 -frames:v 1 out.png
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>tile</h3>
<p> Tile several successive frames together. </p>
<p> The <code>untile</code> filter can do the reverse. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>layout</code></p>
<dd> Set the grid size (i.e. the number of lines and columns). For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>.</p>
<dt><code>nb_frames</code></p>
<dd> Set the maximum number of frames to render in the given area. It must be less than or equal to <i>w</i>x<i>h</i>. The default value is <tt>0</tt>, meaning all the area will be used.</p>
<dt><code>margin</code></p>
<dd> Set the outer border margin in pixels.</p>
<dt><code>padding</code></p>
<dd> Set the inner border thickness (i.e. the number of pixels between frames). For more advanced padding options (such as having different values for the edges), refer to the pad video filter.</p>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the unused area. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. The default value of <i>color</i> is &#8220;black&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>overlap</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of frames to overlap when tiling several successive frames together. The value must be between <tt>0</tt> and <i>nb_frames &#8211; 1</i>.</p>
<dt><code>init_padding</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of frames to initially be empty before displaying first output frame. This controls how soon will one get first output frame. The value must be between <tt>0</tt> and <i>nb_frames &#8211; 1</i>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Produce 8&#215;8 <font SIZE="-1">PNG</font> tiles of all keyframes (<code>-skip_frame nokey</code>) in a movie: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -skip_frame nokey -i file.avi -vf 'scale=128:72,tile=8x8' -an -vsync 0 keyframes%03d.png
</pre>
<p> The <code>-vsync 0</code> is necessary to prevent <code>ffmpeg</code> from duplicating each output frame to accommodate the originally detected frame rate.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Display <tt>5</tt> pictures in an area of <tt>&quot;3x2&quot;</tt> frames, with <tt>7</tt> pixels between them, and <tt>2</tt> pixels of initial margin, using mixed flat and named options: </p>
<pre>
        tile=3x2:nb_frames=5:padding=7:margin=2
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>tinterlace</h3>
<p> Perform various types of temporal field interlacing. </p>
<p> Frames are counted starting from 1, so the first input frame is considered odd. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Specify the mode of the interlacing. This option can also be specified as a value alone. See below for a list of values for this option. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>merge, 0</code></p>
<dd> Move odd frames into the upper field, even into the lower field, generating a double height frame at half frame rate. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        
        Output:
        11111                           33333
        22222                           44444
        11111                           33333
        22222                           44444
        11111                           33333
        22222                           44444
        11111                           33333
        22222                           44444
</pre>
<dt><code>drop_even, 1</code></p>
<dd> Only output odd frames, even frames are dropped, generating a frame with unchanged height at half frame rate. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        
        Output:
        11111                           33333
        11111                           33333
        11111                           33333
        11111                           33333
</pre>
<dt><code>drop_odd, 2</code></p>
<dd> Only output even frames, odd frames are dropped, generating a frame with unchanged height at half frame rate. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        
        Output:
                        22222                           44444
                        22222                           44444
                        22222                           44444
                        22222                           44444
</pre>
<dt><code>pad, 3</code></p>
<dd> Expand each frame to full height, but pad alternate lines with black, generating a frame with double height at the same input frame rate. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        
        Output:
        11111           .....           33333           .....
        .....           22222           .....           44444
        11111           .....           33333           .....
        .....           22222           .....           44444
        11111           .....           33333           .....
        .....           22222           .....           44444
        11111           .....           33333           .....
        .....           22222           .....           44444
</pre>
<dt><code>interleave_top, 4</code></p>
<dd> Interleave the upper field from odd frames with the lower field from even frames, generating a frame with unchanged height at half frame rate. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
        11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
        11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
        11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
        
        Output:
        11111                           33333
        22222                           44444
        11111                           33333
        22222                           44444
</pre>
<dt><code>interleave_bottom, 5</code></p>
<dd> Interleave the lower field from odd frames with the upper field from even frames, generating a frame with unchanged height at half frame rate. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
        11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
        11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
        11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
        
        Output:
        22222                           44444
        11111                           33333
        22222                           44444
        11111                           33333
</pre>
<dt><code>interlacex2, 6</code></p>
<dd> Double frame rate with unchanged height. Frames are inserted each containing the second temporal field from the previous input frame and the first temporal field from the next input frame. This mode relies on the top_field_first flag. Useful for interlaced video displays with no field synchronisation. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111           22222           33333           44444
         11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
         11111           22222           33333           44444
        
        Output:
        11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444   44444
         11111   11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444
        11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444   44444
         11111   11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444
</pre>
<dt><code>mergex2, 7</code></p>
<dd> Move odd frames into the upper field, even into the lower field, generating a double height frame at same frame rate. </p>
<pre>
         ------&gt; time
        Input:
        Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
        
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        11111           22222           33333           44444
        
        Output:
        11111           33333           33333           55555
        22222           22222           44444           44444
        11111           33333           33333           55555
        22222           22222           44444           44444
        11111           33333           33333           55555
        22222           22222           44444           44444
        11111           33333           33333           55555
        22222           22222           44444           44444
</pre>
</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Numeric values are deprecated but are accepted for backward compatibility reasons. </p>
<p> Default mode is <tt>&quot;merge&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>flags</code></p>
<dd> Specify flags influencing the filter process. </p>
<p> Available value for <i>flags</i> is:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>low_pass_filter, vlpf</code></p>
<dd> Enable linear vertical low-pass filtering in the filter. Vertical low-pass filtering is required when creating an interlaced destination from a progressive source which contains high-frequency vertical detail. Filtering will reduce interlace &#8216;twitter&#8217; and Moire patterning.</p>
<dt><code>complex_filter, cvlpf</code></p>
<dd> Enable complex vertical low-pass filtering. This will slightly less reduce interlace &#8216;twitter&#8217; and Moire patterning but better retain detail and subjective sharpness impression.</p>
<dt><code>bypass_il</code></p>
<dd> Bypass already interlaced frames, only adjust the frame rate.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Vertical low-pass filtering and bypassing already interlaced frames can only be enabled for <code>mode</code> <i>interleave_top</i> and <i>interleave_bottom</i>.</dl>
</dl>
<h3>tmedian</h3>
<p> Pick median pixels from several successive input video frames. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>radius</code></p>
<dd> Set radius of median filter. Default is 1. Allowed range is from 1 to 127.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to filter. Default value is <tt>15</tt>, by which all planes are processed.</p>
<dt><code>percentile</code></p>
<dd> Set median percentile. Default value is <tt>0.5</tt>. Default value of <tt>0.5</tt> will pick always median values, while <tt>0</tt> will pick minimum values, and <tt>1</tt> maximum values.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports all above options as <code>commands</code>, excluding option <tt>&quot;radius&quot;</tt>.</p>
<h3>tmidequalizer</h3>
<p> Apply Temporal Midway Video Equalization effect. </p>
<p> Midway Video Equalization adjusts a sequence of video frames to have the same histograms, while maintaining their dynamics as much as possible. It&#8217;s useful for e.g. matching exposures from a video frames sequence. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>radius</code></p>
<dd> Set filtering radius. Default is <tt>5</tt>. Allowed range is from 1 to 127.</p>
<dt><code>sigma</code></p>
<dd> Set filtering sigma. Default is <tt>0.5</tt>. This controls strength of filtering. Setting this option to 0 effectively does nothing.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes to process. Default is <tt>15</tt>, which is all available planes.</dl>
<h3>tmix</h3>
<p> Mix successive video frames. </p>
<p> A description of the accepted options follows.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>frames</code></p>
<dd> The number of successive frames to mix. If unspecified, it defaults to 3.</p>
<dt><code>weights</code></p>
<dd> Specify weight of each input video frame. Each weight is separated by space. If number of weights is smaller than number of <i>frames</i> last specified weight will be used for all remaining unset weights.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Specify scale, if it is set it will be multiplied with sum of each weight multiplied with pixel values to give final destination pixel value. By default <i>scale</i> is auto scaled to sum of weights.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Average 7 successive frames: </p>
<pre>
        tmix=frames=7:weights=&quot;1 1 1 1 1 1 1&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply simple temporal convolution: </p>
<pre>
        tmix=frames=3:weights=&quot;-1 3 -1&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Similar as above but only showing temporal differences: </p>
<pre>
        tmix=frames=3:weights=&quot;-1 2 -1&quot;:scale=1
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>weights</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Syntax is same as option with same name.</dl>
<h3>tonemap</h3>
<p> Tone map colors from different dynamic ranges. </p>
<p> This filter expects data in single precision floating point, as it needs to operate on (and can output) out-of-range values. Another filter, such as <code>zscale</code>, is needed to convert the resulting frame to a usable format. </p>
<p> The tonemapping algorithms implemented only work on linear light, so input data should be linearized beforehand (and possibly correctly tagged). </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf zscale=transfer=linear,tonemap=clip,zscale=transfer=bt709,format=yuv420p OUTPUT
</pre>
<p> <i>Options</i> </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>tonemap</code></p>
<dd> Set the tone map algorithm to use. </p>
<p> Possible values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><i>none</i></p>
<dd> Do not apply any tone map, only desaturate overbright pixels.</p>
<dt><i>clip</i></p>
<dd> Hard-clip any out-of-range values. Use it for perfect color accuracy for in-range values, while distorting out-of-range values.</p>
<dt><i>linear</i></p>
<dd> Stretch the entire reference gamut to a linear multiple of the display.</p>
<dt><i>gamma</i></p>
<dd> Fit a logarithmic transfer between the tone curves.</p>
<dt><i>reinhard</i></p>
<dd> Preserve overall image brightness with a simple curve, using nonlinear contrast, which results in flattening details and degrading color accuracy.</p>
<dt><i>hable</i></p>
<dd> Preserve both dark and bright details better than <i>reinhard</i>, at the cost of slightly darkening everything. Use it when detail preservation is more important than color and brightness accuracy.</p>
<dt><i>mobius</i></p>
<dd> Smoothly map out-of-range values, while retaining contrast and colors for in-range material as much as possible. Use it when color accuracy is more important than detail preservation.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is none.</dl>
<dt><code>param</code></p>
<dd> Tune the tone mapping algorithm. </p>
<p> This affects the following algorithms:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><i>none</i></p>
<dd> Ignored.</p>
<dt><i>linear</i></p>
<dd> Specifies the scale factor to use while stretching. Default to 1.0.</p>
<dt><i>gamma</i></p>
<dd> Specifies the exponent of the function. Default to 1.8.</p>
<dt><i>clip</i></p>
<dd> Specify an extra linear coefficient to multiply into the signal before clipping. Default to 1.0.</p>
<dt><i>reinhard</i></p>
<dd> Specify the local contrast coefficient at the display peak. Default to 0.5, which means that in-gamut values will be about half as bright as when clipping.</p>
<dt><i>hable</i></p>
<dd> Ignored.</p>
<dt><i>mobius</i></p>
<dd> Specify the transition point from linear to mobius transform. Every value below this point is guaranteed to be mapped 1:1. The higher the value, the more accurate the result will be, at the cost of losing bright details. Default to 0.3, which due to the steep initial slope still preserves in-range colors fairly accurately.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>desat</code></p>
<dd> Apply desaturation for highlights that exceed this level of brightness. The higher the parameter, the more color information will be preserved. This setting helps prevent unnaturally blown-out colors for super-highlights, by (smoothly) turning into white instead. This makes images feel more natural, at the cost of reducing information about out-of-range colors. </p>
<p> The default of 2.0 is somewhat conservative and will mostly just apply to skies or directly sunlit surfaces. A setting of 0.0 disables this option. </p>
<p> This option works only if the input frame has a supported color tag.</p>
<dt><code>peak</code></p>
<dd> Override signal/nominal/reference peak with this value. Useful when the embedded peak information in display metadata is not reliable or when tone mapping from a lower range to a higher range.</dl>
<h3>tpad</h3>
<p> Temporarily pad video frames. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>start</code></p>
<dd> Specify number of delay frames before input video stream. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>stop</code></p>
<dd> Specify number of padding frames after input video stream. Set to -1 to pad indefinitely. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>start_mode</code></p>
<dd> Set kind of frames added to beginning of stream. Can be either <i>add</i> or <i>clone</i>. With <i>add</i> frames of solid-color are added. With <i>clone</i> frames are clones of first frame. Default is <i>add</i>.</p>
<dt><code>stop_mode</code></p>
<dd> Set kind of frames added to end of stream. Can be either <i>add</i> or <i>clone</i>. With <i>add</i> frames of solid-color are added. With <i>clone</i> frames are clones of last frame. Default is <i>add</i>.</p>
<dt><code>start_duration, stop_duration</code></p>
<dd> Specify the duration of the start/stop delay. See <code>the Time duration section in the <a href="/docs/linux/man/1-ffmpeg-utils/">ffmpeg-utils</a>(1) manual</code> for the accepted syntax. These options override <i>start</i> and <i>stop</i>. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the padded area. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. </p>
<p> The default value of <i>color</i> is &#8220;black&#8221;.</dl>
<h3>transpose</h3>
<p> Transpose rows with columns in the input video and optionally flip it. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dir</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transposition direction. </p>
<p> Can assume the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>0, 4, cclock_flip</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise and vertically flip (default), that is: </p>
<pre>
        L.R     L.l
        . . -&gt;  . .
        l.r     R.r
</pre>
<dt><code>1, 5, clock</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise, that is: </p>
<pre>
        L.R     l.L
        . . -&gt;  . .
        l.r     r.R
</pre>
<dt><code>2, 6, cclock</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise, that is: </p>
<pre>
        L.R     R.r
        . . -&gt;  . .
        l.r     L.l
</pre>
<dt><code>3, 7, clock_flip</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise and vertically flip, that is: </p>
<pre>
        L.R     r.R
        . . -&gt;  . .
        l.r     l.L
</pre>
</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> For values between 4-7, the transposition is only done if the input video geometry is portrait and not landscape. These values are deprecated, the <tt>&quot;passthrough&quot;</tt> option should be used instead. </p>
<p> Numerical values are deprecated, and should be dropped in favor of symbolic constants.</dl>
<dt><code>passthrough</code></p>
<dd> Do not apply the transposition if the input geometry matches the one specified by the specified value. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> Always apply transposition.</p>
<dt><code>portrait</code></p>
<dd> Preserve portrait geometry (when <i>height</i> &gt;= <i>width</i>).</p>
<dt><code>landscape</code></p>
<dd> Preserve landscape geometry (when <i>width</i> &gt;= <i>height</i>).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;none&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> For example to rotate by 90 degrees clockwise and preserve portrait layout: </p>
<pre>
        transpose=dir=1:passthrough=portrait
</pre>
<p> The command above can also be specified as: </p>
<pre>
        transpose=1:portrait
</pre>
<h3>transpose_npp</h3>
<p> Transpose rows with columns in the input video and optionally flip it. For more in depth examples see the <code>transpose</code> video filter, which shares mostly the same options. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dir</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transposition direction. </p>
<p> Can assume the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>cclock_flip</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise and vertically flip. (default)</p>
<dt><code>clock</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise.</p>
<dt><code>cclock</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise.</p>
<dt><code>clock_flip</code></p>
<dd> Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise and vertically flip.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>passthrough</code></p>
<dd> Do not apply the transposition if the input geometry matches the one specified by the specified value. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> Always apply transposition. (default)</p>
<dt><code>portrait</code></p>
<dd> Preserve portrait geometry (when <i>height</i> &gt;= <i>width</i>).</p>
<dt><code>landscape</code></p>
<dd> Preserve landscape geometry (when <i>width</i> &gt;= <i>height</i>).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<h3>trim</h3>
<p> Trim the input so that the output contains one continuous subpart of the input. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>start</code></p>
<dd> Specify the time of the start of the kept section, i.e. the frame with the timestamp <i>start</i> will be the first frame in the output.</p>
<dt><code>end</code></p>
<dd> Specify the time of the first frame that will be dropped, i.e. the frame immediately preceding the one with the timestamp <i>end</i> will be the last frame in the output.</p>
<dt><code>start_pts</code></p>
<dd> This is the same as <i>start</i>, except this option sets the start timestamp in timebase units instead of seconds.</p>
<dt><code>end_pts</code></p>
<dd> This is the same as <i>end</i>, except this option sets the end timestamp in timebase units instead of seconds.</p>
<dt><code>duration</code></p>
<dd> The maximum duration of the output in seconds.</p>
<dt><code>start_frame</code></p>
<dd> The number of the first frame that should be passed to the output.</p>
<dt><code>end_frame</code></p>
<dd> The number of the first frame that should be dropped.</dl>
<p> <code>start</code>, <code>end</code>, and <code>duration</code> are expressed as time duration specifications; see <code>the Time duration section in the <a href="/docs/linux/man/1-ffmpeg-utils/">ffmpeg-utils</a>(1) manual</code> for the accepted syntax. </p>
<p> Note that the first two sets of the start/end options and the <code>duration</code> option look at the frame timestamp, while the _frame variants simply count the frames that pass through the filter. Also note that this filter does not modify the timestamps. If you wish for the output timestamps to start at zero, insert a setpts filter after the trim filter. </p>
<p> If multiple start or end options are set, this filter tries to be greedy and keep all the frames that match at least one of the specified constraints. To keep only the part that matches all the constraints at once, chain multiple trim filters. </p>
<p> The defaults are such that all the input is kept. So it is possible to set e.g. just the end values to keep everything before the specified time. </p>
<p> Examples:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Drop everything except the second minute of input: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf trim=60:120
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Keep only the first second: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf trim=duration=1
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>unpremultiply</h3>
<p> Apply alpha unpremultiply effect to input video stream using first plane of second stream as alpha. </p>
<p> Both streams must have same dimensions and same pixel format. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following option:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied. By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed. </p>
<p> If the format has 1 or 2 components, then luma is bit 0. If the format has 3 or 4 components: for <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> formats bit 0 is green, bit 1 is blue and bit 2 is red; for <font SIZE="-1">YUV</font> formats bit 0 is luma, bit 1 is chroma-U and bit 2 is chroma-V. If present, the alpha channel is always the last bit.</p>
<dt><code>inplace</code></p>
<dd> Do not require 2nd input for processing, instead use alpha plane from input stream.</dl>
<h3>unsharp</h3>
<p> Sharpen or blur the input video. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>luma_msize_x, lx</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma matrix horizontal size. It must be an odd integer between 3 and 23. The default value is 5.</p>
<dt><code>luma_msize_y, ly</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma matrix vertical size. It must be an odd integer between 3 and 23. The default value is 5.</p>
<dt><code>luma_amount, la</code></p>
<dd> Set the luma effect strength. It must be a floating point number, reasonable values lay between -1.5 and 1.5. </p>
<p> Negative values will blur the input video, while positive values will sharpen it, a value of zero will disable the effect. </p>
<p> Default value is 1.0.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_msize_x, cx</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma matrix horizontal size. It must be an odd integer between 3 and 23. The default value is 5.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_msize_y, cy</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma matrix vertical size. It must be an odd integer between 3 and 23. The default value is 5.</p>
<dt><code>chroma_amount, ca</code></p>
<dd> Set the chroma effect strength. It must be a floating point number, reasonable values lay between -1.5 and 1.5. </p>
<p> Negative values will blur the input video, while positive values will sharpen it, a value of zero will disable the effect. </p>
<p> Default value is 0.0.</dl>
<p> All parameters are optional and default to the equivalent of the string &#8216;5:5:1.0:5:5:0.0&#8217;. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply strong luma sharpen effect: </p>
<pre>
        unsharp=luma_msize_x=7:luma_msize_y=7:luma_amount=2.5
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply a strong blur of both luma and chroma parameters: </p>
<pre>
        unsharp=7:7:-2:7:7:-2
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>untile</h3>
<p> Decompose a video made of tiled images into the individual images. </p>
<p> The frame rate of the output video is the frame rate of the input video multiplied by the number of tiles. </p>
<p> This filter does the reverse of <code>tile</code>. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>layout</code></p>
<dd> Set the grid size (i.e. the number of lines and columns). For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Produce a 1-second video from a still image file made of 25 frames stacked vertically, like an analogic film reel: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -r 1 -i image.jpg -vf untile=1x25 movie.mkv
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>uspp</h3>
<p> Apply ultra slow/simple postprocessing filter that compresses and decompresses the image at several (or &#8211; in the case of <code>quality</code> level <tt>8</tt> &#8211; all) shifts and average the results. </p>
<p> The way this differs from the behavior of spp is that uspp actually encodes &amp; decodes each case with libavcodec Snow, whereas spp uses a simplified intra only 8&#215;8 <font SIZE="-1">DCT</font> similar to <font SIZE="-1">MJPEG.</font> </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>quality</code></p>
<dd> Set quality. This option defines the number of levels for averaging. It accepts an integer in the range 0-8. If set to <tt>0</tt>, the filter will have no effect. A value of <tt>8</tt> means the higher quality. For each increment of that value the speed drops by a factor of approximately 2. Default value is <tt>3</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>qp</code></p>
<dd> Force a constant quantization parameter. If not set, the filter will use the <font SIZE="-1">QP</font> from the video stream (if available).</dl>
<h3>v360</h3>
<p> Convert 360 videos between various formats. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>input</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>output</code></p>
<dd> Set format of the input/output video. </p>
<p> Available formats:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>e</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>equirect</code></p>
<dd> Equirectangular projection.</p>
<dt><code>c3x2</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c6x1</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>c1x6</code></p>
<dd> Cubemap with 3&#215;2/6&#215;1/1&#215;6 layout. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>in_pad</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_pad</code></p>
<dd> Set padding proportion for the input/output cubemap. Values in decimals. </p>
<p> Example values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>0</code></p>
<dd> No padding.</p>
<dt><code>0.01</code></p>
<dd> 1% of face is padding. For example, with 1920&#215;1280 resolution face size would be 640&#215;640 and padding would be 3 pixels from each side. (640 * 0.01 = 6 pixels)</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is @samp<code>{0</code>}. Maximum value is @samp<code>{0.1</code>}.</dl>
<dt><code>fin_pad</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fout_pad</code></p>
<dd> Set fixed padding for the input/output cubemap. Values in pixels. </p>
<p> Default value is @samp<code>{0</code>}. If greater than zero it overrides other padding options.</p>
<dt><code>in_forder</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_forder</code></p>
<dd> Set order of faces for the input/output cubemap. Choose one direction for each position. </p>
<p> Designation of directions:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>r</code></p>
<dd> right</p>
<dt><code>l</code></p>
<dd> left</p>
<dt><code>u</code></p>
<dd> up</p>
<dt><code>d</code></p>
<dd> down</p>
<dt><code>f</code></p>
<dd> forward</p>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd> back</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is @samp<code>{rludfb</code>}.</dl>
<dt><code>in_frot</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_frot</code></p>
<dd> Set rotation of faces for the input/output cubemap. Choose one angle for each position. </p>
<p> Designation of angles:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>0</code></p>
<dd> 0 degrees clockwise</p>
<dt><code>1</code></p>
<dd> 90 degrees clockwise</p>
<dt><code>2</code></p>
<dd> 180 degrees clockwise</p>
<dt><code>3</code></p>
<dd> 270 degrees clockwise</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is @samp<code>{000000</code>}.</dl>
</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>eac</code></p>
<dd> Equi-Angular Cubemap.</p>
<dt><code>flat</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gnomonic</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>rectilinear</code></p>
<dd> Regular video. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>id_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>dfisheye</code></p>
<dd> Dual fisheye. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>id_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>barrel</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>fb</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>barrelsplit</code></p>
<dd> Facebook&#8217;s 360 formats.</p>
<dt><code>sg</code></p>
<dd> Stereographic format. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>id_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>mercator</code></p>
<dd> Mercator format.</p>
<dt><code>ball</code></p>
<dd> Ball format, gives significant distortion toward the back.</p>
<dt><code>hammer</code></p>
<dd> Hammer-Aitoff map projection format.</p>
<dt><code>sinusoidal</code></p>
<dd> Sinusoidal map projection format.</p>
<dt><code>fisheye</code></p>
<dd> Fisheye projection. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>id_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>pannini</code></p>
<dd> Pannini projection. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output pannini parameter.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input pannini parameter.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>cylindrical</code></p>
<dd> Cylindrical projection. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>id_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>perspective</code></p>
<dd> Perspective projection. <i>(output only)</i> </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set perspective parameter.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>tetrahedron</code></p>
<dd> Tetrahedron projection.</p>
<dt><code>tsp</code></p>
<dd> Truncated square pyramid projection.</p>
<dt><code>he</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hequirect</code></p>
<dd> Half equirectangular projection.</p>
<dt><code>equisolid</code></p>
<dd> Equisolid format. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>id_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>og</code></p>
<dd> Orthographic format. </p>
<p> Format specific options:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>h_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set output horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</p>
<dt><code>ih_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_fov</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>id_fov</code></p>
<dd> Set input horizontal/vertical/diagonal field of view. Values in degrees. </p>
<p> If diagonal field of view is set it overrides horizontal and vertical field of view.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>octahedron</code></p>
<dd> Octahedron projection.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>interp</code></p>
<dd> Set interpolation method.<i>Note: more complex interpolation methods require much more memory to run.</i> </p>
<p> Available methods:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>near</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>nearest</code></p>
<dd> Nearest neighbour.</p>
<dt><code>line</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>linear</code></p>
<dd> Bilinear interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>lagrange9</code></p>
<dd> Lagrange9 interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>cube</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cubic</code></p>
<dd> Bicubic interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>lanc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>lanczos</code></p>
<dd> Lanczos interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>sp16</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>spline16</code></p>
<dd> Spline16 interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>gauss</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gaussian</code></p>
<dd> Gaussian interpolation.</p>
<dt><code>mitchell</code></p>
<dd> Mitchell interpolation.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is @samp<code>{line</code>}.</dl>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> Set the output video resolution. </p>
<p> Default resolution depends on formats.</p>
<dt><code>in_stereo</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>out_stereo</code></p>
<dd> Set the input/output stereo format.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>2d</code></p>
<dd> 2D mono</p>
<dt><code>sbs</code></p>
<dd> Side by side</p>
<dt><code>tb</code></p>
<dd> Top bottom</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is @samp<code>{2d</code>} for input and output format.</dl>
<dt><code>yaw</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>pitch</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>roll</code></p>
<dd> Set rotation for the output video. Values in degrees.</p>
<dt><code>rorder</code></p>
<dd> Set rotation order for the output video. Choose one item for each position.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>y, Y</code></p>
<dd> yaw</p>
<dt><code>p, P</code></p>
<dd> pitch</p>
<dt><code>r, R</code></p>
<dd> roll</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is @samp<code>{ypr</code>}.</dl>
<dt><code>h_flip</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>v_flip</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>d_flip</code></p>
<dd> Flip the output video horizontally(swaps left-right)/vertically(swaps up-down)/in-depth(swaps back-forward). Boolean values.</p>
<dt><code>ih_flip</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>iv_flip</code></p>
<dd> Set if input video is flipped horizontally/vertically. Boolean values.</p>
<dt><code>in_trans</code></p>
<dd> Set if input video is transposed. Boolean value, by default disabled.</p>
<dt><code>out_trans</code></p>
<dd> Set if output video needs to be transposed. Boolean value, by default disabled.</p>
<dt><code>alpha_mask</code></p>
<dd> Build mask in alpha plane for all unmapped pixels by marking them fully transparent. Boolean value, by default disabled.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Convert equirectangular video to cubemap with 3&#215;2 layout and 1% padding using bicubic interpolation: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.mkv -vf v360=e:c3x2:cubic:out_pad=0.01 output.mkv
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Extract back view of Equi-Angular Cubemap: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.mkv -vf v360=eac:flat:yaw=180 output.mkv
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Convert transposed and horizontally flipped Equi-Angular Cubemap in side-by-side stereo format to equirectangular top-bottom stereo format: </p>
<pre>
        v360=eac:equirect:in_stereo=sbs:in_trans=1:ih_flip=1:out_stereo=tb
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports subset of above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>vaguedenoiser</h3>
<p> Apply a wavelet based denoiser. </p>
<p> It transforms each frame from the video input into the wavelet domain, using Cohen-Daubechies-Feauveau 9/7. Then it applies some filtering to the obtained coefficients. It does an inverse wavelet transform after. Due to wavelet properties, it should give a nice smoothed result, and reduced noise, without blurring picture features. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>threshold</code></p>
<dd> The filtering strength. The higher, the more filtered the video will be. Hard thresholding can use a higher threshold than soft thresholding before the video looks overfiltered. Default value is 2.</p>
<dt><code>method</code></p>
<dd> The filtering method the filter will use. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>hard</code></p>
<dd> All values under the threshold will be zeroed.</p>
<dt><code>soft</code></p>
<dd> All values under the threshold will be zeroed. All values above will be reduced by the threshold.</p>
<dt><code>garrote</code></p>
<dd> Scales or nullifies coefficients &#8211; intermediary between (more) soft and (less) hard thresholding.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is garrote.</dl>
<dt><code>nsteps</code></p>
<dd> Number of times, the wavelet will decompose the picture. Picture can&#8217;t be decomposed beyond a particular point (typically, 8 for a 640&#215;480 frame &#8211; as 2^9 = 512 &gt; 480). Valid values are integers between 1 and 32. Default value is 6.</p>
<dt><code>percent</code></p>
<dd> Partial of full denoising (limited coefficients shrinking), from 0 to 100. Default value is 85.</p>
<dt><code>planes</code></p>
<dd> A list of the planes to process. By default all planes are processed.</p>
<dt><code>type</code></p>
<dd> The threshold type the filter will use. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>universal</code></p>
<dd> Threshold used is same for all decompositions.</p>
<dt><code>bayes</code></p>
<dd> Threshold used depends also on each decomposition coefficients.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is universal.</dl>
</dl>
<h3>vectorscope</h3>
<p> Display 2 color component values in the two dimensional graph (which is called a vectorscope). </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode, m</code></p>
<dd> Set vectorscope mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>gray</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tint</code></p>
<dd> Gray values are displayed on graph, higher brightness means more pixels have same component color value on location in graph. This is the default mode.</p>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd> Gray values are displayed on graph. Surrounding pixels values which are not present in video frame are drawn in gradient of 2 color components which are set by option <tt>&quot;x&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;y&quot;</tt>. The 3rd color component is static.</p>
<dt><code>color2</code></p>
<dd> Actual color components values present in video frame are displayed on graph.</p>
<dt><code>color3</code></p>
<dd> Similar as color2 but higher frequency of same values <tt>&quot;x&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;y&quot;</tt> on graph increases value of another color component, which is luminance by default values of <tt>&quot;x&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;y&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>color4</code></p>
<dd> Actual colors present in video frame are displayed on graph. If two different colors map to same position on graph then color with higher value of component not present in graph is picked.</p>
<dt><code>color5</code></p>
<dd> Gray values are displayed on graph. Similar to <tt>&quot;color&quot;</tt> but with 3rd color component picked from radial gradient.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd> Set which color component will be represented on X-axis. Default is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Set which color component will be represented on Y-axis. Default is <tt>2</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>intensity, i</code></p>
<dd> Set intensity, used by modes: gray, color, color3 and color5 for increasing brightness of color component which represents frequency of (X, Y) location in graph.</p>
<dt><code>envelope, e</code></p>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> No envelope, this is default.</p>
<dt><code>instant</code></p>
<dd> Instant envelope, even darkest single pixel will be clearly highlighted.</p>
<dt><code>peak</code></p>
<dd> Hold maximum and minimum values presented in graph over time. This way you can still spot out of range values without constantly looking at vectorscope.</p>
<dt><code>peak+instant</code></p>
<dd> Peak and instant envelope combined together.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>graticule, g</code></p>
<dd> Set what kind of graticule to draw.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>green</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>invert</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>opacity, o</code></p>
<dd> Set graticule opacity.</p>
<dt><code>flags, f</code></p>
<dd> Set graticule flags.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>white</code></p>
<dd> Draw graticule for white point.</p>
<dt><code>black</code></p>
<dd> Draw graticule for black point.</p>
<dt><code>name</code></p>
<dd> Draw color points short names.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>bgopacity, b</code></p>
<dd> Set background opacity.</p>
<dt><code>lthreshold, l</code></p>
<dd> Set low threshold for color component not represented on X or Y axis. Values lower than this value will be ignored. Default is 0. Note this value is multiplied with actual max possible value one pixel component can have. So for 8-bit input and low threshold value of 0.1 actual threshold is 0.1 * 255 = 25.</p>
<dt><code>hthreshold, h</code></p>
<dd> Set high threshold for color component not represented on X or Y axis. Values higher than this value will be ignored. Default is 1. Note this value is multiplied with actual max possible value one pixel component can have. So for 8-bit input and high threshold value of 0.9 actual threshold is 0.9 * 255 = 230.</p>
<dt><code>colorspace, c</code></p>
<dd> Set what kind of colorspace to use when drawing graticule.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>601</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>709</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is auto.</dl>
<dt><code>tint0, t0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tint1, t1</code></p>
<dd> Set color tint for gray/tint vectorscope mode. By default both options are zero. This means no tint, and output will remain gray.</dl>
<h3>vidstabdetect</h3>
<p> Analyze video stabilization/deshaking. Perform pass 1 of 2, see <code>vidstabtransform</code> for pass 2. </p>
<p> This filter generates a file with relative translation and rotation transform information about subsequent frames, which is then used by the <code>vidstabtransform</code> filter. </p>
<p> To enable compilation of this filter you need to configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libvidstab&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>result</code></p>
<dd> Set the path to the file used to write the transforms information. Default value is <i>transforms.trf</i>.</p>
<dt><code>shakiness</code></p>
<dd> Set how shaky the video is and how quick the camera is. It accepts an integer in the range 1-10, a value of 1 means little shakiness, a value of 10 means strong shakiness. Default value is 5.</p>
<dt><code>accuracy</code></p>
<dd> Set the accuracy of the detection process. It must be a value in the range 1-15. A value of 1 means low accuracy, a value of 15 means high accuracy. Default value is 15.</p>
<dt><code>stepsize</code></p>
<dd> Set stepsize of the search process. The region around minimum is scanned with 1 pixel resolution. Default value is 6.</p>
<dt><code>mincontrast</code></p>
<dd> Set minimum contrast. Below this value a local measurement field is discarded. Must be a floating point value in the range 0-1. Default value is 0.3.</p>
<dt><code>tripod</code></p>
<dd> Set reference frame number for tripod mode. </p>
<p> If enabled, the motion of the frames is compared to a reference frame in the filtered stream, identified by the specified number. The idea is to compensate all movements in a more-or-less static scene and keep the camera view absolutely still. </p>
<p> If set to 0, it is disabled. The frames are counted starting from 1.</p>
<dt><code>show</code></p>
<dd> Show fields and transforms in the resulting frames. It accepts an integer in the range 0-2. Default value is 0, which disables any visualization.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Use default values: </p>
<pre>
        vidstabdetect
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Analyze strongly shaky movie and put the results in file <i>mytransforms.trf</i>: </p>
<pre>
        vidstabdetect=shakiness=10:accuracy=15:result=&quot;mytransforms.trf&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Visualize the result of internal transformations in the resulting video: </p>
<pre>
        vidstabdetect=show=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Analyze a video with medium shakiness using <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input -vf vidstabdetect=shakiness=5:show=1 dummy.avi
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>vidstabtransform</h3>
<p> Video stabilization/deshaking: pass 2 of 2, see <code>vidstabdetect</code> for pass 1. </p>
<p> Read a file with transform information for each frame and apply/compensate them. Together with the <code>vidstabdetect</code> filter this can be used to deshake videos. See also &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://public.hronopik.de/vid.stab">http://public.hronopik.de/vid.stab</a></code>&gt;. It is important to also use the <code>unsharp</code> filter, see below. </p>
<p> To enable compilation of this filter you need to configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libvidstab&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> <i>Options</i></p>
<dl>
<dt><code>input</code></p>
<dd> Set path to the file used to read the transforms. Default value is <i>transforms.trf</i>.</p>
<dt><code>smoothing</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of frames (value*2 + 1) used for lowpass filtering the camera movements. Default value is 10. </p>
<p> For example a number of 10 means that 21 frames are used (10 in the past and 10 in the future) to smoothen the motion in the video. A larger value leads to a smoother video, but limits the acceleration of the camera (pan/tilt movements). 0 is a special case where a static camera is simulated.</p>
<dt><code>optalgo</code></p>
<dd> Set the camera path optimization algorithm. </p>
<p> Accepted values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>gauss</code></p>
<dd> gaussian kernel low-pass filter on camera motion (default)</p>
<dt><code>avg</code></p>
<dd> averaging on transformations</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>maxshift</code></p>
<dd> Set maximal number of pixels to translate frames. Default value is -1, meaning no limit.</p>
<dt><code>maxangle</code></p>
<dd> Set maximal angle in radians (degree*PI/180) to rotate frames. Default value is -1, meaning no limit.</p>
<dt><code>crop</code></p>
<dd> Specify how to deal with borders that may be visible due to movement compensation. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>keep</code></p>
<dd> keep image information from previous frame (default)</p>
<dt><code>black</code></p>
<dd> fill the border black</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>invert</code></p>
<dd> Invert transforms if set to 1. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>relative</code></p>
<dd> Consider transforms as relative to previous frame if set to 1, absolute if set to 0. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>zoom</code></p>
<dd> Set percentage to zoom. A positive value will result in a zoom-in effect, a negative value in a zoom-out effect. Default value is 0 (no zoom).</p>
<dt><code>optzoom</code></p>
<dd> Set optimal zooming to avoid borders. </p>
<p> Accepted values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>0</code></p>
<dd> disabled</p>
<dt><code>1</code></p>
<dd> optimal static zoom value is determined (only very strong movements will lead to visible borders) (default)</p>
<dt><code>2</code></p>
<dd> optimal adaptive zoom value is determined (no borders will be visible), see <code>zoomspeed</code></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Note that the value given at zoom is added to the one calculated here.</dl>
<dt><code>zoomspeed</code></p>
<dd> Set percent to zoom maximally each frame (enabled when <code>optzoom</code> is set to 2). Range is from 0 to 5, default value is 0.25.</p>
<dt><code>interpol</code></p>
<dd> Specify type of interpolation. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>no</code></p>
<dd> no interpolation</p>
<dt><code>linear</code></p>
<dd> linear only horizontal</p>
<dt><code>bilinear</code></p>
<dd> linear in both directions (default)</p>
<dt><code>bicubic</code></p>
<dd> cubic in both directions (slow)</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>tripod</code></p>
<dd> Enable virtual tripod mode if set to 1, which is equivalent to <tt>&quot;relative=0:smoothing=0&quot;</tt>. Default value is 0. </p>
<p> Use also <tt>&quot;tripod&quot;</tt> option of <code>vidstabdetect</code>.</p>
<dt><code>debug</code></p>
<dd> Increase log verbosity if set to 1. Also the detected global motions are written to the temporary file <i>global_motions.trf</i>. Default value is 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Use <code>ffmpeg</code> for a typical stabilization with default values: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i inp.mpeg -vf vidstabtransform,unsharp=5:5:0.8:3:3:0.4 inp_stabilized.mpeg
</pre>
<p> Note the use of the <code>unsharp</code> filter which is always recommended.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Zoom in a bit more and load transform data from a given file: </p>
<pre>
        vidstabtransform=zoom=5:input=&quot;mytransforms.trf&quot;
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Smoothen the video even more: </p>
<pre>
        vidstabtransform=smoothing=30
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>vflip</h3>
<p> Flip the input video vertically. </p>
<p> For example, to vertically flip a video with <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i in.avi -vf &quot;vflip&quot; out.avi
</pre>
<h3>vfrdet</h3>
<p> Detect variable frame rate video. </p>
<p> This filter tries to detect if the input is variable or constant frame rate. </p>
<p> At end it will output number of frames detected as having variable delta pts, and ones with constant delta pts. If there was frames with variable delta, than it will also show min, max and average delta encountered.</p>
<h3>vibrance</h3>
<p> Boost or alter saturation. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>intensity</code></p>
<dd> Set strength of boost if positive value or strength of alter if negative value. Default is 0. Allowed range is from -2 to 2.</p>
<dt><code>rbal</code></p>
<dd> Set the red balance. Default is 1. Allowed range is from -10 to 10.</p>
<dt><code>gbal</code></p>
<dd> Set the green balance. Default is 1. Allowed range is from -10 to 10.</p>
<dt><code>bbal</code></p>
<dd> Set the blue balance. Default is 1. Allowed range is from -10 to 10.</p>
<dt><code>rlum</code></p>
<dd> Set the red luma coefficient.</p>
<dt><code>glum</code></p>
<dd> Set the green luma coefficient.</p>
<dt><code>blum</code></p>
<dd> Set the blue luma coefficient.</p>
<dt><code>alternate</code></p>
<dd> If <tt>&quot;intensity&quot;</tt> is negative and this is set to 1, colors will change, otherwise colors will be less saturated, more towards gray.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the all above options as <code>commands</code>.</p>
<h3>vif</h3>
<p> Obtain the average <font SIZE="-1">VIF</font> (Visual Information Fidelity) between two input videos. </p>
<p> This filter takes two input videos. </p>
<p> Both input videos must have the same resolution and pixel format for this filter to work correctly. Also it assumes that both inputs have the same number of frames, which are compared one by one. </p>
<p> The obtained average <font SIZE="-1">VIF</font> score is printed through the logging system. </p>
<p> The filter stores the calculated <font SIZE="-1">VIF</font> score of each frame. </p>
<p> In the below example the input file <i>main.mpg</i> being processed is compared with the reference file <i>ref.mpg</i>. </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi vif -f null -
</pre>
<h3>vignette</h3>
<p> Make or reverse a natural vignetting effect. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>angle, a</code></p>
<dd> Set lens angle expression as a number of radians. </p>
<p> The value is clipped in the <tt>&quot;[0,PI/2]&quot;</tt> range. </p>
<p> Default value: <tt>&quot;PI/5&quot;</tt></p>
<dt><code>x0</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>y0</code></p>
<dd> Set center coordinates expressions. Respectively <tt>&quot;w/2&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;h/2&quot;</tt> by default.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set forward/backward mode. </p>
<p> Available modes are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>forward</code></p>
<dd> The larger the distance from the central point, the darker the image becomes.</p>
<dt><code>backward</code></p>
<dd> The larger the distance from the central point, the brighter the image becomes. This can be used to reverse a vignette effect, though there is no automatic detection to extract the lens <code>angle</code> and other settings (yet). It can also be used to create a burning effect.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>forward</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>eval</code></p>
<dd> Set evaluation mode for the expressions (<code>angle</code>, <code>x0</code>, <code>y0</code>). </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>init</code></p>
<dd> Evaluate expressions only once during the filter initialization.</p>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Evaluate expressions for each incoming frame. This is way slower than the <code>init</code> mode since it requires all the scalers to be re-computed, but it allows advanced dynamic expressions.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>init</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>dither</code></p>
<dd> Set dithering to reduce the circular banding effects. Default is <tt>1</tt> (enabled).</p>
<dt><code>aspect</code></p>
<dd> Set vignette aspect. This setting allows one to adjust the shape of the vignette. Setting this value to the <font SIZE="-1">SAR</font> of the input will make a rectangular vignetting following the dimensions of the video. </p>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;1/1&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Expressions</i> </p>
<p> The <code>alpha</code>, <code>x0</code> and <code>y0</code> expressions can contain the following parameters.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> input width and height</p>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> the number of input frame, starting from 0</p>
<dt><code>pts</code></p>
<dd> the <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> (Presentation TimeStamp) time of the filtered video frame, expressed in <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>TB</i></font><i></i> units, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined</p>
<dt><code>r</code></p>
<dd> frame rate of the input video, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if the input frame rate is unknown</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> the <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> (Presentation TimeStamp) of the filtered video frame, expressed in seconds, <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined</p>
<dt><code>tb</code></p>
<dd> time base of the input video</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply simple strong vignetting effect: </p>
<pre>
        vignette=PI/4
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Make a flickering vignetting: </p>
<pre>
        vignette='PI/4+<a href="//www.systutorials.com/docs/linux/man/1-random/">random</a>(1)*PI/50':eval=frame
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>vmafmotion</h3>
<p> Obtain the average <font SIZE="-1">VMAF</font> motion score of a video. It is one of the component metrics of <font SIZE="-1">VMAF.</font> </p>
<p> The obtained average motion score is printed through the logging system. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>stats_file</code></p>
<dd> If specified, the filter will use the named file to save the motion score of each frame with respect to the previous frame. When filename equals &#8220;-&#8221; the data is sent to standard output.</dl>
<p> Example: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i ref.mpg -vf vmafmotion -f null -
</pre>
<h3>vstack</h3>
<p> Stack input videos vertically. </p>
<p> All streams must be of same pixel format and of same width. </p>
<p> Note that this filter is faster than using <code>overlay</code> and <code>pad</code> filter to create same output. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>inputs</code></p>
<dd> Set number of input streams. Default is 2.</p>
<dt><code>shortest</code></p>
<dd> If set to 1, force the output to terminate when the shortest input terminates. Default value is 0.</dl>
<h3>w3fdif</h3>
<p> Deinterlace the input video (&#8220;w3fdif&#8221; stands for &#8220;Weston 3 Field Deinterlacing Filter&#8221;). </p>
<p> Based on the process described by Martin Weston for <font SIZE="-1">BBC R&amp;D,</font> and implemented based on the de-interlace algorithm written by Jim Easterbrook for <font SIZE="-1">BBC R&amp;D,</font> the Weston 3 field deinterlacing filter uses filter coefficients calculated by <font SIZE="-1">BBC R&amp;D.</font> </p>
<p> This filter uses field-dominance information in frame to decide which of each pair of fields to place first in the output. If it gets it wrong use <code>setfield</code> filter before <tt>&quot;w3fdif&quot;</tt> filter. </p>
<p> There are two sets of filter coefficients, so called &#8220;simple&#8221; and &#8220;complex&#8221;. Which set of filter coefficients is used can be set by passing an optional parameter:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filter</code></p>
<dd> Set the interlacing filter coefficients. Accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>simple</code></p>
<dd> Simple filter coefficient set.</p>
<dt><code>complex</code></p>
<dd> More-complex filter coefficient set.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>complex</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> The interlacing mode to adopt. It accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>frame</code></p>
<dd> Output one frame for each frame.</p>
<dt><code>field</code></p>
<dd> Output one frame for each field.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default value is <tt>&quot;field&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>parity</code></p>
<dd> The picture field parity assumed for the input interlaced video. It accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>tff</code></p>
<dd> Assume the top field is first.</p>
<dt><code>bff</code></p>
<dd> Assume the bottom field is first.</p>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Enable automatic detection of field parity.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> The default value is <tt>&quot;auto&quot;</tt>. If the interlacing is unknown or the decoder does not export this information, top field first will be assumed.</dl>
<dt><code>deint</code></p>
<dd> Specify which frames to deinterlace. Accepts one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>all</code></p>
<dd> Deinterlace all frames,</p>
<dt><code>interlaced</code></p>
<dd> Only deinterlace frames marked as interlaced.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>all</code>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports same <code>commands</code> as options.</p>
<h3>waveform</h3>
<p> Video waveform monitor. </p>
<p> The waveform monitor plots color component intensity. By default luminance only. Each column of the waveform corresponds to a column of pixels in the source video. </p>
<p> It accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode, m</code></p>
<dd> Can be either <tt>&quot;row&quot;</tt>, or <tt>&quot;column&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;column&quot;</tt>. In row mode, the graph on the left side represents color component value 0 and the right side represents value = 255. In column mode, the top side represents color component value = 0 and bottom side represents value = 255.</p>
<dt><code>intensity, i</code></p>
<dd> Set intensity. Smaller values are useful to find out how many values of the same luminance are distributed across input rows/columns. Default value is <tt>0.04</tt>. Allowed range is [0, 1].</p>
<dt><code>mirror, r</code></p>
<dd> Set mirroring mode. <tt>0</tt> means unmirrored, <tt>1</tt> means mirrored. In mirrored mode, higher values will be represented on the left side for <tt>&quot;row&quot;</tt> mode and at the top for <tt>&quot;column&quot;</tt> mode. Default is <tt>1</tt> (mirrored).</p>
<dt><code>display, d</code></p>
<dd> Set display mode. It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>overlay</code></p>
<dd> Presents information identical to that in the <tt>&quot;parade&quot;</tt>, except that the graphs representing color components are superimposed directly over one another. </p>
<p> This display mode makes it easier to spot relative differences or similarities in overlapping areas of the color components that are supposed to be identical, such as neutral whites, grays, or blacks.</p>
<dt><code>stack</code></p>
<dd> Display separate graph for the color components side by side in <tt>&quot;row&quot;</tt> mode or one below the other in <tt>&quot;column&quot;</tt> mode.</p>
<dt><code>parade</code></p>
<dd> Display separate graph for the color components side by side in <tt>&quot;column&quot;</tt> mode or one below the other in <tt>&quot;row&quot;</tt> mode. </p>
<p> Using this display mode makes it easy to spot color casts in the highlights and shadows of an image, by comparing the contours of the top and the bottom graphs of each waveform. Since whites, grays, and blacks are characterized by exactly equal amounts of red, green, and blue, neutral areas of the picture should display three waveforms of roughly equal width/height.
