<dd> A &#8216;|&#8217;-separated list of parameters to pass to the frei0r source.</dl>
<p> For example, to generate a frei0r partik0l source with size 200&#215;200 and frame rate 10 which is overlaid on the overlay filter main input: </p>
<p>frei0r_src = size = 200&#215;200 : framerate = 10 : filter_name = partik0l : filter_params = 1234 [overlay];<br />
[in][overlay] overlay</p>
<h3>life</h3>
<p> Generate a life pattern. </p>
<p> This source is based on a generalization of John Conway&#8217;s life game. </p>
<p> The sourced input represents a life grid, each pixel represents a cell which can be in one of two possible states, alive or dead. Every cell interacts with its eight neighbours, which are the cells that are horizontally, vertically, or diagonally adjacent. </p>
<p> At each interaction the grid evolves according to the adopted rule, which specifies the number of neighbor alive cells which will make a cell stay alive or born. The <code>rule</code> option allows one to specify the rule to adopt. </p>
<p> This source accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filename, f</code></p>
<dd> Set the file from which to read the initial grid state. In the file, each non-whitespace character is considered an alive cell, and newline is used to delimit the end of each row. </p>
<p> If this option is not specified, the initial grid is generated randomly.</p>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the video rate, that is the number of frames generated per second. Default is 25.</p>
<dt><code>random_fill_ratio, ratio</code></p>
<dd> Set the random fill ratio for the initial random grid. It is a floating point number value ranging from 0 to 1, defaults to 1/PHI. It is ignored when a file is specified.</p>
<dt><code>random_seed, seed</code></p>
<dd> Set the seed for filling the initial random grid, must be an integer included between 0 and <font SIZE="-1">UINT32_MAX.</font> If not specified, or if explicitly set to -1, the filter will try to use a good random seed on a best effort basis.</p>
<dt><code>rule</code></p>
<dd> Set the life rule. </p>
<p> A rule can be specified with a code of the kind &quot;S<i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>NS</i></font><i></i>/B<i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>NB</i></font><i></i>&quot;, where <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>NS</i></font><i></i> and <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>NB</i></font><i></i> are sequences of numbers in the range 0-8, <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>NS</i></font><i></i> specifies the number of alive neighbor cells which make a live cell stay alive, and <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>NB</i></font><i></i> the number of alive neighbor cells which make a dead cell to become alive (i.e. to &#8220;born&#8221;). &#8220;s&#8221; and &#8220;b&#8221; can be used in place of &#8220;S&#8221; and &#8220;B&#8221;, respectively. </p>
<p> Alternatively a rule can be specified by an 18-bits integer. The 9 high order bits are used to encode the next cell state if it is alive for each number of neighbor alive cells, the low order bits specify the rule for &#8220;borning&#8221; new cells. Higher order bits encode for an higher number of neighbor cells. For example the number 6153 = <tt>&quot;(12&lt;&lt;9)+9&quot;</tt> specifies a stay alive rule of 12 and a born rule of 9, which corresponds to &#8220;S23/B03&#8221;. </p>
<p> Default value is &#8220;S23/B3&#8221;, which is the original Conway&#8217;s game of life rule, and will keep a cell alive if it has 2 or 3 neighbor alive cells, and will born a new cell if there are three alive cells around a dead cell.</p>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Set the size of the output video. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. </p>
<p> If <code>filename</code> is specified, the size is set by default to the same size of the input file. If <code>size</code> is set, it must contain the size specified in the input file, and the initial grid defined in that file is centered in the larger resulting area. </p>
<p> If a filename is not specified, the size value defaults to &#8220;320&#215;240&#8221; (used for a randomly generated initial grid).</p>
<dt><code>stitch</code></p>
<dd> If set to 1, stitch the left and right grid edges together, and the top and bottom edges also. Defaults to 1.</p>
<dt><code>mold</code></p>
<dd> Set cell mold speed. If set, a dead cell will go from <code>death_color</code> to <code>mold_color</code> with a step of <code>mold</code>. <code>mold</code> can have a value from 0 to 255.</p>
<dt><code>life_color</code></p>
<dd> Set the color of living (or new born) cells.</p>
<dt><code>death_color</code></p>
<dd> Set the color of dead cells. If <code>mold</code> is set, this is the first color used to represent a dead cell.</p>
<dt><code>mold_color</code></p>
<dd> Set mold color, for definitely dead and moldy cells. </p>
<p> For the syntax of these 3 color options, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Read a grid from <i>pattern</i>, and center it on a grid of size 300&#215;300 pixels: </p>
<pre>
        life=f=pattern:s=300x300
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a random grid of size 200&#215;200, with a fill ratio of 2/3: </p>
<pre>
        life=ratio=2/3:s=200x200
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Specify a custom rule for evolving a randomly generated grid: </p>
<pre>
        life=rule=S14/B34
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Full example with slow death effect (mold) using <code>ffplay</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffplay -f lavfi life=s=300x200:mold=10:r=60:ratio=0.1:death_color=#C83232:life_color=#00ff00,scale=1200:800:flags=16
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>allrgb, allyuv, color, haldclutsrc, nullsrc, pal75bars, pal100bars, rgbtestsrc, smptebars, smptehdbars, testsrc, testsrc2, yuvtestsrc</h3>
<p> The <tt>&quot;allrgb&quot;</tt> source returns frames of size 4096&#215;4096 of all rgb colors. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;allyuv&quot;</tt> source returns frames of size 4096&#215;4096 of all yuv colors. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;color&quot;</tt> source provides an uniformly colored input. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;haldclutsrc&quot;</tt> source provides an identity Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT.</font> See also <code>haldclut</code> filter. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;nullsrc&quot;</tt> source returns unprocessed video frames. It is mainly useful to be employed in analysis / debugging tools, or as the source for filters which ignore the input data. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;pal75bars&quot;</tt> source generates a color bars pattern, based on <font SIZE="-1">EBU PAL</font> recommendations with 75% color levels. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;pal100bars&quot;</tt> source generates a color bars pattern, based on <font SIZE="-1">EBU PAL</font> recommendations with 100% color levels. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;rgbtestsrc&quot;</tt> source generates an <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> test pattern useful for detecting <font SIZE="-1">RGB</font> vs <font SIZE="-1">BGR</font> issues. You should see a red, green and blue stripe from top to bottom. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;smptebars&quot;</tt> source generates a color bars pattern, based on the <font SIZE="-1">SMPTE</font> Engineering Guideline <font SIZE="-1">EG 1-1990.</font> </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;smptehdbars&quot;</tt> source generates a color bars pattern, based on the <font SIZE="-1">SMPTE RP 219-2002.</font> </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;testsrc&quot;</tt> source generates a test video pattern, showing a color pattern, a scrolling gradient and a timestamp. This is mainly intended for testing purposes. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;testsrc2&quot;</tt> source is similar to testsrc, but supports more pixel formats instead of just <tt>&quot;rgb24&quot;</tt>. This allows using it as an input for other tests without requiring a format conversion. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;yuvtestsrc&quot;</tt> source generates an <font SIZE="-1">YUV</font> test pattern. You should see a y, cb and cr stripe from top to bottom. </p>
<p> The sources accept the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>level</code></p>
<dd> Specify the level of the Hald <font SIZE="-1">CLUT,</font> only available in the <tt>&quot;haldclutsrc&quot;</tt> source. A level of <tt>&quot;N&quot;</tt> generates a picture of <tt>&quot;N*N*N&quot;</tt> by <tt>&quot;N*N*N&quot;</tt> pixels to be used as identity matrix for 3D lookup tables. Each component is coded on a <tt>&quot;1/(N*N)&quot;</tt> scale.</p>
<dt><code>color, c</code></p>
<dd> Specify the color of the source, only available in the <tt>&quot;color&quot;</tt> source. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Color&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>.</p>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the size of the sourced video. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. The default value is <tt>&quot;320x240&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> This option is not available with the <tt>&quot;allrgb&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;allyuv&quot;</tt>, and <tt>&quot;haldclutsrc&quot;</tt> filters.</p>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Specify the frame rate of the sourced video, as the number of frames generated per second. It has to be a string in the format <i>frame_rate_num</i>/<i>frame_rate_den</i>, an integer number, a floating point number or a valid video frame rate abbreviation. The default value is &#8220;25&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>duration, d</code></p>
<dd> Set the duration of the sourced video. See <code>the Time duration section in the <a href="/docs/linux/man/1-ffmpeg-utils/">ffmpeg-utils</a>(1) manual</code> for the accepted syntax. </p>
<p> If not specified, or the expressed duration is negative, the video is supposed to be generated forever. </p>
<p> Since the frame rate is used as time base, all frames including the last one will have their full duration. If the specified duration is not a multiple of the frame duration, it will be rounded up.</p>
<dt><code>sar</code></p>
<dd> Set the sample aspect ratio of the sourced video.</p>
<dt><code>alpha</code></p>
<dd> Specify the alpha (opacity) of the background, only available in the <tt>&quot;testsrc2&quot;</tt> source. The value must be between 0 (fully transparent) and 255 (fully opaque, the default).</p>
<dt><code>decimals, n</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of decimals to show in the timestamp, only available in the <tt>&quot;testsrc&quot;</tt> source. </p>
<p> The displayed timestamp value will correspond to the original timestamp value multiplied by the power of 10 of the specified value. Default value is 0.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a video with a duration of 5.3 seconds, with size 176&#215;144 and a frame rate of 10 frames per second: </p>
<pre>
        testsrc=duration=5.3:size=qcif:rate=10
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> The following graph description will generate a red source with an opacity of 0.2, with size &#8220;qcif&#8221; and a frame rate of 10 frames per second: </p>
<pre>
        color=c=red@0.2:s=qcif:r=10
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> If the input content is to be ignored, <tt>&quot;nullsrc&quot;</tt> can be used. The following command generates noise in the luminance plane by employing the <tt>&quot;geq&quot;</tt> filter: </p>
<pre>
        nullsrc=s=256x256, geq=<a href="//www.systutorials.com/docs/linux/man/1-random/">random</a>(1)*255:128:128
</pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;color&quot;</tt> source supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>c, color</code></p>
<dd> Set the color of the created image. Accepts the same syntax of the corresponding <code>color</code> option.</dl>
<h3>openclsrc</h3>
<p> Generate video using an OpenCL program.</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>source</code></p>
<dd> OpenCL program source file.</p>
<dt><code>kernel</code></p>
<dd> Kernel name in program.</p>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Size of frames to generate. This must be set.</p>
<dt><code>format</code></p>
<dd> Pixel format to use for the generated frames. This must be set.</p>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Number of frames generated every second. Default value is &#8217;25&#8217;.</dl>
<p> For details of how the program loading works, see the <code>program_opencl</code> filter. </p>
<p> Example programs:</p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a colour ramp by setting pixel values from the position of the pixel in the output image. (Note that this will work with all pixel formats, but the generated output will not be the same.) </p>
<p>__kernel void ramp(__write_only image2d_t dst,<br />
                   unsigned int index)<br />
{<br />
    int2 loc = (int2)(get_global_id(0), get_global_id(1));</p>
<p>    float4 val;<br />
    val.xy = val.zw = convert_float2(loc) / convert_float2(get_image_dim(dst));</p>
<p>    write_imagef(dst, loc, val);<br />
}</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate a Sierpinski carpet pattern, panning by a single pixel each frame. </p>
<p>__kernel void sierpinski_carpet(__write_only image2d_t dst,<br />
                                unsigned int index)<br />
{<br />
    int2 loc = (int2)(get_global_id(0), get_global_id(1));</p>
<p>    float4 value = 0.0f;<br />
    int x = loc.x + index;<br />
    int y = loc.y + index;<br />
    while (x &gt; 0 || y &gt; 0) {<br />
        if (x % 3 == 1 &amp;&amp; y % 3 == 1) {<br />
            value = 1.0f;<br />
            break;<br />
        }<br />
        x /= 3;<br />
        y /= 3;<br />
    }</p>
<p>    write_imagef(dst, loc, value);<br />
}
</dl>
<h3>sierpinski</h3>
<p> Generate a Sierpinski carpet/triangle fractal, and randomly pan around. </p>
<p> This source accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Set frame size. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is &#8220;640&#215;480&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set frame rate, expressed as number of frames per second. Default value is &#8220;25&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>seed</code></p>
<dd> Set seed which is used for random panning.</p>
<dt><code>jump</code></p>
<dd> Set max jump for single pan destination. Allowed range is from 1 to 10000.</p>
<dt><code>type</code></p>
<dd> Set fractal type, can be default <tt>&quot;carpet&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;triangle&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<h2>VIDEO SINKS</h2>
<p> Below is a description of the currently available video sinks.</p>
<h3>buffersink</h3>
<p> Buffer video frames, and make them available to the end of the filter graph. </p>
<p> This sink is mainly intended for programmatic use, in particular through the interface defined in <i>libavfilter/buffersink.h</i> or the options system. </p>
<p> It accepts a pointer to an AVBufferSinkContext structure, which defines the incoming buffers&#8217; formats, to be passed as the opaque parameter to <tt>&quot;avfilter_init_filter&quot;</tt> for initialization.</p>
<h3>nullsink</h3>
<p> Null video sink: do absolutely nothing with the input video. It is mainly useful as a template and for use in analysis / debugging tools.</p>
<h2>MULTIMEDIA FILTERS</h2>
<p> Below is a description of the currently available multimedia filters.</p>
<h3>abitscope</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a video output, displaying the audio bit scope. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set frame rate, expressed as number of frames per second. Default value is &#8220;25&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;1024x256&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>colors</code></p>
<dd> Specify list of colors separated by space or by &#8216;|&#8217; which will be used to draw channels. Unrecognized or missing colors will be replaced by white color.</dl>
<h3>adrawgraph</h3>
<p> Draw a graph using input audio metadata. </p>
<p> See <code>drawgraph</code></p>
<h3>agraphmonitor</h3>
<p> See <code>graphmonitor</code>.</p>
<h3>ahistogram</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a video output, displaying the volume histogram. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>dmode</code></p>
<dd> Specify how histogram is calculated. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>single</code></p>
<dd> Use single histogram for all channels.</p>
<dt><code>separate</code></p>
<dd> Use separate histogram for each channel.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;single&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set frame rate, expressed as number of frames per second. Default value is &#8220;25&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;hd720&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set display scale. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> logarithmic</p>
<dt><code>sqrt</code></p>
<dd> square root</p>
<dt><code>cbrt</code></p>
<dd> cubic root</p>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> linear</p>
<dt><code>rlog</code></p>
<dd> reverse logarithmic</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>ascale</code></p>
<dd> Set amplitude scale. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> logarithmic</p>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> linear</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>acount</code></p>
<dd> Set how much frames to accumulate in histogram. Default is 1. Setting this to -1 accumulates all frames.</p>
<dt><code>rheight</code></p>
<dd> Set histogram ratio of window height.</p>
<dt><code>slide</code></p>
<dd> Set sonogram sliding. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>replace</code></p>
<dd> replace old rows with new ones.</p>
<dt><code>scroll</code></p>
<dd> scroll from top to bottom.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;replace&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<h3>aphasemeter</h3>
<p> Measures phase of input audio, which is exported as metadata <tt>&quot;lavfi.aphasemeter.phase&quot;</tt>, representing mean phase of current audio frame. A video output can also be produced and is enabled by default. The audio is passed through as first output. </p>
<p> Audio will be rematrixed to stereo if it has a different channel layout. Phase value is in range <tt>&quot;[-1, 1]&quot;</tt> where <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt> means left and right channels are completely out of phase and <tt>1</tt> means channels are in phase. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options, all related to its video output:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the output frame rate. Default value is <tt>25</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Set the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;800x400&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>rc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bc</code></p>
<dd> Specify the red, green, blue contrast. Default values are <tt>2</tt>, <tt>7</tt> and <tt>1</tt>. Allowed range is <tt>&quot;[0, 255]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>mpc</code></p>
<dd> Set color which will be used for drawing median phase. If color is <tt>&quot;none&quot;</tt> which is default, no median phase value will be drawn.</p>
<dt><code>video</code></p>
<dd> Enable video output. Default is enabled.</dl>
<p> <i>phasing detection</i> </p>
<p> The filter also detects out of phase and mono sequences in stereo streams. It logs the sequence start, end and duration when it lasts longer or as long as the minimum set. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options for this detection:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>phasing</code></p>
<dd> Enable mono and out of phase detection. Default is disabled.</p>
<dt><code>tolerance, t</code></p>
<dd> Set phase tolerance for mono detection, in amplitude ratio. Default is <tt>0</tt>. Allowed range is <tt>&quot;[0, 1]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>angle, a</code></p>
<dd> Set angle threshold for out of phase detection, in degree. Default is <tt>170</tt>. Allowed range is <tt>&quot;[90, 180]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>duration, d</code></p>
<dd> Set mono or out of phase duration until notification, expressed in seconds. Default is <tt>2</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Complete example with <code>ffmpeg</code> to detect 1 second of mono with 0.001 phase tolerance: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i stereo.wav -af aphasemeter=video=0:phasing=1:duration=1:tolerance=0.001 -f null -
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>avectorscope</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a video output, representing the audio vector scope. </p>
<p> The filter is used to measure the difference between channels of stereo audio stream. A monaural signal, consisting of identical left and right signal, results in straight vertical line. Any stereo separation is visible as a deviation from this line, creating a Lissajous figure. If the straight (or deviation from it) but horizontal line appears this indicates that the left and right channels are out of phase. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode, m</code></p>
<dd> Set the vectorscope mode. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lissajous</code></p>
<dd> Lissajous rotated by 45 degrees.</p>
<dt><code>lissajous_xy</code></p>
<dd> Same as above but not rotated.</p>
<dt><code>polar</code></p>
<dd> Shape resembling half of circle.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>lissajous</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Set the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;400x400&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the output frame rate. Default value is <tt>25</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>rc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bc</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>ac</code></p>
<dd> Specify the red, green, blue and alpha contrast. Default values are <tt>40</tt>, <tt>160</tt>, <tt>80</tt> and <tt>255</tt>. Allowed range is <tt>&quot;[0, 255]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>rf</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gf</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bf</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>af</code></p>
<dd> Specify the red, green, blue and alpha fade. Default values are <tt>15</tt>, <tt>10</tt>, <tt>5</tt> and <tt>5</tt>. Allowed range is <tt>&quot;[0, 255]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>zoom</code></p>
<dd> Set the zoom factor. Default value is <tt>1</tt>. Allowed range is <tt>&quot;[0, 10]&quot;</tt>. Values lower than <i>1</i> will auto adjust zoom factor to maximal possible value.</p>
<dt><code>draw</code></p>
<dd> Set the vectorscope drawing mode. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>dot</code></p>
<dd> Draw dot for each sample.</p>
<dt><code>line</code></p>
<dd> Draw line between previous and current sample.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>dot</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Specify amplitude scale of audio samples. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> Linear.</p>
<dt><code>sqrt</code></p>
<dd> Square root.</p>
<dt><code>cbrt</code></p>
<dd> Cubic root.</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> Logarithmic.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>swap</code></p>
<dd> Swap left channel axis with right channel axis.</p>
<dt><code>mirror</code></p>
<dd> Mirror axis.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> No mirror.</p>
<dt><code>x</code></p>
<dd> Mirror only x axis.</p>
<dt><code>y</code></p>
<dd> Mirror only y axis.</p>
<dt><code>xy</code></p>
<dd> Mirror both axis.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Complete example using <code>ffplay</code>: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8216;amovie=input.mp3, asplit [a][out1];<br />
             [a] avectorscope = zoom = 1.3 : rc = 2 : gc = 200 : bc = 10 : rf = 1 : gf = 8 : bf = 7 [out0]&#8217;
</dl>
<h3>bench, abench</h3>
<p> Benchmark part of a filtergraph. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>action</code></p>
<dd> Start or stop a timer. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>start</code></p>
<dd> Get the current time, set it as frame metadata (using the key <tt>&quot;lavfi.bench.start_time&quot;</tt>), and forward the frame to the next filter.</p>
<dt><code>stop</code></p>
<dd> Get the current time and fetch the <tt>&quot;lavfi.bench.start_time&quot;</tt> metadata from the input frame metadata to get the time difference. Time difference, average, maximum and minimum time (respectively <tt>&quot;t&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;avg&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;max&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;min&quot;</tt>) are then printed. The timestamps are expressed in seconds.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Benchmark <code>selectivecolor</code> filter: </p>
<pre>
        bench=start,selectivecolor=reds=-.2 .12 -.49,bench=stop
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>concat</h3>
<p> Concatenate audio and video streams, joining them together one after the other. </p>
<p> The filter works on segments of synchronized video and audio streams. All segments must have the same number of streams of each type, and that will also be the number of streams at output. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of segments. Default is 2.</p>
<dt><code>v</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of output video streams, that is also the number of video streams in each segment. Default is 1.</p>
<dt><code>a</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of output audio streams, that is also the number of audio streams in each segment. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>unsafe</code></p>
<dd> Activate unsafe mode: do not fail if segments have a different format.</dl>
<p> The filter has <i>v</i>+<i>a</i> outputs: first <i>v</i> video outputs, then <i>a</i> audio outputs. </p>
<p> There are <i>n</i>x(<i>v</i>+<i>a</i>) inputs: first the inputs for the first segment, in the same order as the outputs, then the inputs for the second segment, etc. </p>
<p> Related streams do not always have exactly the same duration, for various reasons including codec frame size or sloppy authoring. For that reason, related synchronized streams (e.g. a video and its audio track) should be concatenated at once. The concat filter will use the duration of the longest stream in each segment (except the last one), and if necessary pad shorter audio streams with silence. </p>
<p> For this filter to work correctly, all segments must start at timestamp 0. </p>
<p> All corresponding streams must have the same parameters in all segments; the filtering system will automatically select a common pixel format for video streams, and a common sample format, sample rate and channel layout for audio streams, but other settings, such as resolution, must be converted explicitly by the user. </p>
<p> Different frame rates are acceptable but will result in variable frame rate at output; be sure to configure the output file to handle it. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Concatenate an opening, an episode and an ending, all in bilingual version (video in stream 0, audio in streams 1 and 2): </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i opening.mkv &#8211; i episode.mkv &#8211; i ending.mkv &#8211; filter_complex \<br />
          &#8216;[0:0] [0:1] [0:2] [1:0] [1:1] [1:2] [2:0] [2:1] [2:2] concat = n = 3 : v = 1 : a = 2 [v][a1][a2] &#8216; \<br />
          -map &#8216;[v] &#8216; -map &#8216;[a1] &#8216; -map &#8216;[a2]&#8217; output.mkv</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Concatenate two parts, handling audio and video separately, using the (a)movie sources, and adjusting the resolution: </p>
<p>movie = part1.mp4, scale = 512 : 288 [v1];<br />
amovie = part1.mp4[a1];<br />
movie = part2.mp4, scale = 512 : 288 [v2];<br />
amovie = part2.mp4[a2];<br />
[v1][v2] concat[outv];<br />
[a1][a2] concat = v = 0 : a = 1 [outa]</p>
<p> Note that a desync will happen at the stitch if the audio and video streams do not have exactly the same duration in the first file.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> This filter supports the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>next</code></p>
<dd> Close the current segment and step to the next one</dl>
<h3>ebur128</h3>
<p> <font SIZE="-1">EBU R128</font> scanner filter. This filter takes an audio stream and analyzes its loudness level. By default, it logs a message at a frequency of 10Hz with the Momentary loudness (identified by <tt>&quot;M&quot;</tt>), Short-term loudness (<tt>&quot;S&quot;</tt>), Integrated loudness (<tt>&quot;I&quot;</tt>) and Loudness Range (<tt>&quot;LRA&quot;</tt>). </p>
<p> The filter can only analyze streams which have a sampling rate of 48000 Hz and whose sample format is double-precision floating point. The input stream will be converted to this specification, if needed. Users may need to insert aformat and/or aresample filters after this filter to obtain the original parameters. </p>
<p> The filter also has a video output (see the <i>video</i> option) with a real time graph to observe the loudness evolution. The graphic contains the logged message mentioned above, so it is not printed anymore when this option is set, unless the verbose logging is set. The main graphing area contains the short-term loudness (3 seconds of analysis), and the gauge on the right is for the momentary loudness (400 milliseconds), but can optionally be configured to instead display short-term loudness (see <i>gauge</i>). </p>
<p> The green area marks a +/- 1LU target range around the target loudness (-23LUFS by default, unless modified through <i>target</i>). </p>
<p> More information about the Loudness Recommendation <font SIZE="-1">EBU R128</font> on &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://tech.ebu.ch/loudness">http://tech.ebu.ch/loudness</a></code>&gt;. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>video</code></p>
<dd> Activate the video output. The audio stream is passed unchanged whether this option is set or no. The video stream will be the first output stream if activated. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>size</code></p>
<dd> Set the video size. This option is for video only. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default and minimum resolution is <tt>&quot;640x480&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>meter</code></p>
<dd> Set the <font SIZE="-1">EBU</font> scale meter. Default is <tt>9</tt>. Common values are <tt>9</tt> and <tt>18</tt>, respectively for <font SIZE="-1">EBU</font> scale meter +9 and <font SIZE="-1">EBU</font> scale meter +18. Any other integer value between this range is allowed.</p>
<dt><code>metadata</code></p>
<dd> Set metadata injection. If set to <tt>1</tt>, the audio input will be segmented into 100ms output frames, each of them containing various loudness information in metadata. All the metadata keys are prefixed with <tt>&quot;lavfi.r128.&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>framelog</code></p>
<dd> Force the frame logging level. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>info</code></p>
<dd> information logging level</p>
<dt><code>verbose</code></p>
<dd> verbose logging level</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> By default, the logging level is set to <i>info</i>. If the <code>video</code> or the <code>metadata</code> options are set, it switches to <i>verbose</i>.</dl>
<dt><code>peak</code></p>
<dd> Set peak mode(s). </p>
<p> Available modes can be cumulated (the option is a <tt>&quot;flag&quot;</tt> type). Possible values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> Disable any peak mode (default).</p>
<dt><code>sample</code></p>
<dd> Enable sample-peak mode. </p>
<p> Simple peak mode looking for the higher sample value. It logs a message for sample-peak (identified by <tt>&quot;SPK&quot;</tt>).</p>
<dt><code>true</code></p>
<dd> Enable true-peak mode. </p>
<p> If enabled, the peak lookup is done on an over-sampled version of the input stream for better peak accuracy. It logs a message for true-peak. (identified by <tt>&quot;TPK&quot;</tt>) and true-peak per frame (identified by <tt>&quot;FTPK&quot;</tt>). This mode requires a build with <tt>&quot;libswresample&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>dualmono</code></p>
<dd> Treat mono input files as &#8220;dual mono&#8221;. If a mono file is intended for playback on a stereo system, its <font SIZE="-1">EBU R128</font> measurement will be perceptually incorrect. If set to <tt>&quot;true&quot;</tt>, this option will compensate for this effect. Multi-channel input files are not affected by this option.</p>
<dt><code>panlaw</code></p>
<dd> Set a specific pan law to be used for the measurement of dual mono files. This parameter is optional, and has a default value of -3.01dB.</p>
<dt><code>target</code></p>
<dd> Set a specific target level (in <font SIZE="-1">LUFS</font>) used as relative zero in the visualization. This parameter is optional and has a default value of -23LUFS as specified by <font SIZE="-1">EBU R128.</font> However, material published online may prefer a level of -16LUFS (e.g. for use with podcasts or video platforms).</p>
<dt><code>gauge</code></p>
<dd> Set the value displayed by the gauge. Valid values are <tt>&quot;momentary&quot;</tt> and s <tt>&quot;shortterm&quot;</tt>. By default the momentary value will be used, but in certain scenarios it may be more useful to observe the short term value instead (e.g. live mixing).</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Sets the display scale for the loudness. Valid parameters are <tt>&quot;absolute&quot;</tt> (in <font SIZE="-1">LUFS</font>) or <tt>&quot;relative&quot;</tt> (<font SIZE="-1">LU</font>) relative to the target. This only affects the video output, not the summary or continuous log output.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Real-time graph using <code>ffplay</code>, with a <font SIZE="-1">EBU</font> scale meter +18: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8211; i &#8220;amovie=input.mp3,ebur128=video=1:meter=18 [out0][out1]&#8221;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Run an analysis with <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -nostats -i input.mp3 -filter_complex ebur128 -f null -
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>interleave, ainterleave</h3>
<p> Temporally interleave frames from several inputs. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;interleave&quot;</tt> works with video inputs, <tt>&quot;ainterleave&quot;</tt> with audio. </p>
<p> These filters read frames from several inputs and send the oldest queued frame to the output. </p>
<p> Input streams must have well defined, monotonically increasing frame timestamp values. </p>
<p> In order to submit one frame to output, these filters need to enqueue at least one frame for each input, so they cannot work in case one input is not yet terminated and will not receive incoming frames. </p>
<p> For example consider the case when one input is a <tt>&quot;select&quot;</tt> filter which always drops input frames. The <tt>&quot;interleave&quot;</tt> filter will keep reading from that input, but it will never be able to send new frames to output until the input sends an end-of-stream signal. </p>
<p> Also, depending on inputs synchronization, the filters will drop frames in case one input receives more frames than the other ones, and the queue is already filled. </p>
<p> These filters accept the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>nb_inputs, n</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of different inputs, it is 2 by default.</p>
<dt><code>duration</code></p>
<dd> How to determine the end-of-stream.</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>longest</code></p>
<dd> The duration of the longest input. (default)</p>
<dt><code>shortest</code></p>
<dd> The duration of the shortest input.</p>
<dt><code>first</code></p>
<dd> The duration of the first input.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Interleave frames belonging to different streams using <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<p>ffmpeg &#8211; i bambi.avi &#8211; i pr0n.mkv &#8211; filter_complex &#8220;[0:v][1:v] interleave&#8221; out.avi</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Add flickering blur effect: </p>
<p>select = &#8216;if(gt(random(0), 0.2), 1, 2)&#8217; : n = 2 [tmp], boxblur = 2 : 2, [tmp] interleave
</dl>
<h3>metadata, ametadata</h3>
<p> Manipulate frame metadata. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set mode of operation of the filter. </p>
<p> Can be one of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>select</code></p>
<dd> If both <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;key&quot;</tt> is set, select frames which have such metadata. If only <tt>&quot;key&quot;</tt> is set, select every frame that has such key in metadata.</p>
<dt><code>add</code></p>
<dd> Add new metadata <tt>&quot;key&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt>. If key is already available do nothing.</p>
<dt><code>modify</code></p>
<dd> Modify value of already present key.</p>
<dt><code>delete</code></p>
<dd> If <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> is set, delete only keys that have such value. Otherwise, delete key. If <tt>&quot;key&quot;</tt> is not set, delete all metadata values in the frame.</p>
<dt><code>print</code></p>
<dd> Print key and its value if metadata was found. If <tt>&quot;key&quot;</tt> is not set print all metadata values available in frame.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>key</code></p>
<dd> Set key used with all modes. Must be set for all modes except <tt>&quot;print&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;delete&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>value</code></p>
<dd> Set metadata value which will be used. This option is mandatory for <tt>&quot;modify&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;add&quot;</tt> mode.</p>
<dt><code>function</code></p>
<dd> Which function to use when comparing metadata value and <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> Can be one of following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>same_str</code></p>
<dd> Values are interpreted as strings, returns true if metadata value is same as <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>starts_with</code></p>
<dd> Values are interpreted as strings, returns true if metadata value starts with the <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> option string.</p>
<dt><code>less</code></p>
<dd> Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if metadata value is less than <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>equal</code></p>
<dd> Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> is equal with metadata value.</p>
<dt><code>greater</code></p>
<dd> Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if metadata value is greater than <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>expr</code></p>
<dd> Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if expression from option <tt>&quot;expr&quot;</tt> evaluates to true.</p>
<dt><code>ends_with</code></p>
<dd> Values are interpreted as strings, returns true if metadata value ends with the <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> option string.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>expr</code></p>
<dd> Set expression which is used when <tt>&quot;function&quot;</tt> is set to <tt>&quot;expr&quot;</tt>. The expression is evaluated through the eval <font SIZE="-1">API</font> and can contain the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VALUE1</code></font></p>
<dd> Float representation of <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> from metadata key.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VALUE2</code></font></p>
<dd> Float representation of <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> as supplied by user in <tt>&quot;value&quot;</tt> option.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>file</code></p>
<dd> If specified in <tt>&quot;print&quot;</tt> mode, output is written to the named file. Instead of plain filename any writable url can be specified. Filename &#8220;-&#8221; is a shorthand for standard output. If <tt>&quot;file&quot;</tt> option is not set, output is written to the log with <font SIZE="-1">AV_LOG_INFO</font> loglevel.</p>
<dt><code>direct</code></p>
<dd> Reduces buffering in print mode when output is written to a <font SIZE="-1">URL</font> set using <i>file</i>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Print all metadata values for frames with key <tt>&quot;lavfi.signalstats.YDIF&quot;</tt> with values between 0 and 1. </p>
<pre>
        signalstats,metadata=print:key=lavfi.signalstats.YDIF:value=0:function=expr:expr='between(VALUE1,0,1)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Print silencedetect output to file <i>metadata.txt</i>. </p>
<pre>
        silencedetect,ametadata=mode=print:file=metadata.txt
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Direct all metadata to a pipe with file descriptor 4. </p>
<pre>
        metadata=mode=print:file='pipe\:4'
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>perms, aperms</h3>
<p> Set read/write permissions for the output frames. </p>
<p> These filters are mainly aimed at developers to test direct path in the following filter in the filtergraph. </p>
<p> The filters accept the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Select the permissions mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>none</code></p>
<dd> Do nothing. This is the default.</p>
<dt><code>ro</code></p>
<dd> Set all the output frames read-only.</p>
<dt><code>rw</code></p>
<dd> Set all the output frames directly writable.</p>
<dt><code>toggle</code></p>
<dd> Make the frame read-only if writable, and writable if read-only.</p>
<dt><code>random</code></p>
<dd> Set each output frame read-only or writable randomly.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>seed</code></p>
<dd> Set the seed for the <i>random</i> mode, must be an integer included between <tt>0</tt> and <tt>&quot;UINT32_MAX&quot;</tt>. If not specified, or if explicitly set to <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt>, the filter will try to use a good random seed on a best effort basis.</dl>
<p> Note: in case of auto-inserted filter between the permission filter and the following one, the permission might not be received as expected in that following filter. Inserting a <code>format</code> or <code>aformat</code> filter before the perms/aperms filter can avoid this problem.</p>
<h3>realtime, arealtime</h3>
<p> Slow down filtering to match real time approximately. </p>
<p> These filters will pause the filtering for a variable amount of time to match the output rate with the input timestamps. They are similar to the <code>re</code> option to <tt>&quot;ffmpeg&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> They accept the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>limit</code></p>
<dd> Time limit for the pauses. Any pause longer than that will be considered a timestamp discontinuity and reset the timer. Default is 2 seconds.</p>
<dt><code>speed</code></p>
<dd> Speed factor for processing. The value must be a float larger than zero. Values larger than 1.0 will result in faster than realtime processing, smaller will slow processing down. The <i>limit</i> is automatically adapted accordingly. Default is 1.0. </p>
<p> A processing speed faster than what is possible without these filters cannot be achieved.</dl>
<h3>select, aselect</h3>
<p> Select frames to pass in output. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>expr, e</code></p>
<dd> Set expression, which is evaluated for each input frame. </p>
<p> If the expression is evaluated to zero, the frame is discarded. </p>
<p> If the evaluation result is negative or NaN, the frame is sent to the first output; otherwise it is sent to the output with index <tt>&quot;ceil(val)-1&quot;</tt>, assuming that the input index starts from 0. </p>
<p> For example a value of <tt>1.2</tt> corresponds to the output with index <tt>&quot;ceil(1.2)-1 = 2-1 = 1&quot;</tt>, that is the second output.</p>
<dt><code>outputs, n</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of outputs. The output to which to send the selected frame is based on the result of the evaluation. Default value is 1.</dl>
<p> The expression can contain the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> The (sequential) number of the filtered frame, starting from 0.</p>
<dt><code>selected_n</code></p>
<dd> The (sequential) number of the selected frame, starting from 0.</p>
<dt><code>prev_selected_n</code></p>
<dd> The sequential number of the last selected frame. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>TB</code></font></p>
<dd> The timebase of the input timestamps.</p>
<dt><code>pts</code></p>
<dd> The <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> (Presentation TimeStamp) of the filtered video frame, expressed in <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>TB</i></font><i></i> units. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> The <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> of the filtered video frame, expressed in seconds. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><code>prev_pts</code></p>
<dd> The <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> of the previously filtered video frame. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><code>prev_selected_pts</code></p>
<dd> The <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> of the last previously filtered video frame. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><code>prev_selected_t</code></p>
<dd> The <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> of the last previously selected video frame, expressed in seconds. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><code>start_pts</code></p>
<dd> The <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> of the first video frame in the video. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><code>start_t</code></p>
<dd> The time of the first video frame in the video. It&#8217;s <font SIZE="-1">NAN</font> if undefined.</p>
<dt><code>pict_type</code> <i>(video only)</i></p>
<dd> The type of the filtered frame. It can assume one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>I</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>P</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>B</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>S</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SI</code></font></p>
<dd>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SP</code></font></p>
<dd>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>BI</code></font></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>interlace_type</code> <i>(video only)</i></p>
<dd> The frame interlace type. It can assume one of the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PROGRESSIVE</code></font></p>
<dd> The frame is progressive (not interlaced).</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>TOPFIRST</code></font></p>
<dd> The frame is top-field-first.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>BOTTOMFIRST</code></font></p>
<dd> The frame is bottom-field-first.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>consumed_sample_n</code> <i>(audio only)</i></p>
<dd> the number of selected samples before the current frame</p>
<dt><code>samples_n</code> <i>(audio only)</i></p>
<dd> the number of samples in the current frame</p>
<dt><code>sample_rate</code> <i>(audio only)</i></p>
<dd> the input sample rate</p>
<dt><code>key</code></p>
<dd> This is 1 if the filtered frame is a key-frame, 0 otherwise.</p>
<dt><code>pos</code></p>
<dd> the position in the file of the filtered frame, -1 if the information is not available (e.g. for synthetic video)</p>
<dt><code>scene</code> <i>(video only)</i></p>
<dd> value between 0 and 1 to indicate a new scene; a low value reflects a low probability for the current frame to introduce a new scene, while a higher value means the current frame is more likely to be one (see the example below)</p>
<dt><code>concatdec_select</code></p>
<dd> The concat demuxer can select only part of a concat input file by setting an inpoint and an outpoint, but the output packets may not be entirely contained in the selected interval. By using this variable, it is possible to skip frames generated by the concat demuxer which are not exactly contained in the selected interval. </p>
<p> This works by comparing the frame pts against the <i>lavf.concat.start_time</i> and the <i>lavf.concat.duration</i> packet metadata values which are also present in the decoded frames. </p>
<p> The <i>concatdec_select</i> variable is -1 if the frame pts is at least start_time and either the duration metadata is missing or the frame pts is less than start_time + duration, 0 otherwise, and NaN if the start_time metadata is missing. </p>
<p> That basically means that an input frame is selected if its pts is within the interval set by the concat demuxer.</dl>
<p> The default value of the select expression is &#8220;1&#8221;. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select all frames in input: </p>
<pre>
        select
</pre>
<p> The example above is the same as: </p>
<pre>
        select=1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Skip all frames: </p>
<pre>
        select=0
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select only I-frames: </p>
<pre>
        select='eq(pict_type\,I)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select one frame every 100: </p>
<pre>
        select='not(mod(n\,100))'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select only frames contained in the 10-20 time interval: </p>
<pre>
        select=between(t\,10\,20)
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select only I-frames contained in the 10-20 time interval: </p>
<pre>
        select=between(t\,10\,20)*eq(pict_type\,I)
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select frames with a minimum distance of 10 seconds: </p>
<pre>
        select='isnan(prev_selected_t)+gte(t-prev_selected_t\,10)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Use aselect to select only audio frames with samples number &gt; 100: </p>
<pre>
        aselect='gt(samples_n\,100)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Create a mosaic of the first scenes: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i video.avi -vf select='gt(scene\,0.4)',scale=160:120,tile -frames:v 1 preview.png
</pre>
<p> Comparing <i>scene</i> against a value between 0.3 and 0.5 is generally a sane choice.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Send even and odd frames to separate outputs, and compose them: </p>
<p>select = n = 2 : e = &#8216;mod(n, 2)+1&#8242;[odd][even];<br />
[odd] pad = h = 2 * ih[tmp];<br />
[tmp][even] overlay = y = h</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Select useful frames from an ffconcat file which is using inpoints and outpoints but where the source files are not intra frame only. </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -copyts -vsync 0 -segment_time_metadata 1 -i input.ffconcat -vf select=concatdec_select -af aselect=concatdec_select output.avi
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>sendcmd, asendcmd</h3>
<p> Send commands to filters in the filtergraph. </p>
<p> These filters read commands to be sent to other filters in the filtergraph. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;sendcmd&quot;</tt> must be inserted between two video filters, <tt>&quot;asendcmd&quot;</tt> must be inserted between two audio filters, but apart from that they act the same way. </p>
<p> The specification of commands can be provided in the filter arguments with the <i>commands</i> option, or in a file specified by the <i>filename</i> option. </p>
<p> These filters accept the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>commands, c</code></p>
<dd> Set the commands to be read and sent to the other filters.</p>
<dt><code>filename, f</code></p>
<dd> Set the filename of the commands to be read and sent to the other filters.</dl>
<p> <i>Commands syntax</i> </p>
<p> A commands description consists of a sequence of interval specifications, comprising a list of commands to be executed when a particular event related to that interval occurs. The occurring event is typically the current frame time entering or leaving a given time interval. </p>
<p> An interval is specified by the following syntax: </p>
<pre>
        &lt;START&gt;[-&lt;END&gt;] &lt;COMMANDS&gt;;
</pre>
<p> The time interval is specified by the <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>START</i></font><i></i> and <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>END</i></font><i></i> times. <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>END</i></font><i></i> is optional and defaults to the maximum time. </p>
<p> The current frame time is considered within the specified interval if it is included in the interval [<i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>START</i></font><i></i>, <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>END</i></font><i></i>), that is when the time is greater or equal to <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>START</i></font><i></i> and is lesser than <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>END</i></font><i></i>. </p>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>COMMANDS</i></font><i></i> consists of a sequence of one or more command specifications, separated by &#8220;,&#8221;, relating to that interval. The syntax of a command specification is given by: </p>
<pre>
        [&lt;FLAGS&gt;] &lt;TARGET&gt; &lt;COMMAND&gt; &lt;ARG&gt;
</pre>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>FLAGS</i></font><i></i> is optional and specifies the type of events relating to the time interval which enable sending the specified command, and must be a non-null sequence of identifier flags separated by &#8220;+&#8221; or &#8220;|&#8221; and enclosed between &#8220;[&#8221; and &#8220;]&#8221;. </p>
<p> The following flags are recognized:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>enter</code></p>
<dd> The command is sent when the current frame timestamp enters the specified interval. In other words, the command is sent when the previous frame timestamp was not in the given interval, and the current is.</p>
<dt><code>leave</code></p>
<dd> The command is sent when the current frame timestamp leaves the specified interval. In other words, the command is sent when the previous frame timestamp was in the given interval, and the current is not.</p>
<dt><code>expr</code></p>
<dd> The command <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>ARG</i></font><i></i> is interpreted as expression and result of expression is passed as <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>ARG</i></font><i></i>. </p>
<p> The expression is evaluated through the eval <font SIZE="-1">API</font> and can contain the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>POS</code></font></p>
<dd> Original position in the file of the frame, or undefined if undefined for the current frame.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PTS</code></font></p>
<dd> The presentation timestamp in input.</p>
<dt><code>N</code></p>
<dd> The count of the input frame for video or audio, starting from 0.</p>
<dt><code>T</code></p>
<dd> The time in seconds of the current frame.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>TS</code></font></p>
<dd> The start time in seconds of the current command interval.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>TE</code></font></p>
<dd> The end time in seconds of the current command interval.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>TI</code></font></p>
<dd> The interpolated time of the current command interval, <font SIZE="-1">TI</font> = (T &#8211; <font SIZE="-1">TS</font>) / (<font SIZE="-1">TE</font> &#8211; <font SIZE="-1">TS</font>).</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<p> If <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>FLAGS</i></font><i></i> is not specified, a default value of <tt>&quot;[enter]&quot;</tt> is assumed. </p>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>TARGET</i></font><i></i> specifies the target of the command, usually the name of the filter class or a specific filter instance name. </p>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>COMMAND</i></font><i></i> specifies the name of the command for the target filter. </p>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>ARG</i></font><i></i> is optional and specifies the optional list of argument for the given <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>COMMAND</i></font><i></i>. </p>
<p> Between one interval specification and another, whitespaces, or sequences of characters starting with <tt>&quot;#&quot;</tt> until the end of line, are ignored and can be used to annotate comments. </p>
<p> A simplified <font SIZE="-1">BNF</font> description of the commands specification syntax follows: </p>
<p>        &lt;COMMAND_FLAG&gt;  ::= &#8220;enter&#8221; | &#8220;leave&#8221;<br />
        &lt;COMMAND_FLAGS&gt; ::= &lt;COMMAND_FLAG&gt; [(+|&#8221;|&#8221;)&lt;COMMAND_FLAG&gt;]<br />
        &lt;COMMAND&gt;       ::= [&#8220;[&#8221; &lt;COMMAND_FLAGS&gt; &#8220;]&#8221;] &lt;TARGET&gt; &lt;COMMAND&gt; [&lt;ARG&gt;]<br />
        &lt;COMMANDS&gt;      ::= &lt;COMMAND&gt; [,&lt;COMMANDS&gt;]<br />
        &lt;INTERVAL&gt;      ::= &lt;START&gt;[-&lt;END&gt;] &lt;COMMANDS&gt;<br />
        &lt;INTERVALS&gt;     ::= &lt;INTERVAL&gt;[;&lt;INTERVALS&gt;]</p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Specify audio tempo change at second 4: </p>
<pre>
        asendcmd=c='4.0 atempo tempo 1.5',atempo
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Target a specific filter instance: </p>
<pre>
        asendcmd=c='4.0 atempo@my tempo 1.5',atempo@my
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Specify a list of drawtext and hue commands in a file. </p>
<p>#show text in the interval 5 &#8211; 10<br />
5.0 &#8211; 10.0 [enter] drawtext reinit &#8216;fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=hello world&#8217;,<br />
    [leave] drawtext reinit &#8216;fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=&#8217;;</p>
<p>#desaturate the image in the interval 15 &#8211; 20<br />
15.0 &#8211; 20.0 [enter] hue s 0,<br />
    [enter] drawtext reinit &#8216;fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=nocolor&#8217;,<br />
    [leave] hue s 1,<br />
    [leave] drawtext reinit &#8216;fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=color&#8217;;</p>
<p>#apply an exponential saturation fade &#8211; out effect, starting from time 25<br />
25 [enter] hue s exp(25 &#8211; t)</p>
<p> A filtergraph allowing to read and process the above command list stored in a file <i>test.cmd</i>, can be specified with: </p>
<pre>
        sendcmd=f=test.cmd,drawtext=fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text='',hue
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>setpts, asetpts</h3>
<p> Change the <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> (presentation timestamp) of the input frames. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;setpts&quot;</tt> works on video frames, <tt>&quot;asetpts&quot;</tt> on audio frames. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>expr</code></p>
<dd> The expression which is evaluated for each frame to construct its timestamp.</dl>
<p> The expression is evaluated through the eval <font SIZE="-1">API</font> and can contain the following constants:</p>
<dl>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>FRAME_RATE, FR</code></font></p>
<dd> frame rate, only defined for constant frame-rate video</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PTS</code></font></p>
<dd> The presentation timestamp in input</p>
<dt><code>N</code></p>
<dd> The count of the input frame for video or the number of consumed samples, not including the current frame for audio, starting from 0.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>NB_CONSUMED_SAMPLES</code></font></p>
<dd> The number of consumed samples, not including the current frame (only audio)</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>NB_SAMPLES, S</code></font></p>
<dd> The number of samples in the current frame (only audio)</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>SAMPLE_RATE, SR</code></font></p>
<dd> The audio sample rate.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>STARTPTS</code></font></p>
<dd> The <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> of the first frame.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>STARTT</code></font></p>
<dd> the time in seconds of the first frame</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>INTERLACED</code></font></p>
<dd> State whether the current frame is interlaced.</p>
<dt><code>T</code></p>
<dd> the time in seconds of the current frame</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>POS</code></font></p>
<dd> original position in the file of the frame, or undefined if undefined for the current frame</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PREV_INPTS</code></font></p>
<dd> The previous input <font SIZE="-1">PTS.</font></p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PREV_INT</code></font></p>
<dd> previous input time in seconds</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PREV_OUTPTS</code></font></p>
<dd> The previous output <font SIZE="-1">PTS.</font></p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PREV_OUTT</code></font></p>
<dd> previous output time in seconds</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>RTCTIME</code></font></p>
<dd> The wallclock (<font SIZE="-1">RTC</font>) time in microseconds. This is deprecated, use <code>time</code>(0) instead.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>RTCSTART</code></font></p>
<dd> The wallclock (<font SIZE="-1">RTC</font>) time at the start of the movie in microseconds.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>TB</code></font></p>
<dd> The timebase of the input timestamps.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Start counting <font SIZE="-1">PTS</font> from zero </p>
<pre>
        setpts=PTS-STARTPTS
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply fast motion effect: </p>
<pre>
        setpts=0.5*PTS
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply slow motion effect: </p>
<pre>
        setpts=2.0*PTS
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set fixed rate of 25 frames per second: </p>
<pre>
        setpts=N/(25*TB)
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set fixed rate 25 fps with some jitter: </p>
<pre>
        setpts='1/(25*TB) * (N + 0.05 * sin(N*2*PI/25))'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Apply an offset of 10 seconds to the input <font SIZE="-1">PTS:</font> </p>
<pre>
        setpts=PTS+10/TB
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate timestamps from a &#8220;live source&#8221; and rebase onto the current timebase: </p>
<pre>
        setpts='(RTCTIME - RTCSTART) / (TB * 1000000)'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Generate timestamps by counting samples: </p>
<pre>
        asetpts=N/SR/TB
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>setrange</h3>
<p> Force color range for the output video frame. </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;setrange&quot;</tt> filter marks the color range property for the output frames. It does not change the input frame, but only sets the corresponding property, which affects how the frame is treated by following filters. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>range</code></p>
<dd> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>auto</code></p>
<dd> Keep the same color range property.</p>
<dt><code>unspecified, unknown</code></p>
<dd> Set the color range as unspecified.</p>
<dt><code>limited, tv, mpeg</code></p>
<dd> Set the color range as limited.</p>
<dt><code>full, pc, jpeg</code></p>
<dd> Set the color range as full.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<h3>settb, asettb</h3>
<p> Set the timebase to use for the output frames timestamps. It is mainly useful for testing timebase configuration. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>expr, tb</code></p>
<dd> The expression which is evaluated into the output timebase.</dl>
<p> The value for <code>tb</code> is an arithmetic expression representing a rational. The expression can contain the constants &#8220;<font SIZE="-1">AVTB&#8221;</font> (the default timebase), &#8220;intb&#8221; (the input timebase) and &#8220;sr&#8221; (the sample rate, audio only). Default value is &#8220;intb&#8221;. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set the timebase to 1/25: </p>
<pre>
        settb=expr=1/25
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set the timebase to 1/10: </p>
<pre>
        settb=expr=0.1
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set the timebase to 1001/1000: </p>
<pre>
        settb=1+0.001
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set the timebase to 2*intb: </p>
<pre>
        settb=2*intb
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Set the default timebase value: </p>
<pre>
        settb=AVTB
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>showcqt</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a video output representing frequency spectrum logarithmically using Brown-Puckette constant Q transform algorithm with direct frequency domain coefficient calculation (but the transform itself is not really constant Q, instead the Q factor is actually variable/clamped), with musical tone scale, from E0 to D#10. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. It must be even. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;1920x1080&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>fps, rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the output frame rate. Default value is <tt>25</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>bar_h</code></p>
<dd> Set the bargraph height. It must be even. Default value is <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt> which computes the bargraph height automatically.</p>
<dt><code>axis_h</code></p>
<dd> Set the axis height. It must be even. Default value is <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt> which computes the axis height automatically.</p>
<dt><code>sono_h</code></p>
<dd> Set the sonogram height. It must be even. Default value is <tt>&quot;-1&quot;</tt> which computes the sonogram height automatically.</p>
<dt><code>fullhd</code></p>
<dd> Set the fullhd resolution. This option is deprecated, use <i>size</i>, <i>s</i> instead. Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>sono_v, volume</code></p>
<dd> Specify the sonogram volume expression. It can contain variables:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>bar_v</code></p>
<dd> the <i>bar_v</i> evaluated expression</p>
<dt><code>frequency, freq, f</code></p>
<dd> the frequency where it is evaluated</p>
<dt><code>timeclamp, tc</code></p>
<dd> the value of <i>timeclamp</i> option</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>a_weighting(f)</code></p>
<dd> A-weighting of equal loudness</p>
<dt><code>b_weighting(f)</code></p>
<dd> B-weighting of equal loudness</p>
<dt><code>c_weighting(f)</code></p>
<dd> C-weighting of equal loudness.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>16</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>bar_v, volume2</code></p>
<dd> Specify the bargraph volume expression. It can contain variables:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>sono_v</code></p>
<dd> the <i>sono_v</i> evaluated expression</p>
<dt><code>frequency, freq, f</code></p>
<dd> the frequency where it is evaluated</p>
<dt><code>timeclamp, tc</code></p>
<dd> the value of <i>timeclamp</i> option</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>a_weighting(f)</code></p>
<dd> A-weighting of equal loudness</p>
<dt><code>b_weighting(f)</code></p>
<dd> B-weighting of equal loudness</p>
<dt><code>c_weighting(f)</code></p>
<dd> C-weighting of equal loudness.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;sono_v&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>sono_g, gamma</code></p>
<dd> Specify the sonogram gamma. Lower gamma makes the spectrum more contrast, higher gamma makes the spectrum having more range. Default value is <tt>3</tt>. Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[1, 7]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>bar_g, gamma2</code></p>
<dd> Specify the bargraph gamma. Default value is <tt>1</tt>. Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[1, 7]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>bar_t</code></p>
<dd> Specify the bargraph transparency level. Lower value makes the bargraph sharper. Default value is <tt>1</tt>. Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[0, 1]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>timeclamp, tc</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transform timeclamp. At low frequency, there is trade-off between accuracy in time domain and frequency domain. If timeclamp is lower, event in time domain is represented more accurately (such as fast bass drum), otherwise event in frequency domain is represented more accurately (such as bass guitar). Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[0.002, 1]&quot;</tt>. Default value is <tt>0.17</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>attack</code></p>
<dd> Set attack time in seconds. The default is <tt>0</tt> (disabled). Otherwise, it limits future samples by applying asymmetric windowing in time domain, useful when low latency is required. Accepted range is <tt>&quot;[0, 1]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>basefreq</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transform base frequency. Default value is <tt>20.01523126408007475</tt>, which is frequency 50 cents below E0. Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[10, 100000]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>endfreq</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transform end frequency. Default value is <tt>20495.59681441799654</tt>, which is frequency 50 cents above D#10. Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[10, 100000]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>coeffclamp</code></p>
<dd> This option is deprecated and ignored.</p>
<dt><code>tlength</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transform length in time domain. Use this option to control accuracy trade-off between time domain and frequency domain at every frequency sample. It can contain variables:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>frequency, freq, f</code></p>
<dd> the frequency where it is evaluated</p>
<dt><code>timeclamp, tc</code></p>
<dd> the value of <i>timeclamp</i> option.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;384*tc/(384+tc*f)&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>count</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transform count for every video frame. Default value is <tt>6</tt>. Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[1, 30]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>fcount</code></p>
<dd> Specify the transform count for every single pixel. Default value is <tt>0</tt>, which makes it computed automatically. Acceptable range is <tt>&quot;[0, 10]&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>fontfile</code></p>
<dd> Specify font file for use with freetype to draw the axis. If not specified, use embedded font. Note that drawing with font file or embedded font is not implemented with custom <i>basefreq</i> and <i>endfreq</i>, use <i>axisfile</i> option instead.</p>
<dt><code>font</code></p>
<dd> Specify fontconfig pattern. This has lower priority than <i>fontfile</i>. The <tt>&quot;:&quot;</tt> in the pattern may be replaced by <tt>&quot;|&quot;</tt> to avoid unnecessary escaping.</p>
<dt><code>fontcolor</code></p>
<dd> Specify font color expression. This is arithmetic expression that should return integer value 0xRRGGBB. It can contain variables:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>frequency, freq, f</code></p>
<dd> the frequency where it is evaluated</p>
<dt><code>timeclamp, tc</code></p>
<dd> the value of <i>timeclamp</i> option</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> and functions:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>midi(f)</code></p>
<dd> midi number of frequency f, some midi numbers: E0(16), C1(24), C2(36), A4(69)</p>
<dt><code>r(x), g(x), b(x)</code></p>
<dd> red, green, and blue value of intensity x.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;st(0, (midi(f)-59.5)/12); st(1, if(between(ld(0),0,1), 0.5-0.5*cos(2*PI*ld(0)), 0)); r(<a href="/docs/linux/man/1-1-ld/">1-ld</a>(1)) + b(<a href="/docs/linux/man/1-ld/">ld</a>(1))&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>axisfile</code></p>
<dd> Specify image file to draw the axis. This option override <i>fontfile</i> and <i>fontcolor</i> option.</p>
<dt><code>axis, text</code></p>
<dd> Enable/disable drawing text to the axis. If it is set to <tt>0</tt>, drawing to the axis is disabled, ignoring <i>fontfile</i> and <i>axisfile</i> option. Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>csp</code></p>
<dd> Set colorspace. The accepted values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>unspecified</code></p>
<dd> Unspecified (default)</p>
<dt><code>bt709</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">BT.709</font></p>
<dt><code>fcc</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">FCC</font></p>
<dt><code>bt470bg</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">BT.470BG</font> or <font SIZE="-1">BT.601-6 625</font></p>
<dt><code>smpte170m</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">SMPTE-170M</font> or <font SIZE="-1">BT.601-6 525</font></p>
<dt><code>smpte240m</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">SMPTE-240M</font></p>
<dt><code>bt2020ncl</code></p>
<dd> <font SIZE="-1">BT.2020</font> with non-constant luminance</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>cscheme</code></p>
<dd> Set spectrogram color scheme. This is list of floating point values with format <tt>&quot;left_r|left_g|left_b|right_r|right_g|right_b&quot;</tt>. The default is <tt>&quot;1|0.5|0|0|0.5|1&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Playing audio while showing the spectrum: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8216;amovie=a.mp3, asplit [a][out1]; [a] showcqt [out0]&#8217;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Same as above, but with frame rate 30 fps: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8216;amovie=a.mp3, asplit [a][out1]; [a] showcqt=fps=30:count=5 [out0]&#8217;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Playing at 1280&#215;720: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8216;amovie=a.mp3, asplit [a][out1]; [a] showcqt=s=1280&#215;720:count=4 [out0]&#8217;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Disable sonogram display: </p>
<pre>
        sono_h=0
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> A1 and its harmonics: A1, A2, (near)E3, A3: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8216;aevalsrc=0.1*sin(2*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(4*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(6*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(8*PI*55*t), asplit[a][out1];<br />
[a] showcqt[out0]&#8217;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Same as above, but with more accuracy in frequency domain: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8216;aevalsrc=0.1*sin(2*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(4*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(6*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(8*PI*55*t), asplit[a][out1];<br />
[a] showcqt = timeclamp = 0.5 [out0]&#8217;</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Custom volume: </p>
<pre>
        bar_v=10:sono_v=bar_v*a_weighting(f)
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Custom gamma, now spectrum is linear to the amplitude. </p>
<pre>
        bar_g=2:sono_g=2
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Custom tlength equation: </p>
<pre>
        tc=0.33:tlength='st(0,0.17); 384*tc / (384 / ld(0) + tc*f /(1-ld(0))) + 384*tc / (tc*f / ld(0) + 384 /(1-ld(0)))'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Custom fontcolor and fontfile, C-note is colored green, others are colored blue: </p>
<pre>
        fontcolor='if(mod(floor(midi(f)+0.5),12), 0x0000FF, <a href="//www.systutorials.com/docs/linux/man/1-g/">g</a>(1))':fontfile=myfont.ttf
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Custom font using fontconfig: </p>
<pre>
        font='Courier New,Monospace,mono|bold'
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Custom frequency range with custom axis using image file: </p>
<pre>
        axisfile=myaxis.png:basefreq=40:endfreq=10000
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>showfreqs</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to video output representing the audio power spectrum. Audio amplitude is on Y-axis while frequency is on X-axis. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify size of video. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default is <tt>&quot;1024x512&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set display mode. This set how each frequency bin will be represented. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>line</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bar</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>dot</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;bar&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>ascale</code></p>
<dd> Set amplitude scale. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> Linear scale.</p>
<dt><code>sqrt</code></p>
<dd> Square root scale.</p>
<dt><code>cbrt</code></p>
<dd> Cubic root scale.</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> Logarithmic scale.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>fscale</code></p>
<dd> Set frequency scale. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> Linear scale.</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> Logarithmic scale.</p>
<dt><code>rlog</code></p>
<dd> Reverse logarithmic scale.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;lin&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>win_size</code></p>
<dd> Set window size. Allowed range is from 16 to 65536. </p>
<p> Default is <tt>2048</tt></p>
<dt><code>win_func</code></p>
<dd> Set windowing function. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>rect</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bartlett</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hanning</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hamming</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>blackman</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>welch</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>flattop</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bharris</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bnuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bhann</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>sine</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>nuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>lanczos</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gauss</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tukey</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>dolph</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cauchy</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>parzen</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>poisson</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bohman</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;hanning&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>overlap</code></p>
<dd> Set window overlap. In range <tt>&quot;[0, 1]&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>1</tt>, which means optimal overlap for selected window function will be picked.</p>
<dt><code>averaging</code></p>
<dd> Set time averaging. Setting this to 0 will display current maximal peaks. Default is <tt>1</tt>, which means time averaging is disabled.</p>
<dt><code>colors</code></p>
<dd> Specify list of colors separated by space or by &#8216;|&#8217; which will be used to draw channel frequencies. Unrecognized or missing colors will be replaced by white color.</p>
<dt><code>cmode</code></p>
<dd> Set channel display mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>combined</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>separate</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;combined&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>minamp</code></p>
<dd> Set minimum amplitude used in <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt> amplitude scaler.</p>
<dt><code>data</code></p>
<dd> Set data display mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>magnitude</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>phase</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>delay</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is <tt>&quot;magnitude&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<h3>showspatial</h3>
<p> Convert stereo input audio to a video output, representing the spatial relationship between two channels. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;512x512&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>win_size</code></p>
<dd> Set window size. Allowed range is from <i>1024</i> to <i>65536</i>. Default size is <i>4096</i>.</p>
<dt><code>win_func</code></p>
<dd> Set window function. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>rect</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bartlett</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hann</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hanning</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hamming</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>blackman</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>welch</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>flattop</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bharris</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bnuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bhann</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>sine</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>nuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>lanczos</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gauss</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tukey</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>dolph</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cauchy</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>parzen</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>poisson</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bohman</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;hann&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>overlap</code></p>
<dd> Set ratio of overlap window. Default value is <tt>0.5</tt>. When value is <tt>1</tt> overlap is set to recommended size for specific window function currently used.</dl>
<h3>showspectrum</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a video output, representing the audio frequency spectrum. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;640x512&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>slide</code></p>
<dd> Specify how the spectrum should slide along the window. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>replace</code></p>
<dd> the samples start again on the left when they reach the right</p>
<dt><code>scroll</code></p>
<dd> the samples scroll from right to left</p>
<dt><code>fullframe</code></p>
<dd> frames are only produced when the samples reach the right</p>
<dt><code>rscroll</code></p>
<dd> the samples scroll from left to right</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;replace&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Specify display mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
