<dt><code>combined</code></p>
<dd> all channels are displayed in the same row</p>
<dt><code>separate</code></p>
<dd> all channels are displayed in separate rows</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>combined</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd> Specify display color mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>channel</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed in a separate color</p>
<dt><code>intensity</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the same color scheme</p>
<dt><code>rainbow</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the rainbow color scheme</p>
<dt><code>moreland</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the moreland color scheme</p>
<dt><code>nebulae</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the nebulae color scheme</p>
<dt><code>fire</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the fire color scheme</p>
<dt><code>fiery</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the fiery color scheme</p>
<dt><code>fruit</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the fruit color scheme</p>
<dt><code>cool</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the cool color scheme</p>
<dt><code>magma</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the magma color scheme</p>
<dt><code>green</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the green color scheme</p>
<dt><code>viridis</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the viridis color scheme</p>
<dt><code>plasma</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the plasma color scheme</p>
<dt><code>cividis</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the cividis color scheme</p>
<dt><code>terrain</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the terrain color scheme</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>channel</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Specify scale used for calculating intensity color values. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> linear</p>
<dt><code>sqrt</code></p>
<dd> square root, default</p>
<dt><code>cbrt</code></p>
<dd> cubic root</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> logarithmic</p>
<dt><code>4thrt</code></p>
<dd> 4th root</p>
<dt><code>5thrt</code></p>
<dd> 5th root</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>sqrt</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>fscale</code></p>
<dd> Specify frequency scale. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> linear</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> logarithmic</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>lin</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>saturation</code></p>
<dd> Set saturation modifier for displayed colors. Negative values provide alternative color scheme. <tt>0</tt> is no saturation at all. Saturation must be in [-10.0, 10.0] range. Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>win_func</code></p>
<dd> Set window function. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>rect</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bartlett</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hann</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hanning</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hamming</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>blackman</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>welch</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>flattop</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bharris</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bnuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bhann</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>sine</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>nuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>lanczos</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gauss</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tukey</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>dolph</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cauchy</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>parzen</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>poisson</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bohman</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;hann&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>orientation</code></p>
<dd> Set orientation of time vs frequency axis. Can be <tt>&quot;vertical&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;horizontal&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;vertical&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>overlap</code></p>
<dd> Set ratio of overlap window. Default value is <tt>0</tt>. When value is <tt>1</tt> overlap is set to recommended size for specific window function currently used.</p>
<dt><code>gain</code></p>
<dd> Set scale gain for calculating intensity color values. Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>data</code></p>
<dd> Set which data to display. Can be <tt>&quot;magnitude&quot;</tt>, default or <tt>&quot;phase&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>rotation</code></p>
<dd> Set color rotation, must be in [-1.0, 1.0] range. Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>start</code></p>
<dd> Set start frequency from which to display spectrogram. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>stop</code></p>
<dd> Set stop frequency to which to display spectrogram. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>fps</code></p>
<dd> Set upper frame rate limit. Default is <tt>&quot;auto&quot;</tt>, unlimited.</p>
<dt><code>legend</code></p>
<dd> Draw time and frequency axes and legends. Default is disabled.</dl>
<p> The usage is very similar to the showwaves filter; see the examples in that section. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Large window with logarithmic color scaling: </p>
<pre>
        showspectrum=s=1280x480:scale=log
</pre>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Complete example for a colored and sliding spectrum per channel using <code>ffplay</code>: </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; f lavfi &#8216;amovie=input.mp3, asplit [a][out1];<br />
             [a] showspectrum = mode = separate : color = intensity : slide = 1 : scale = cbrt[out0]&#8217;
</dl>
<h3>showspectrumpic</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a single video frame, representing the audio frequency spectrum. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;4096x2048&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Specify display mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>combined</code></p>
<dd> all channels are displayed in the same row</p>
<dt><code>separate</code></p>
<dd> all channels are displayed in separate rows</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>combined</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>color</code></p>
<dd> Specify display color mode. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>channel</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed in a separate color</p>
<dt><code>intensity</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the same color scheme</p>
<dt><code>rainbow</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the rainbow color scheme</p>
<dt><code>moreland</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the moreland color scheme</p>
<dt><code>nebulae</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the nebulae color scheme</p>
<dt><code>fire</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the fire color scheme</p>
<dt><code>fiery</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the fiery color scheme</p>
<dt><code>fruit</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the fruit color scheme</p>
<dt><code>cool</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the cool color scheme</p>
<dt><code>magma</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the magma color scheme</p>
<dt><code>green</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the green color scheme</p>
<dt><code>viridis</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the viridis color scheme</p>
<dt><code>plasma</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the plasma color scheme</p>
<dt><code>cividis</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the cividis color scheme</p>
<dt><code>terrain</code></p>
<dd> each channel is displayed using the terrain color scheme</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>intensity</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Specify scale used for calculating intensity color values. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> linear</p>
<dt><code>sqrt</code></p>
<dd> square root, default</p>
<dt><code>cbrt</code></p>
<dd> cubic root</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> logarithmic</p>
<dt><code>4thrt</code></p>
<dd> 4th root</p>
<dt><code>5thrt</code></p>
<dd> 5th root</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>log</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>fscale</code></p>
<dd> Specify frequency scale. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> linear</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> logarithmic</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <code>lin</code>.</dl>
<dt><code>saturation</code></p>
<dd> Set saturation modifier for displayed colors. Negative values provide alternative color scheme. <tt>0</tt> is no saturation at all. Saturation must be in [-10.0, 10.0] range. Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>win_func</code></p>
<dd> Set window function. </p>
<p> It accepts the following values:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>rect</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bartlett</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hann</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hanning</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>hamming</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>blackman</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>welch</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>flattop</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bharris</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bnuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bhann</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>sine</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>nuttall</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>lanczos</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>gauss</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>tukey</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>dolph</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>cauchy</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>parzen</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>poisson</code></p>
<dd>
<dt><code>bohman</code></p>
<dd></dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;hann&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>orientation</code></p>
<dd> Set orientation of time vs frequency axis. Can be <tt>&quot;vertical&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;horizontal&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;vertical&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>gain</code></p>
<dd> Set scale gain for calculating intensity color values. Default value is <tt>1</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>legend</code></p>
<dd> Draw time and frequency axes and legends. Default is enabled.</p>
<dt><code>rotation</code></p>
<dd> Set color rotation, must be in [-1.0, 1.0] range. Default value is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>start</code></p>
<dd> Set start frequency from which to display spectrogram. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>stop</code></p>
<dd> Set stop frequency to which to display spectrogram. Default is <tt>0</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Extract an audio spectrogram of a whole audio track in a 1024&#215;1024 picture using <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i audio.flac -lavfi showspectrumpic=s=1024x1024 spectrogram.png
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>showvolume</h3>
<p> Convert input audio volume to a video output. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set video rate.</p>
<dt><code>b</code></p>
<dd> Set border width, allowed range is [0, 5]. Default is 1.</p>
<dt><code>w</code></p>
<dd> Set channel width, allowed range is [80, 8192]. Default is 400.</p>
<dt><code>h</code></p>
<dd> Set channel height, allowed range is [1, 900]. Default is 20.</p>
<dt><code>f</code></p>
<dd> Set fade, allowed range is [0, 1]. Default is 0.95.</p>
<dt><code>c</code></p>
<dd> Set volume color expression. </p>
<p> The expression can use the following variables:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>VOLUME</code></font></p>
<dd> Current max volume of channel in dB.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>PEAK</code></font></p>
<dd> Current peak.</p>
<dt><font SIZE="-1"><code>CHANNEL</code></font></p>
<dd> Current channel number, starting from 0.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>t</code></p>
<dd> If set, displays channel names. Default is enabled.</p>
<dt><code>v</code></p>
<dd> If set, displays volume values. Default is enabled.</p>
<dt><code>o</code></p>
<dd> Set orientation, can be horizontal: <tt>&quot;h&quot;</tt> or vertical: <tt>&quot;v&quot;</tt>, default is <tt>&quot;h&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>s</code></p>
<dd> Set step size, allowed range is [0, 5]. Default is 0, which means step is disabled.</p>
<dt><code>p</code></p>
<dd> Set background opacity, allowed range is [0, 1]. Default is 0.</p>
<dt><code>m</code></p>
<dd> Set metering mode, can be peak: <tt>&quot;p&quot;</tt> or rms: <tt>&quot;r&quot;</tt>, default is <tt>&quot;p&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>ds</code></p>
<dd> Set display scale, can be linear: <tt>&quot;lin&quot;</tt> or log: <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt>, default is <tt>&quot;lin&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>dm</code></p>
<dd> In second. If set to &gt; 0., display a line for the max level in the previous seconds. default is disabled: <tt>0.</tt></p>
<dt><code>dmc</code></p>
<dd> The color of the max line. Use when <tt>&quot;dm&quot;</tt> option is set to &gt; 0. default is: <tt>&quot;orange&quot;</tt></dl>
<h3>showwaves</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a video output, representing the samples waves. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;600x240&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set display mode. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>point</code></p>
<dd> Draw a point for each sample.</p>
<dt><code>line</code></p>
<dd> Draw a vertical line for each sample.</p>
<dt><code>p2p</code></p>
<dd> Draw a point for each sample and a line between them.</p>
<dt><code>cline</code></p>
<dd> Draw a centered vertical line for each sample.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;point&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>n</code></p>
<dd> Set the number of samples which are printed on the same column. A larger value will decrease the frame rate. Must be a positive integer. This option can be set only if the value for <i>rate</i> is not explicitly specified.</p>
<dt><code>rate, r</code></p>
<dd> Set the (approximate) output frame rate. This is done by setting the option <i>n</i>. Default value is &#8220;25&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>split_channels</code></p>
<dd> Set if channels should be drawn separately or overlap. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>colors</code></p>
<dd> Set colors separated by &#8216;|&#8217; which are going to be used for drawing of each channel.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set amplitude scale. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> Linear.</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> Logarithmic.</p>
<dt><code>sqrt</code></p>
<dd> Square root.</p>
<dt><code>cbrt</code></p>
<dd> Cubic root.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is linear.</dl>
<dt><code>draw</code></p>
<dd> Set the draw mode. This is mostly useful to set for high <i>n</i>. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Scale pixel values for each drawn sample.</p>
<dt><code>full</code></p>
<dd> Draw every sample directly.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;scale&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Output the input file audio and the corresponding video representation at the same time: </p>
<p>amovie = a.mp3, asplit[out0], showwaves[out1]</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Create a synthetic signal and show it with showwaves, forcing a frame rate of 30 frames per second: </p>
<p>aevalsrc = sin(1 * 2 * PI * t) * sin(880 * 2 * PI * t)<br />
    : cos(2 * PI * 200 * t),<br />
asplit[out0], showwaves = r = 30 [out1]
</dl>
<h3>showwavespic</h3>
<p> Convert input audio to a single video frame, representing the samples waves. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>size, s</code></p>
<dd> Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the <code>&#8220;Video size&#8221; section in the ffmpeg-utils manual</code>. Default value is <tt>&quot;600x240&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>split_channels</code></p>
<dd> Set if channels should be drawn separately or overlap. Default value is 0.</p>
<dt><code>colors</code></p>
<dd> Set colors separated by &#8216;|&#8217; which are going to be used for drawing of each channel.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set amplitude scale. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>lin</code></p>
<dd> Linear.</p>
<dt><code>log</code></p>
<dd> Logarithmic.</p>
<dt><code>sqrt</code></p>
<dd> Square root.</p>
<dt><code>cbrt</code></p>
<dd> Cubic root.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default is linear.</dl>
<dt><code>draw</code></p>
<dd> Set the draw mode. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Scale pixel values for each drawn sample.</p>
<dt><code>full</code></p>
<dd> Draw every sample directly.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;scale&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<dt><code>filter</code></p>
<dd> Set the filter mode. </p>
<p> Available values are:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>average</code></p>
<dd> Use average samples values for each drawn sample.</p>
<dt><code>peak</code></p>
<dd> Use peak samples values for each drawn sample.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<p> Default value is <tt>&quot;average&quot;</tt>.</dl>
</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Extract a channel split representation of the wave form of a whole audio track in a 1024&#215;800 picture using <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i audio.flac -lavfi showwavespic=split_channels=1:s=1024x800 waveform.png
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>sidedata, asidedata</h3>
<p> Delete frame side data, or select frames based on it. </p>
<p> This filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>mode</code></p>
<dd> Set mode of operation of the filter. </p>
<p> Can be one of the following:</p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt><code>select</code></p>
<dd> Select every frame with side data of <tt>&quot;type&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>delete</code></p>
<dd> Delete side data of <tt>&quot;type&quot;</tt>. If <tt>&quot;type&quot;</tt> is not set, delete all side data in the frame.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>type</code></p>
<dd> Set side data type used with all modes. Must be set for <tt>&quot;select&quot;</tt> mode. For the list of frame side data types, refer to the <tt>&quot;AVFrameSideDataType&quot;</tt> enum in <i>libavutil/frame.h</i>. For example, to choose <tt>&quot;AV_FRAME_DATA_PANSCAN&quot;</tt> side data, you must specify <tt>&quot;PANSCAN&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<h3>spectrumsynth</h3>
<p> Synthesize audio from 2 input video spectrums, first input stream represents magnitude across time and second represents phase across time. The filter will transform from frequency domain as displayed in videos back to time domain as presented in audio output. </p>
<p> This filter is primarily created for reversing processed <code>showspectrum</code> filter outputs, but can synthesize sound from other spectrograms too. But in such case results are going to be poor if the phase data is not available, because in such cases phase data need to be recreated, usually it&#8217;s just recreated from random noise. For best results use gray only output (<tt>&quot;channel&quot;</tt> color mode in <code>showspectrum</code> filter) and <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt> scale for magnitude video and <tt>&quot;lin&quot;</tt> scale for phase video. To produce phase, for 2nd video, use <tt>&quot;data&quot;</tt> option. Inputs videos should generally use <tt>&quot;fullframe&quot;</tt> slide mode as that saves resources needed for decoding video. </p>
<p> The filter accepts the following options:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>sample_rate</code></p>
<dd> Specify sample rate of output audio, the sample rate of audio from which spectrum was generated may differ.</p>
<dt><code>channels</code></p>
<dd> Set number of channels represented in input video spectrums.</p>
<dt><code>scale</code></p>
<dd> Set scale which was used when generating magnitude input spectrum. Can be <tt>&quot;lin&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;log&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>slide</code></p>
<dd> Set slide which was used when generating inputs spectrums. Can be <tt>&quot;replace&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;scroll&quot;</tt>, <tt>&quot;fullframe&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;rscroll&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;fullframe&quot;</tt>.</p>
<dt><code>win_func</code></p>
<dd> Set window function used for resynthesis.</p>
<dt><code>overlap</code></p>
<dd> Set window overlap. In range <tt>&quot;[0, 1]&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>1</tt>, which means optimal overlap for selected window function will be picked.</p>
<dt><code>orientation</code></p>
<dd> Set orientation of input videos. Can be <tt>&quot;vertical&quot;</tt> or <tt>&quot;horizontal&quot;</tt>. Default is <tt>&quot;vertical&quot;</tt>.</dl>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> First create magnitude and phase videos from audio, assuming audio is stereo with 44100 sample rate, then resynthesize videos back to audio with spectrumsynth: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i input.flac -lavfi showspectrum=mode=separate:scale=log:overlap=0.875:color=channel:slide=fullframe:data=magnitude -an -c:v rawvideo magnitude.nut
        ffmpeg -i input.flac -lavfi showspectrum=mode=separate:scale=lin:overlap=0.875:color=channel:slide=fullframe:data=phase -an -c:v rawvideo phase.nut
        ffmpeg -i magnitude.nut -i phase.nut -lavfi spectrumsynth=channels=2:sample_rate=44100:win_func=hann:overlap=0.875:slide=fullframe output.flac
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>split, asplit</h3>
<p> Split input into several identical outputs. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;asplit&quot;</tt> works with audio input, <tt>&quot;split&quot;</tt> with video. </p>
<p> The filter accepts a single parameter which specifies the number of outputs. If unspecified, it defaults to 2. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Create two separate outputs from the same input: </p>
<p>[in] split[out0][out1]</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> To create 3 or more outputs, you need to specify the number of outputs, like in: </p>
<p>[in] asplit = 3 [out0][out1][out2]</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Create two separate outputs from the same input, one cropped and one padded: </p>
<p>[in] split[splitout1][splitout2];<br />
[splitout1] crop = 100 : 100 : 0 : 0 [cropout];<br />
[splitout2] pad = 200 : 200 : 100 : 100 [padout];</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Create 5 copies of the input audio with <code>ffmpeg</code>: </p>
<pre>
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -filter_complex asplit=5 OUTPUT
</pre>
</dl>
<h3>zmq, azmq</h3>
<p> Receive commands sent through a libzmq client, and forward them to filters in the filtergraph. </p>
<p> <tt>&quot;zmq&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;azmq&quot;</tt> work as a pass-through filters. <tt>&quot;zmq&quot;</tt> must be inserted between two video filters, <tt>&quot;azmq&quot;</tt> between two audio filters. Both are capable to send messages to any filter type. </p>
<p> To enable these filters you need to install the libzmq library and headers and configure FFmpeg with <tt>&quot;--enable-libzmq&quot;</tt>. </p>
<p> For more information about libzmq see: &lt;<code><a rel="nofollow" href="http://www.zeromq.org/">http://www.zeromq.org/</a></code>&gt; </p>
<p> The <tt>&quot;zmq&quot;</tt> and <tt>&quot;azmq&quot;</tt> filters work as a libzmq server, which receives messages sent through a network interface defined by the <code>bind_address</code> (or the abbreviation &quot;<code>b</code>&quot;) option. Default value of this option is <i><a href="tcp://localhost:5555">tcp://localhost:5555</a></i>. You may want to alter this value to your needs, but do not forget to escape any &#8216;:&#8217; signs (see <code>filtergraph escaping</code>). </p>
<p> The received message must be in the form: </p>
<pre>
        &lt;TARGET&gt; &lt;COMMAND&gt; [&lt;ARG&gt;]
</pre>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>TARGET</i></font><i></i> specifies the target of the command, usually the name of the filter class or a specific filter instance name. The default filter instance name uses the pattern <code>Parsed_&lt;filter_name&gt;_&lt;index&gt;</code>, but you can override this by using the <code>filter_name@id</code> syntax (see <code>Filtergraph syntax</code>). </p>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>COMMAND</i></font><i></i> specifies the name of the command for the target filter. </p>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>ARG</i></font><i></i> is optional and specifies the optional argument list for the given <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>COMMAND</i></font><i></i>. </p>
<p> Upon reception, the message is processed and the corresponding command is injected into the filtergraph. Depending on the result, the filter will send a reply to the client, adopting the format: </p>
<pre>
        &lt;ERROR_CODE&gt; &lt;ERROR_REASON&gt;
        &lt;MESSAGE&gt;
</pre>
<p> <i></i><font SIZE="-1"><i>MESSAGE</i></font><i></i> is optional. </p>
<p> <i>Examples</i> </p>
<p> Look at <i>tools/zmqsend</i> for an example of a zmq client which can be used to send commands processed by these filters. </p>
<p> Consider the following filtergraph generated by <code>ffplay</code>. In this example the last overlay filter has an instance name. All other filters will have default instance names. </p>
<p>ffplay &#8211; dumpgraph 1 &#8211; f lavfi &#8221;<br />
    color = s = 100&#215;100 : c = red[l];<br />
color = s = 100&#215;100 : c = blue[r];<br />
nullsrc = s = 200&#215;100, zmq[bg];<br />
[bg][l] overlay[bg + l];<br />
[bg + l][r] overlay @my = x = 100 &#8221;</p>
<p> To change the color of the left side of the video, the following command can be used: </p>
<pre>
        echo Parsed_color_0 c yellow | tools/zmqsend
</pre>
<p> To change the right side: </p>
<pre>
        echo Parsed_color_1 c pink | tools/zmqsend
</pre>
<p> To change the position of the right side: </p>
<pre>
        echo overlay@my x 150 | tools/zmqsend
</pre>
<h2>MULTIMEDIA SOURCES</h2>
<p> Below is a description of the currently available multimedia sources.</p>
<h3>amovie</h3>
<p> This is the same as <code>movie</code> source, except it selects an audio stream by default.</p>
<h3>movie</h3>
<p> Read audio and/or video stream(s) from a movie container. </p>
<p> It accepts the following parameters:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>filename</code></p>
<dd> The name of the resource to read (not necessarily a file; it can also be a device or a stream accessed through some protocol).</p>
<dt><code>format_name, f</code></p>
<dd> Specifies the format assumed for the movie to read, and can be either the name of a container or an input device. If not specified, the format is guessed from <i>movie_name</i> or by probing.</p>
<dt><code>seek_point, sp</code></p>
<dd> Specifies the seek point in seconds. The frames will be output starting from this seek point. The parameter is evaluated with <tt>&quot;av_strtod&quot;</tt>, so the numerical value may be suffixed by an <font SIZE="-1">IS</font> postfix. The default value is &#8220;0&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>streams, s</code></p>
<dd> Specifies the streams to read. Several streams can be specified, separated by &#8220;+&#8221;. The source will then have as many outputs, in the same order. The syntax is explained in the <code>&#8220;Stream specifiers&#8221; section in the ffmpeg manual</code>. Two special names, &#8220;dv&#8221; and &#8220;da&#8221; specify respectively the default (best suited) video and audio stream. Default is &#8220;dv&#8221;, or &#8220;da&#8221; if the filter is called as &#8220;amovie&#8221;.</p>
<dt><code>stream_index, si</code></p>
<dd> Specifies the index of the video stream to read. If the value is -1, the most suitable video stream will be automatically selected. The default value is &#8220;-1&#8221;. Deprecated. If the filter is called &#8220;amovie&#8221;, it will select audio instead of video.</p>
<dt><code>loop</code></p>
<dd> Specifies how many times to read the stream in sequence. If the value is 0, the stream will be looped infinitely. Default value is &#8220;1&#8221;. </p>
<p> Note that when the movie is looped the source timestamps are not changed, so it will generate non monotonically increasing timestamps.</p>
<dt><code>discontinuity</code></p>
<dd> Specifies the time difference between frames above which the point is considered a timestamp discontinuity which is removed by adjusting the later timestamps.</dl>
<p> It allows overlaying a second video on top of the main input of a filtergraph, as shown in this graph: </p>
<p>input&#8211; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212; &#8212;&gt;deltapts0&#8211; &gt; overlay&#8211; &gt; output ^<br />
    |<br />
    movie&#8211; &gt; scale&#8211; &gt; deltapts1&#8211; &#8212; &#8212; -+</p>
<p> <i>Examples</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Skip 3.2 seconds from the start of the <font SIZE="-1">AVI</font> file in.avi, and overlay it on top of the input labelled &#8220;in&#8221;: </p>
<p>movie = in.avi : seek_point = 3.2, scale = 180 : -1, setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS[over];<br />
[in] setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS[main];<br />
[main][over] overlay = 16 : 16 [out]</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Read from a video4linux2 device, and overlay it on top of the input labelled &#8220;in&#8221;: </p>
<p>movie = / dev / video0 : f = video4linux2, scale = 180 : -1, setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS[over];<br />
[in] setpts = PTS &#8211; STARTPTS[main];<br />
[main][over] overlay = 16 : 16 [out]</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> Read the first video stream and the audio stream with id 0x81 from dvd.vob; the video is connected to the pad named &#8220;video&#8221; and the audio is connected to the pad named &#8220;audio&#8221;: </p>
<pre><code>
movie = dvd.vob : s = v : 0 + #0x81 
</code></pre>
</dl>
<p> <i>Commands</i> </p>
<p> Both movie and amovie support the following commands:</p>
<dl>
<dt><code>seek</code></p>
<dd> Perform seek using &#8220;av_seek_frame&#8221;. The syntax is: seek <i>stream_index</i>|<i>timestamp</i>|<i>flags</i></p>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd>
<dl>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> <i>stream_index</i>: If stream_index is -1, a default stream is selected, and <i>timestamp</i> is automatically converted from <font SIZE="-1">AV_TIME_BASE</font> units to the stream specific time_base.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> <i>timestamp</i>: Timestamp in AVStream.time_base units or, if no stream is specified, in <font SIZE="-1">AV_TIME_BASE</font> units.</p>
<dt>&bull;</p>
<dd> <i>flags</i>: Flags which select direction and seeking mode.</dl>
</dl>
<dl>
<dt>
<dd></dl>
<dt><code>get_duration</code></p>
<dd> Get movie duration in <font SIZE="-1">AV_TIME_BASE</font> units.</dl>
